\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma^{-1}\|\mathcal{F}(i_{0},\alpha_{0})\|_{q,s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|g\|_{q,s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}},

(5.127)

\displaystyle\forall b\geqslant 0,\quad\|\mathcal{E}^{(n)}_{2}g\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma^{-1}N_{n}^{-b}\big(\|g\|_{q,s_{0}+b+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\varepsilon\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s_{0}+b+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\big\|g\|_{q,s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\big)\,,

(5.128)

\displaystyle\forall b\in[0,S],\quad\|\mathcal{E}^{(n)}_{3}g\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{n}^{-b}\gamma^{-2}\Big(\|g\|_{q,s_{0}+b+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+{\varepsilon\gamma^{-2}}\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s_{0}+b+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|g\|_{q,s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\varepsilon\gamma^{-4}N_{0}^{{\mu}_{2}}{N_{n}^{-\mu_{2}}}\|g\|_{q,s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(5.129)

Proof.

The estimate (
5.125
) is a consequence of (
5.124
), Proposition
5.3
-(iv) and Lemma
5.3
-(i). Then, according to (
5.91
) and
(
5.107
), in the Cantor set
\mathcal{G}_{n}
we have the decomposition

\displaystyle d_{i,\alpha}\mathcal{F}(i_{0},\alpha_{0})

\displaystyle=DG_{0}({\mathtt{u}}_{0})\circ\mathbb{L}_{\textnormal{ext}}\circ D[\widetilde{G}_{0}({\mathtt{u}}_{0})]^{-1}

\displaystyle=DG_{0}({\mathtt{u}}_{0})\circ{\mathbb{D}}_{n}\circ D[\widetilde{G}_{0}({\mathtt{u}}_{0})]^{-1}+DG_{0}({\mathtt{u}}_{0})\circ\mathbb{E}_{n}^{\textnormal{ext}}\circ[\widetilde{G}_{0}({\mathtt{u}}_{0})]^{-1}

\displaystyle+DG_{0}({\mathtt{u}}_{0})\circ{\mathscr{P}}_{n}\circ[\widetilde{G}_{0}({\mathtt{u}}_{0})]^{-1}+DG_{0}({\mathtt{u}}_{0})\circ{\mathscr{Q}}_{n}\circ[\widetilde{G}_{0}({\mathtt{u}}_{0})]^{-1}.

By applying
{\rm T}_{0}
, defined in (
5.124
), to the last identity we get for all
(\lambda,\omega)\in\mathcal{G}_{n}

d_{i,\alpha}\mathcal{F}(i_{0},\alpha_{0})\circ{\rm T}_{0}-\textnormal{Id}=\mathcal{E}^{(n)}_{1}+\mathcal{E}^{(n)}_{2}+\mathcal{E}^{(n)}_{3},

with

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{(n)}_{1}:=DG_{0}({\mathtt{u}}_{0})\circ\mathbb{E}_{n}^{\textnormal{ext}}\circ[\widetilde{G}_{0}({\mathtt{u}}_{0})]^{-1}\circ{\rm T}_{0},

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{(n)}_{2}:=DG_{0}({\mathtt{u}}_{0})\circ{\mathscr{P}}_{n}\circ[\widetilde{G}_{0}({\mathtt{u}}_{0})]^{-1}\circ{\rm T}_{0},

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{(n)}_{3}:=DG_{0}({\mathtt{u}}_{0})\circ{\mathscr{Q}}_{n}\circ[\widetilde{G}_{0}({\mathtt{u}}_{0})]^{-1}\circ{\rm T}_{0}.

The estimates on
\mathcal{E}^{(n)}_{1}
,
\mathcal{E}^{(n)}_{2}
and
\mathcal{E}^{(n)}_{3}
come from (
5.125
), Proposition
5.3
and Lemma
5.3
-(i).

∎

6
Reduction of the linearized operator in the normal directions

In this section, we fix a torus
i_{0}=(\vartheta_{0},I_{0},z_{0})
close to the flat one and satisfying the reversibility condition (
5.25
), that is

\begin{array}[]{ccc}\vartheta_{0}(-\varphi)=-\vartheta_{0}(\varphi),&I_{0}(-\varphi)=I_{0}(\varphi),&z_{0}(-\varphi)=(\mathscr{S}z_{0})(\varphi).\end{array}

(6.1)

As in the previous section, we denote

\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}_{0}(\varphi)=i_{0}(\varphi)-(\varphi,0,0).

Our main goal here is to explore the invertibility of the operator

\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\omega}=\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\omega}(i_{0})=\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\left(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}-\partial_{\theta}\mathsf{K}_{02}(\varphi)\right)\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}

(6.2)

defined through (
5.87
) and (
5.72
) with the suitable tame estimates for the inverse. For a precise statement we refer to Proposition
6.6
. Notice that this operator will be described as a quasilinear perturbation of the diagonal operator stated in Lemma
3.1
and we expect that suitable standard reductions can be performed to conjugate it to a diagonal one provided that the exterior parameters are subject to live in a Cantor set allowing to prevent resonances. For this aim, we shall implement with suitable adaptions the strategy developed in the works
[
4
,
14
]
. We distinguish two long reduction steps. First, we perform a quasi-periodic change of variables such that in the new coordinates system the transport part is straightened to a constant coefficient operator. The construction of this transformation is based on a KAM reducibility procedure as in
[
26
]
. The outcome of this first step is a new operator whose positive part is diagonal with a small nonlocal perturbation of order
-1
. Then the second step consists in applying KAM scheme in order to reduce the remainder and conjugate the resulting operator from step 1 into a diagonal one up to small errors. The proof follows basically a common procedure that can be found for instance in
[
10
]
. We point out that our results differ slightly from the preceding ones in
[
4
,
14
]
, especially at the level of Cantor sets which are constructed over the final targets.

We shall use throughout the proofs some frequency cut-offs with respect to the sequence defined in (
5.82
),
with
N_{0}
a constant needed to be large enough.
In the current section, the numbers
N_{0}\geqslant 2
and
\gamma\in(0,1)
are a priori free parameters, but during the Nash-Moser scheme, see Proposition
7.1
, they will be adjusted with respect to
\varepsilon
according to the relations

N_{0}=\gamma^{-1}\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\gamma=\varepsilon^{a}\quad\mbox{ for some }a>0.

We shall set the following parameters required along the different reductions that we intend to perform,

\begin{array}[]{ll}s_{l}:=s_{0}+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+2,&\overline{\mu}_{2}:=4\tau_{1}q+6\tau_{1}+3,\\
\overline{s}_{l}:=s_{l}+\tau_{2}q+\tau_{2},&\overline{s}_{h}:=\frac{3}{2}\overline{\mu}_{2}+s_{l}+1,\end{array}

(6.3)

supplemented with the assumptions (
4.1
) and (
4.2
).

6.1
Localization on the normal directions

According to Theorem
5.1
, the construction of an approximate inverse for
d_{i,\alpha}\mathcal{F}(i_{0},\alpha_{0})
is based on Proposition
5.2
dealing with finding an approximate right inverse for the operator
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\omega}
. This program will be achieved along several steps and in the first one we shall describe its asymptotic structure around the linearized operator at the equilibrium state described in Lemma
3.1
. More precisely, we shall prove the following result.

Proposition 6.1
.

Let
(\gamma,q,d,s_{0})
satisfy (
4.1
).
Then the operator
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\omega}
defined in (
6.2
) takes the form

\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\omega}=\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\left(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon r}-\varepsilon\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{R}\right)\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp},\quad\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon r}=\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+\partial_{\theta}\left(V_{\varepsilon r}\cdot\right)-\partial_{\theta}\mathbf{L}_{\varepsilon r},

where
V_{\varepsilon r}
and
\mathbf{L}_{\varepsilon r}
are defined in Lemma
\ref{lemma general form of the linearized operator},

and from (
5.13
) we have

\displaystyle r(\varphi)

\displaystyle=A\big(\vartheta_{0}(\varphi),\,I_{0}(\varphi),z_{0}(\varphi)\big)

\displaystyle=v\big(\vartheta_{0}(\varphi),I_{0}(\varphi)\big)+z_{0}(\varphi),

supplemented with the reversibility assumption

r(\lambda,\omega,-\varphi,-\theta)=r(\lambda,\omega,\varphi,\theta).

(6.4)

Moreover,
\mathcal{R}
is an integral operator in the sense of the Definition
\ref{Defin-Rever-1},
whose kernel
J
satisfies the symmetry property

J(\lambda,\omega,-\varphi,-\theta,-\eta)=J(\lambda,\omega,\varphi,\theta,\eta).

(6.5)

and under the assumption

\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant 1,

(6.6)

we have for all
s\geqslant s_{0}
,

(i)

The function
r
satisfies the estimates,

\|r\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim 1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

(6.7)

and

\|\Delta_{12}r\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\max_{j=1,2}\|\mathfrak{I}_{j}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.8)

(ii)

The kernel
J
satisfies the following estimates for all
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
,

\sup_{\eta\in\mathbb{T}}\|(\partial_{\theta}^{\ell}J)(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot,\eta+\centerdot)\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim 1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+3+\ell}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

(6.9)

and

\sup_{\eta\in\mathbb{T}}\|\Delta_{12}(\partial_{\theta}^{\ell}J)(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot,\eta+\centerdot)\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,s+3+\ell}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,s_{0}+3}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\max_{j=1,2}\|\mathfrak{I}_{j}\|_{q,s+3+\ell}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.10)

Here
\ast,\cdot,\centerdot
stand for
(\lambda,\omega),\varphi,\theta
, respectively and
\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}_{\ell}(\varphi)=i_{\ell}(\varphi)-(\varphi,0,0).
In addition, for any function
f
,
\Delta_{12}f:=f(i_{1})-f(i_{2})
refers for the difference of
f
taken at two different states
i_{1}
and
i_{2}
satisfying (
6.6
).

Proof.

To alleviate the notation we shall at several stages of the proof remove the dependence of the involved functions/operators with respect to
(\lambda,\omega)
and keep it when we deem it relevant. Recall that the operator
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\omega}
is defined in (
6.2
). To describe
\mathsf{K}_{02}(\varphi)
we follow
[
4
,
14
]
. First, we observe from (
5.72
) and (
5.22
) that

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}_{02}(\varphi)=\mathrm{L}(\lambda)+\varepsilon\partial_{w}\nabla_{w}(\mathcal{P}_{\varepsilon}(i_{0}(\varphi))+\varepsilon\mathcal{R}(\varphi),

with

\mathcal{R}(\varphi)=\mathcal{R}_{1}(\varphi)+\mathcal{R}_{2}(\varphi)+\mathcal{R}_{3}(\varphi),

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}(\varphi)

\displaystyle:=L_{2}^{\top}(\varphi)\partial_{I}\nabla_{I}\mathcal{P}_{\varepsilon}(i_{0}(\varphi))L_{2}(\varphi),

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{2}(\varphi)

\displaystyle:=L_{2}^{\top}(\varphi)\partial_{z}\nabla_{I}\mathcal{P}_{\varepsilon}(i_{0}(\varphi)),

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{3}(\varphi)

\displaystyle:=\partial_{I}\nabla_{z}\mathcal{P}_{\varepsilon}(i_{0}(\varphi))L_{2}(\varphi).

As we shall see, all the operators
\mathcal{R}_{1}(\varphi),

\mathcal{R}_{2}(\varphi)
and
\mathcal{R}_{3}(\varphi)
have a finite-dimensional rank. This property is obvious for the operator
L_{2}(\varphi)
defined in (
5.65
), which sends in view of (
5.62
) the space
H_{\perp}^{s}
to
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and therefore for any
\rho\in H_{\perp}^{s}
we write

L_{2}(\varphi)[\rho]=\sum_{k=1}^{d}\big\langle L_{2}(\varphi)[\rho],\underline{e}_{k}\big\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\,\underline{e}_{k}=\sum_{k=1}^{d}\big\langle\rho,L_{2}^{\top}(\varphi)[\underline{e}_{k}]\big\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\,\underline{e}_{k},

with
\displaystyle(\underline{e}_{k})_{k=1}^{d}
being the canonical basis of
\mathbb{R}^{d}.

Hence

\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}(\varphi)[\rho]=\sum_{k=1}^{d}\big\langle\rho,L_{2}^{\top}(\varphi)[\underline{e}_{k}]\big\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}A_{1}(\varphi)[\underline{e}_{k}]&\quad\mbox{with }\quad A_{1}(\varphi)=L_{2}^{\top}(\varphi)\partial_{I}\nabla_{I}\mathcal{P}_{\varepsilon}(i_{0}(\varphi)),\\
\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{3}(\varphi)[\rho]=\sum_{k=1}^{d}\big\langle\rho,L_{2}^{\top}(\varphi)[\underline{e}_{k}]\big\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}A_{3}(\varphi)[\underline{e}_{k}]&\quad\mbox{with }\quad A_{3}(\varphi)=\partial_{I}\nabla_{z}\mathcal{P}_{\varepsilon}(i_{0}(\varphi)).\end{array}

In a similar way, by setting
A_{2}(\varphi):=\partial_{z}\nabla_{I}\mathcal{P}_{\varepsilon}(i_{0}(\varphi)):H_{\perp}^{s}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d},
then we may write

\mathcal{R}_{2}(\varphi)[\rho]=\sum_{k=1}^{d}\big\langle\rho,A_{2}^{\top}(\varphi)[\underline{e}_{k}]\big\rangle_{L^{2}\,(\mathbb{T})}L_{2}^{\top}(\varphi)[\underline{e}_{k}].

Define

g_{k,1}(\varphi,\theta)=g_{k,3}(\varphi,\theta)=\chi_{k,2}(\varphi,\theta):=L_{2}^{\top}(\varphi)[\underline{e}_{k}](\theta),\quad g_{k,2}(\varphi,\theta):=A_{2}^{\top}(\varphi)[\underline{e}_{k}](\theta)

and

\mbox{ }\chi_{k,1}(\varphi,\theta):=A_{1}(\varphi)[\underline{e}_{k}](\theta),\quad\chi_{k,3}(\varphi,\theta):=A_{3}(\varphi)[\underline{e}_{k}](\theta),

then we can see that the operator
\mathcal{R}
takes the integral form

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}\rho(\varphi,\theta)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{3}\sum_{k=1}^{d}\langle\rho(\varphi,\cdot),g_{k,k^{\prime}}(\varphi,\cdot)\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\chi_{k,k^{\prime}}(\varphi,\theta)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}}\rho(\varphi,\eta)J(\varphi,\theta,\eta)d\eta,

with

J(\varphi,\theta,\eta):=\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{3}\sum_{k=1}^{d}g_{k,k^{\prime}}(\varphi,\eta)\chi_{k,k^{\prime}}(\varphi,\theta).

Now we remark that by construction
g_{k,k^{\prime}},\chi_{k,k^{\prime}}\in H_{\perp}^{s}
with

\|g_{k,k^{\prime}}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\chi_{k,k^{\prime}}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim 1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+3}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

(6.11)

and straightforward computations yield

\|d_{i}g_{k,k^{\prime}}[\widehat{i}]\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|d_{i}\chi_{k,k^{\prime}}[\widehat{i}]\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\|\widehat{i}\|_{q,s+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+4}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\widehat{i}\|_{q,s_{0}+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.12)

On the other hand, one has from direct computations that

\forall\ell\in\mathbb{N},\quad(\partial_{\theta}^{\ell}J)(\varphi,\theta,\eta+\theta)=\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{3}\sum_{k=1}^{d}g_{k,k^{\prime}}(\varphi,\eta+\theta)(\partial_{\theta}^{\ell}\chi_{k,k^{\prime}})(\varphi,\theta).

Hence, we may combine (
6.11
) with Lemma
4.1
-(iv) and (
6.6
) allowing to get

\displaystyle\sup_{\eta\in\mathbb{T}}\|(\partial_{\theta}^{\ell}J)(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot,\eta+\centerdot)\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{3}\sum_{k=1}^{d}\|g_{k,k^{\prime}}(\ast,\cdot,\eta+\centerdot)\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\chi_{k,k^{\prime}}(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot)\|_{q,s_{0}+\ell}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{3}\sum_{k=1}^{d}\|g_{k,k^{\prime}}(\ast,\cdot,\eta+\centerdot)\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\chi_{k,k^{\prime}}(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot)\|_{q,s+\ell}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+3+\ell}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}},

where we have used the interpolation inequality: for
s\geqslant s_{0}

\displaystyle\|g_{k,k^{\prime}}(\ast,\cdot,\eta+\centerdot)\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\chi_{k,k^{\prime}}(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot)\|_{q,s_{0}+\ell}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|g_{k,k^{\prime}}(\ast,\cdot,\eta+\centerdot)\|_{q,s+\ell}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\chi_{k,k^{\prime}}(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot)\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\quad+\|g_{k,k^{\prime}}(\ast,\cdot,\eta+\centerdot)\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\chi_{k,k^{\prime}}(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot)\|_{q,s+\ell}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

In addition, to estimate the difference we simply write

\displaystyle\forall\ell\in\mathbb{N},\quad\Delta_{12}(\partial_{\theta}^{\ell}J)(\varphi,\theta,\eta+\theta)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{3}\sum_{k=1}^{d}\Delta_{12}g_{k,k^{\prime}}(\varphi,\eta+\theta)(\partial_{\theta}^{\ell}(\chi_{k,k^{\prime}})_{r_{1}})(\varphi,\theta)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{3}\sum_{k=1}^{d}(g_{k,k^{\prime}})_{r_{2}}(\varphi,\eta+\theta)(\Delta_{12}\partial_{\theta}^{\ell}\chi_{k,k^{\prime}})(\varphi,\theta).

By applying the mean value theorem combined with (
6.12
) and (
6.6
) combined with interpolation inequalities

\displaystyle\sup_{\eta\in\mathbb{T}}\|\Delta_{12}(\partial_{\theta}^{\ell}J)(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot,\eta+\centerdot)\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{3}\sum_{k=1}^{d}\|\Delta_{12}g_{k,k^{\prime}}(\ast,\cdot,\eta+\centerdot)\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\chi_{k,k^{\prime}}(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot)\|_{q,s_{0}+\ell}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{3}\sum_{k=1}^{d}\|\Delta_{12}g_{k,k^{\prime}}(\ast,\cdot,\eta+\centerdot)\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\chi_{k,k^{\prime}}(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot)\|_{q,s+\ell}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{3}\sum_{k=1}^{d}\|g_{k,k^{\prime}}(\ast,\cdot,\eta+\centerdot)\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\Delta_{12}\chi_{k,k^{\prime}}(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot)\|_{q,s+\ell}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{3}\sum_{k=1}^{d}\|g_{k,k^{\prime}}(\ast,\cdot,\eta+\centerdot)\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\Delta_{12}\chi_{k,k^{\prime}}(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot)\|_{q,s_{0}+\ell}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,s+3+\ell}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,s_{0}+3}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\max_{j=1,2}\|\mathfrak{I}_{j}\|_{q,s+3+\ell}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

The symmetry property detailed in (
6.5
) is a consequence of the definition of
r
and the reversibility condition (
6.1
) imposed on the torus
i_{0}.
Consequently, putting together (
5
) and (
5.13
) gives

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}_{02}(\varphi)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{L}(\lambda)\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}+\varepsilon\partial_{z}\nabla_{z}\mathcal{P}_{\varepsilon}(i_{0}(\varphi))+\varepsilon\mathcal{R}(\varphi)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{L}(\lambda)\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}+\varepsilon\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\partial_{r}\nabla_{r}P_{\varepsilon}(A(i_{0}(\varphi)))\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}+\varepsilon\mathcal{R}(\varphi)

\displaystyle=\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\partial_{r}\nabla_{r}\mathcal{H}_{\varepsilon}(A(i_{0}(\varphi)))\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}+\varepsilon\mathcal{R}(\varphi)

\displaystyle=\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\partial_{r}\nabla_{r}H(\varepsilon A(i_{0}(\varphi)))\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}+\varepsilon\mathcal{R}(\varphi).

Recall from (
5.13
) that

r(\varphi,\cdot)=A(i_{0}(\varphi)),

(6.13)

then according to the general form of the linearized operator stated in Lemma
3.1
one has

-\partial_{\theta}\partial_{r}\nabla_{r}H(\varepsilon r(\varphi,\cdot))=\partial_{\theta}\left(V_{\varepsilon r}\cdot\right)-\partial_{\theta}\mathbf{L}_{\varepsilon r},

which implies in turn

\begin{array}[]{rcl}-\mathsf{K}_{02}(\varphi)=\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\big(\partial_{\theta}\left(V_{\varepsilon r}\cdot\right)-\partial_{\theta}\mathbf{L}_{\varepsilon r})-\varepsilon\mathcal{R}(\varphi)\big)\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}.\end{array}

Plugging this identity into (
6.2
) gives the desired result. Next, using (
6.13
), (
5.13
) and (
5.59
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|r\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|v(\vartheta_{0},I_{0})\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|z_{0}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

We shall now move to the proof of the bound (
6.8
). First, we observe from (
5.13
) that

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}r\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Delta_{12}v(\vartheta,I)\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\Delta_{12}z\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Therefore, Taylor Formula with (
5.59
) and law products allow to get

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}v(\vartheta,I)\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Delta_{12}(I,\vartheta)\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\Delta_{12}(I,\vartheta)\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\max_{j=1,2}\|\mathfrak{I}_{j}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}},

which implies that

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}r\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\max_{j=1,2}\|\mathfrak{I}_{j}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

This achieves the proof of Proposition
6.1
.

∎

6.2
Reduction of order 1

In this section, we perform the reduction of the transport part of the linearized operator
\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon r}
described in Proposition
6.1
. More precisely, we conjugate the operator
\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon r}
by a quasi-periodic symplectic change of variables
\mathscr{B}
leading to a transport part with constant coefficients depending only on the torus
i_{0}
and the parameters
\varepsilon,

\lambda
and
\omega.
To get a precise information on the remainder, which is of order
-1
in
\theta
, we need to describe the action of this conjugation on the nonlocal term using the kernel structure rather than pseudo-differential theory. The reduction to a constant coefficient operator is based on KAM scheme through the construction of successive quasi-periodic symplectic change of coordinates. This will be implemented in the same spirit of
[
13
,
26
]
. Here we need to extend their construction to the framework of of symplectic change of coordinates with
C^{q}
regularity. We point out that similar results with slight variations have been established in
[
7
,
13
]
in a non-symplectic framework.

6.2.1
Reduction of the transport part

Before stating our result, we need to introduce some transformations.
Let
\beta:\mathcal{O}\times\mathbb{T}^{d+1}\to\mathbb{T}
be a smooth function such that
\displaystyle\sup_{\mu\in\mathcal{O}}\|\beta(\mu,\cdot,\centerdot)\|_{\textnormal{Lip}}<1

then the map

(\varphi,\theta)\in\mathbb{T}^{d+1}\mapsto(\varphi,\theta+\beta(\mu,\varphi,\theta))\in\mathbb{T}^{d+1}

is a diffeomorphism and its inverse takes the form

(\varphi,\theta)\in\mathbb{T}^{d+1}\mapsto(\varphi,\theta+\widehat{\beta}(\mu,\varphi,\theta))\in\mathbb{T}^{d+1}.

The relation between
\beta
and
\widehat{\beta}
is described through,

y=\theta+\beta(\mu,\varphi,\theta)\Longleftrightarrow\theta=y+\widehat{\beta}(\mu,\varphi,y).

(6.14)

Now we define the operators

\mathscr{B}=(1+\partial_{\theta}\beta)\mathcal{B},

(6.15)

with

\mathcal{B}\rho(\mu,\varphi,\theta)=\rho\big(\mu,\varphi,\theta+\beta(\mu,\varphi,\theta)\big).

Direct computations show that the inverse
\mathscr{B}^{-1}
keeps the same form, that is,

\mathscr{B}^{-1}\rho(\mu,\varphi,y)=\Big(1+\partial_{y}\widehat{\beta}(\mu,\varphi,y)\Big)\rho\big(\mu,\varphi,y+\widehat{\beta}(\mu,\varphi,y)\big)

(6.16)

and

\mathcal{B}^{-1}\rho(\mu,\varphi,y)=\rho\big(\mu,\varphi,y+\widehat{\beta}(\mu,\varphi,y)\big).

We shall now give some elementary algebraic properties for
\mathcal{B}^{\pm 1}
and
\mathscr{B}^{\pm 1}
which can be checked by straightforward computations.

Lemma 6.1
.

The following assertions hold true.

(i)

The action of
\mathscr{B}^{-1}
on the derivative is given by

\mathscr{B}^{-1}\partial_{\theta}=\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{B}^{-1}.

(ii)

The conjugation of the transport operator by
\mathscr{B}
keeps the same structure

\mathscr{B}^{-1}\Big(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+\partial_{\theta}\big(V(\varphi,\theta)\cdot\big)\Big)\mathscr{B}=\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+\partial_{y}(\mathscr{V}(\varphi,y)\cdot\big),

with

\mathscr{V}(\varphi,y)=\mathcal{B}^{-1}\Big(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}\beta(\varphi,\theta)+V(\varphi,\theta)\big(1+\partial_{\theta}\beta(\varphi,\theta)\big)\Big).

(iii)

Denote by
\mathscr{B}^{\star}
the
L^{2}_{\theta}(\mathbb{T})
-adjoint of
\mathscr{B}
, then

\mathscr{B}^{\star}=\mathcal{B}^{-1}\quad\hbox{and}\quad\mathcal{B}^{\star}=\mathscr{B}^{-1}.

Now we shall state the following result proved in
[
26
]
for
q=1
and which can be obtained by induction for a general
q\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
up to slight modifications. We also refer to
[
13
, (A.2)]
.

Lemma 6.2
.

Let
(q,d\gamma,s_{0})
as in (
4.1
).
Let
\beta\in W^{q,\infty,\gamma}\big(\mathcal{O},H^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d+1})\big)
such that

\|\beta\|_{q,2s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant\varepsilon_{0},

(6.17)

with
\varepsilon_{0}
small enough. Then the following assertions hold true.

(i)

The linear operators
\mathcal{B},\mathscr{B}:W^{q,\infty,\gamma}\big(\mathcal{O},H^{s}(\mathbb{T}^{d+1})\big)\to W^{q,\infty,\gamma}\big(\mathcal{O},H^{s}(\mathbb{T}^{d+1})\big)
are continuous and invertible, with

\forall s\geqslant s_{0},\quad\|\mathcal{B}^{\pm 1}\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant\|\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\left(1+C\|\beta\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)+C\|\beta\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

(6.18)

and

\forall s\geqslant s_{0},\quad\|\mathscr{B}^{\pm 1}\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant\|\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\left(1+C\|\beta\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)+C\|\beta\|_{q,s+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.19)

(ii)

The functions
\beta
and
\widehat{\beta}
are linked through

\forall s\geqslant s_{0},\quad\|\widehat{\beta}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant C\|\beta\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.20)

(iii)

Let
\beta_{1},\beta_{2}\in W^{q,\infty,\gamma}(\mathcal{O},H^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d+1}))
satisfying (
6.17
). If we denote

\Delta_{12}\beta=\beta_{1}-\beta_{2}\quad\textnormal{ and }\quad\Delta_{12}\widehat{\beta}=\widehat{\beta}_{1}-\widehat{\beta}_{2},

then they are linked through

\forall s\geqslant s_{0},\quad\|\Delta_{12}\widehat{\beta}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant C\left(\|\Delta_{12}\beta\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\Delta_{12}\beta\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\max_{j\in\{1,2\}}\|\beta_{j}\|_{q,s+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right).

(6.21)

Proof.

(i)
-
(ii)
For (
6.18
) and (
6.20
), we refer to
[
13
, (A.2)]
and
[
26
, Lem. A.3.]
. The estimate (
6.19
) is obtained from (
6.18
) and law product in Lemma
4.1
.

(iii)
One has by Taylor Formula

\displaystyle\Delta_{12}\widehat{\beta}(y)

\displaystyle=\widehat{\beta}_{1}(y)-\widehat{\beta}_{2}(y)

\displaystyle=\beta_{2}(y+\widehat{\beta}_{2}(y))-\beta_{1}(y+\widehat{\beta}_{1}(y))

\displaystyle=-\Delta_{12}\beta(y+\widehat{\beta}_{2}(y))-\Delta_{12}\widehat{\beta}(y)\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{\theta}\beta_{1}(y+\widehat{\beta}_{1}(y)-t\Delta_{12}\widehat{\beta}(y))dt.

Hence

\Delta_{12}\widehat{\beta}(y)=\tfrac{-\mathcal{B}_{2}^{-1}\Delta_{12}\beta(y)}{1+\mathscr{I}(y)}\quad\textnormal{with}\quad\mathscr{I}(y):=\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{\theta}\beta_{1}(y+\widehat{\beta}_{1}(y)-t\Delta_{12}\widehat{\beta}(y))dt.

By composition estimate in Lemma
4.1
, one has

\Big\|\tfrac{1}{1+\mathscr{I}}\Big\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim 1+\|\mathscr{I}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Thus, applying the law product in Lemma
4.1
implies

\|\Delta_{12}\widehat{\beta}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\left(1+\|\mathscr{I}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)\|\mathcal{B}_{2}^{-1}\Delta_{12}\beta\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\left(1+\|\mathscr{I}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)\|\mathcal{B}_{2}^{-1}\Delta_{12}\beta\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Using (
6.18
), (
6.20
) and (
6.17
) yields

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{B}_{2}^{-1}\Delta_{12}\beta\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Delta_{12}\beta\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\left(1+\|\widehat{\beta}_{2}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)+\|\widehat{\beta}_{2}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\Delta_{12}\beta\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Delta_{12}\beta\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\widehat{\beta}_{2}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\Delta_{12}\beta\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Delta_{12}\beta\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\beta_{2}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\Delta_{12}\beta\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

and

\displaystyle\|\mathscr{I}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\beta_{1}\|_{q,s+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\left(1+\|\widehat{\beta}_{1}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\Delta_{12}\widehat{\beta}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)+\left(\|\widehat{\beta}_{1}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\Delta_{12}\widehat{\beta}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)\|\beta_{1}\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\beta_{1}\|_{q,s+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\Delta_{12}\widehat{\beta}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\beta_{1}\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Putting together the foregoing estimates gives

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}\widehat{\beta}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left(1+\|\beta_{1}\|_{q,s+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\Delta_{12}\widehat{\beta}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\beta_{1}\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)\left(1+\|\beta_{2}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)\|\Delta_{12}\beta\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle+C\left(1+\|\beta_{1}\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\Delta_{12}\widehat{\beta}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\beta_{1}\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)\left(\|\Delta_{12}\beta\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\beta_{2}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\Delta_{12}\beta\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right).

(6.22)

From the triangle inequality, (
6.20
) and (
6.17
), one has

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}\widehat{\beta}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\widehat{\beta}_{1}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\widehat{\beta}_{2}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\beta_{1}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\beta_{2}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\varepsilon_{0}.

From Sobolev embeddings we infer that

\max_{j\in\{1,2\}}\|\beta_{j}\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant\max_{j\in\{1,2\}}\|\beta_{j}\|_{q,2s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant\varepsilon_{0}.

Thus, by choosing
\varepsilon_{0}
small enough, we can ensure

C\|\Delta_{12}\widehat{\beta}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\beta_{1}\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\left(1+\|\beta_{2}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)\|\Delta_{12}\beta\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant\frac{1}{2}\|\Delta_{12}\widehat{\beta}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Inserting this term into the left hand side in (
6.2.1
) and using Sobolev embeddings, we find

\|\Delta_{12}\widehat{\beta}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant C\left(\|\Delta_{12}\beta\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\Delta_{12}\beta\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\max_{j\in\{1,2\}}\|\beta_{j}\|_{q,s+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right).

This ends the proof of Lemma
6.2
.
∎

Now we shall state the main result of this section concerning the reduction of the transport part of the linearized operator
\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon r}.

Proposition 6.2
.

Let
(\gamma,q,d,\tau_{1},s_{0},S,s_{l},\overline{s}_{h},\overline{\mu}_{2})
satisfy (
4.1
), (
4.2
) and (
6.3
). Let
\upsilon\in\left(0,\frac{1}{q+2}\right].
We set

\sigma_{1}=s_{0}+\tau_{1}q+2\tau_{1}+4.

(6.23)

For any
(\mu_{2},\mathtt{p},s_{h})
satisfying

\mu_{2}\geqslant\overline{\mu}_{2}:=4\tau_{1}q+6\tau_{1}+3,\quad\mathtt{p}\geqslant 0,\quad s_{h}\geqslant\max\left(\frac{3}{2}\mu_{2}+s_{l}+1,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}\right),

(6.24)

there exists
\varepsilon_{0}>0
such that if

\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}\leqslant\varepsilon_{0}\quad\textnormal{and}\quad\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s_{h}+\sigma_{1}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant 1,

(6.25)

there exist

c_{i_{0}}\in W^{q,\infty,\gamma}(\mathcal{O},\mathbb{R})\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\beta\in\bigcap_{s\in[s_{0},S]}W^{q,\infty,\gamma}(\mathcal{O},H_{\mbox{\tiny{{odd}}}}^{s})

such that with
\mathscr{B}
defined in (
6.15
) one gets the following results.

(i)

The function
c_{i_{0}}
satisfies the following estimate,

\|c_{i_{0}}-V_{0}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon,

(6.26)

where
V_{0}
is defined in Lemma
\ref{lemma linearized operator at equilibrium}.

(ii)

The transformations
\mathscr{B}^{\pm 1},\mathcal{B}^{\pm 1},{\beta}
and
\widehat{\beta}
satisfy the following estimates for all
s\in[s_{0},S]

\|\mathscr{B}^{\pm 1}\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\mathcal{B}^{\pm 1}\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\|\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{1}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

(6.27)

and

\|\widehat{\beta}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\|\beta\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{1}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right).

(6.28)

(iii)

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
, then in the truncated Cantor set

\mathcal{O}_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0})=\bigcap_{(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{(0,0)\}\atop|l|\leqslant N_{n}}\left\{(\lambda,\omega)\in\mathcal{O}\quad\textnormal{s.t.}\quad\big|\omega\cdot l+jc_{i_{0}}(\lambda,\omega)\big|>\tfrac{4\gamma^{\upsilon}\langle j\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}\right\},

we have

\mathscr{B}^{-1}\big(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+\partial_{\theta}\big(V_{\varepsilon r}\cdot\big)\big)\mathscr{B}=\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+c_{i_{0}}\partial_{\theta}+\mathtt{E}_{n}^{0},

with
\mathtt{E}_{n}^{0}=\mathtt{E}_{n}^{0}(\lambda,\omega,i_{0})
a linear operator satisfying

\|\mathtt{E}_{n}^{0}\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}N_{n+1}^{-\mu_{2}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.29)

(iv)

Given two tori
i_{1}
and
i_{2}
both satisfying (
6.25
), we have

\|\Delta_{12}c_{i}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

(6.30)

and

\|\Delta_{12}\beta\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\Delta_{12}\widehat{\beta}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\sigma_{1}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.31)

Before giving the proof, some remarks are in order.

Remark 6.1
.

•

The final Cantor set
\mathcal{O}_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0})
is constructed over the limit coefficient
c_{i_{0}}
but it is still truncated in the time frequency, that is
|l|\leqslant N_{n}
, leading to a residual remainder with enough decay. This induces a suitable stability property that is crucial during the Nash-Moser scheme achieved with the nonlinear functional.

•

Notice that, since
4\gamma^{\upsilon}\geqslant\gamma
, then looking at
j=0
we find that the Cantor set
\mathcal{O}_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0})
is contained in the Diophantine Cantor set
(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1})\times\mathtt{DC}_{N_{n}}(\gamma,\tau_{1})
introduced in (
5.79
).

•

The parameter
\upsilon
is introduced for technical reasons appearing later in the measure estimates of the final Cantor set and it will be fixed in (
7.64
).

•

The constant
4
used in the definition of the Cantor set
\mathcal{O}_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0})
is useful to ensure the inclusion of this set in all the Cantor sets built in the KAM procedure (see (
6.90
) in the proof below) and also to establish some inclusions related to the final Cantor set (see the proof of Lemma
7.2
).

•

We emphasize here that the functions
\beta
and
\widehat{\beta}
are odd in the sense

\beta(\lambda,\omega,-\varphi,-\theta)=-\beta(\lambda,\omega,\varphi,\theta)\quad\textnormal{and}\quad\widehat{\beta}(\lambda,\omega,-\varphi,-\theta)=-\widehat{\beta}(\lambda,\omega,\varphi,\theta)

(6.32)

which will be crucial later to get the Toeplitz structure of the new remainder term emerging after this reduction.

Proof.

Since we are looking at a state near the disc, we can split
V_{\varepsilon r}
defined by (
3.1
) according to

V_{\varepsilon r}(\lambda,\varphi,\theta)=V_{0}(\lambda)+f_{0}(\lambda,\varphi,\theta),

(6.33)

with
f_{0}
being a perturbation term of small size. We refer to (
6.56
) for a more precise quantification of this smallness. The proof is an iteration process introducing at each step a linear quasi-periodic symplectic change of coordinates. This transformation is linked to the remainder term of the previous step. Roughly speaking, if the latter is of size
\varepsilon
, then we choose the change of coordinates in such a way that we extract the main diagonal part of the previous remainder and keep a new perturbation term of size
\varepsilon^{2}.
The choice of the transformation is done through the resolution of an homological equation requiring non-resonance conditions capted by a suitable selection of the parameters of the system. Thus, by iteration, we can construct a final Cantor set gathering all the parameters restrictions of all steps in which we completely reduced the transport operator into a constant coefficient one. We shall now explain a typical step of the procedure Later, we shall implement the scheme.

(i)-(ii)

\blacktriangleright

KAM step
.
Let us consider a transport operator in the form,

\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+\partial_{\theta}\Big(V+f\Big)

for suitable parameters
(\lambda,\omega)
that belong to a subset
\mathcal{O}_{-}^{\gamma}\subset\mathcal{O}
, where
\mathcal{O}
is the ambient set and

V=V(\lambda,\omega)\quad\textnormal{and}\quad f=f(\lambda,\omega,\varphi,\theta),

where
f
enjoys the following symmetry condition

f(\lambda,\omega,-\varphi,-\theta)=f(\lambda,\omega,\varphi,\theta).

(6.34)

To alleviate the notations we shall use during the proof the variable
\mu:=(\lambda,\omega)
. We consider a symplectic quasi-periodic change of coordinates close to the identity taking the form

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathscr{G}\rho(\mu,\varphi,\theta)&:=&\big(1+\partial_{\theta}g(\mu,\varphi,\theta)\big)\mathcal{G}\rho(\mu,\varphi,\theta)\\
&:=&\big(1+\partial_{\theta}g(\mu,\varphi,\theta)\big)\rho\big(\mu,\varphi,\theta+g(\mu,\varphi,\theta)\big),\end{array}

(6.35)

where
g:\mathcal{O}\times\mathbb{T}^{d+1}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
is a small which will be later linked to
f.
Then, by using Lemma
6.1
, we can write for any
N\geqslant 2

\mathscr{G}^{-1}\Big(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+\partial_{\theta}\left(V+f\right)\Big)\mathscr{G}=\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{G}^{-1}\left(V+\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}g+V\partial_{\theta}g+\Pi_{N}f+\Pi_{N}^{\perp}f+f\partial_{\theta}g\right).

(6.36)

Recall that the projections
\Pi_{N}
are defined in (
4.4
). The basic idea is to obtain after this transformation a new transport operator in the form

\mathscr{G}^{-1}\Big(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+\partial_{\theta}\left(V+f\right)\Big)\mathscr{G}=\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+\partial_{\theta}\big(V_{+}+f_{+}\big),

(6.37)

where

V_{+}=V_{+}(\mu)\quad\textnormal{and}\quad f_{+}=f_{+}(\mu,\varphi,\theta),

with
f_{+}
quadratically smaller than
f.
In order to get rid of the terms wich are not small of quadratic in
f
, then, in view of (
6.36
), we shall select
g
solving the following
homological equation

\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}g+V\partial_{\theta}g+\Pi_{N}f=\langle f\rangle_{\varphi,\theta},

(6.38)

where

\langle f\rangle_{\varphi,\theta}(\mu):=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d+1}}f(\mu,\varphi,\theta)d\varphi d\theta.

To find a solution to the
homological equation
(
6.38
), we use Fourier decomposition and look for
g
in the form

g(\mu,\varphi,\theta):={\rm i}\sum_{(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d+1}\setminus\{0\}\atop\langle l,j\rangle\leqslant N}\tfrac{f_{l,j}(\mu)}{\omega\cdot l+jV(\mu)}e^{{\rm i}(l\cdot\varphi+j\theta)}.

(6.39)

The denominators appearing in the Fourier decomposition of
g
may be small and generate problems in the convergence of the series in (
6.39
) for large
N.
This is a well-knonw phenomenon in KAM theory called ”small divisors problem”. To overcome this difficulty, one has to avoid the resonances and, following the ideas of Kolmogorov, we introduce Diophantine conditions gathered in the following Cantor set

\mathcal{O}_{+}^{\gamma}:=\bigcap_{(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d+1}\setminus\{0\}\atop\langle l,j\rangle\leqslant N}\left\{\mu:=(\lambda,\omega)\in\mathcal{O}_{-}^{\gamma}\quad\textnormal{s.t.}\quad\big|\omega\cdot l+jV(\mu)\big|>\tfrac{\gamma^{\upsilon}\langle j\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}\right\}.

(6.40)

Such a selection of the external parameters allows us to control the size of the denominators in (
6.39
). As we shall see in (
6.48
), the quantification of this control, linked to the parameters
\gamma
and
\tau_{1},
allows to get suitable estimates for
g
with some loss of regularity uniform with respect to
N
. Before performing this estmate, we shall first construct an extension of
g
to the whole set
\mathcal{O}.
In what follows, we still denote
g
this extension. This is done by extending the Fourier coefficients of
g
using the cut-off function
\chi
defined in (
5.81
). More precisely, we define

\displaystyle g_{l,j}(\mu)

\displaystyle:={\rm i}\tfrac{\chi\big((\omega\cdot l+jV(\mu))(\gamma^{\upsilon}\langle j\rangle)^{-1}\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}\big)}{\omega\cdot l+jV(\mu)}f_{l,j}(\mu)

(6.41)

\displaystyle:=\widetilde{g_{l,j}}(\mu)f_{l,j}(\mu).

Notice that the extension
g
is a solution to (
6.38
) only when the parameters are restricted to the Cantor set
\mathcal{O}_{+}^{\gamma}.
Then, we define

V_{+}=V+\langle f\rangle_{\varphi,\theta}\quad\textnormal{and}\quad f_{+}=\mathcal{G}^{-1}\big(\Pi_{N}^{\perp}f+f\partial_{\theta}g\big),

so that in restriction to the Cantor set
\mathcal{O}_{+}^{\gamma},
the identity (
6.37
) holds. Remark that
V_{+}
and
f_{+}
are well-defined in the whole set of parameters
\mathcal{O}
and the function
g
is smooth since it is generated by a finite number of frequencies. According to (
6.34
), we obtain that
g
is odd. As a consequence,

g\in\bigcap_{s\geqslant 0}W^{q,\infty,\gamma}(\mathcal{O},H_{\mbox{\tiny{odd}}}^{s}).

(6.42)

Our next task is to estimate the Fourier coefficients
\widetilde{g_{l,j}}
defined by (
6.41
). Notice that we can write them in the following form

\displaystyle\widetilde{g_{l,j}}(\mu)

\displaystyle={\rm i}\,a_{l,j}\widehat{\chi}(a_{l,j}A_{l,j}(\mu)),\quad\widehat{\chi}(x):=\tfrac{\chi(x)}{x}

(6.43)

\displaystyle A_{l,j}(\mu)

\displaystyle:=\omega\cdot l+jV(\mu),\quad a_{l,j}:=(\gamma^{\upsilon}\langle j\rangle)^{-1}\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}.

Since
\widehat{\chi}
is
C^{\infty}
with bounded derivatives and
\widehat{\chi}(0)=0,
then applying Lemma
4.1
-(vi), we obtain

\forall q^{\prime}\in\llbracket 0,q\rrbracket,\quad\|\widetilde{g_{l,j}}\|_{q^{\prime}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim a_{l,j}^{2}\|A_{l,j}\|_{q^{\prime}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\Big(1+a_{l,j}^{q^{\prime}-1}\|A_{l,j}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{O})}^{q^{\prime}-1}\Big).

Direct computations lead to

\displaystyle\forall(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d+1},\,\forall\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{d+1},\quad|\alpha|\leqslant q,\quad\sup_{\mu\in\mathcal{O}}\left|\partial_{\mu}^{\alpha}A(\mu)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\langle l,j\rangle\max\left(1,\sup_{\mu\in\mathcal{O}}\left|\partial_{\mu}^{\alpha}V(\mu)\right|\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma^{-|\alpha|}\langle l,j\rangle\max\left(1,\|V\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right).

Assuming

\|V\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant C,

(6.44)

we then obtain

\forall q^{\prime}\in\llbracket 0,q\rrbracket,\quad\forall(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d+1},\quad\|A_{l,j}\|_{q^{\prime}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\langle l,j\rangle.

(6.45)

Added to the fact that
0\leqslant a_{l,j}\leqslant\gamma^{-\upsilon}\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}
, we then find that

\forall q^{\prime}\in\llbracket 0,q\rrbracket,\quad\|\widetilde{g_{l,j}}\|_{q^{\prime}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\gamma^{-\upsilon(q^{\prime}+1)}\langle l,j\rangle^{\tau_{1}q^{\prime}+\tau_{1}+q^{\prime}}.

(6.46)

Our choice of
\upsilon
in Proposition
6.2
implies in particular that

\upsilon\leqslant\tfrac{1}{q+1}.

(6.47)

Therefore, we deduce from (
6.41
) and Leibniz rule that for all
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{d+1}
with
|\alpha|\leqslant q

\displaystyle\gamma^{2|\alpha|}\|\partial_{\mu}^{\alpha}g(\mu,\cdot,\centerdot)\|_{H^{s-|\alpha|}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d+1}\setminus\{(0,0)\}\atop\langle l,j\rangle\leqslant N}\sum_{\beta\in\mathbb{N}^{d+1}\atop\beta\leqslant\alpha}\gamma^{2|\alpha|-2|\beta|}\big|\partial_{\mu}^{\alpha-\beta}\widetilde{g_{l,j}}(\mu)\big|^{2}\gamma^{2|\beta|}\big|\partial_{\mu}^{\beta}f_{l,j}(\mu)\big|^{2}\langle l,j\rangle^{2s-2|\alpha|}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d+1}\setminus\{(0,0)\}\atop\langle l,j\rangle\leqslant N}\sum_{\beta\in\mathbb{N}^{d+1}\atop\beta\leqslant\alpha}\left(\|\widetilde{g_{l,j}}\|_{|\alpha|-|\beta|}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)^{2}\gamma^{2|\beta|}\big|\partial_{\mu}^{\beta}f_{l,j}(\mu)\big|^{2}\langle l,j\rangle^{2s-2|\alpha|}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d+1}\setminus\{(0,0)\}\atop\langle l,j\rangle\leqslant N}\sum_{\beta\in\mathbb{N}^{d+1}\atop\beta\leqslant\alpha}\gamma^{-2}\gamma^{2|\beta|}\big|\partial_{\mu}^{\beta}f_{l,j}(\mu)\big|^{2}\langle l,j\rangle^{2(s+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}-|\beta|)}.

As a consequence, by interverting the summation symbols, we find

\|g\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\gamma^{-1}\|\Pi_{N}f\|_{q,s+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.48)

Assume now that

\gamma^{-1}N^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1}\|f\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant\varepsilon_{0}.

(6.49)

Then added to (
6.48
) and Lemma
4.1
-(ii), we get

\|g\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant C\gamma^{-1}N^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}}\|f\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant C\varepsilon_{0}.

On the other hand if we assume

\|f\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right),

then (
6.48
) gives

\displaystyle\|g\|_{q,2s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma^{-1}\|f\|_{q,2s_{0}+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,2s_{0}+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s_{h}+\sigma_{1}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right).

Notice that to obtain the last inequality we used the fact that (
6.24
) and (
6.23
) imply

2s_{0}+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+2\leqslant s_{h}+\sigma_{1}.

Using interpolation inequality and (
6.25
), one gets for some
\overline{\theta}\in(0,1).

\displaystyle\|g\|_{q,2s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\|g\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)^{\overline{\theta}}\left(\|g\|_{q,2s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)^{1-\overline{\theta}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}.

(6.50)

Thus, taking
\varepsilon_{0}
small enough, we can ensure the smallness condition in Lemma
6.2
and get that the linear operator
\mathscr{G}
is invertible. Now, we introduce

u=\Pi_{N}^{\perp}f+f\partial_{\theta}g.

By the triangle inequality, Lemma
4.1
-(ii) and (
6.48
), we obtain for all
s\in[s_{0},S]

\displaystyle\|u\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\Pi_{N}^{\perp}f\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+C\Big(\|f\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\partial_{\theta}g\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|f\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\partial_{\theta}g\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\Pi_{N}^{\perp}f\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+C\Big(\|f\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|g\|_{q,s+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|f\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|g\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\Pi_{N}^{\perp}f\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+C\gamma^{-1}N^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1}\|f\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|f\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Combined with Lemma
6.2
, Lemma
4.1
-(ii) and (
6.49
), we get for all
s\in[s_{0},S]

\displaystyle\|f_{+}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle=\|\mathcal{G}^{-1}(u)\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|u\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+C\Big(\|u\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\widehat{g}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\widehat{g}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|u\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leqslant\|u\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+C\Big(\|u\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|g\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|g\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|u\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\Pi_{N}^{\perp}f\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+C\gamma^{-1}N^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1}\|f\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|f\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Using once again Lemma
4.1
-(ii), we find for
S\geqslant\overline{s}\geqslant s\geqslant s_{0}

\|f_{+}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant N^{s-\overline{s}}\|f\|_{q,\overline{s}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+C\gamma^{-1}N^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1}\|f\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|f\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.51)

\blacktriangleright

KAM scheme.
Let us now assume that we have constructed
V_{m}
and
f_{m}
, well-defined in the whole set of parameters
\mathcal{O}
and satisfying the assumptions (
6.44
) and (
6.49
). We shall now construct the corresponding quantity at the next order, namely
V_{m+1}
and
f_{m+1}
, still satisfying (
6.44
) and (
6.49
). For this aim, we shall implement the KAM step with
(V,f,V_{+},f_{+},N)
replaced by
(V_{m},f_{m},V_{m+1},f_{m+1},N_{m}).
More precisely, we will shall prove by induction the existence of a sequence
\{V_{m},f_{m}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
such that

\delta_{m}(s_{l})\leqslant\delta_{0}(s_{h})N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}N_{m}^{-\mu_{2}}\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\delta_{m}(s_{h})\leqslant\left(2-\tfrac{1}{m+1}\right)\delta_{0}(s_{h})

(6.52)

and

\|V_{m}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant C\quad\textnormal{and}\quad N_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1}\delta_{m}(s_{0})\leqslant\varepsilon_{0},

(6.53)

with
f_{m}
satisfying the following symmetry condition

f_{m}(\mu,-\varphi,-\theta)=f_{m}(\mu,\varphi,\theta)

(6.54)

and where we denote

\delta_{m}(s):=\gamma^{-1}\|f_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Recall that the parameters
s_{l}
and
s_{h}
were intruduced in (
6.3
) and (
6.24
).

➢
Initialization.
We shall first check that the estimates (
6.52
) and (
6.53
) are satisfied for
m=0.
In which case the functions
V_{0}
and
f_{0}
are defined by (
3.11
) and (
6.33
). By (
5.48
) and (
6.7
) we infer

\displaystyle\delta_{0}(s)

\displaystyle=\gamma^{-1}\|V_{\varepsilon r}-V_{0}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|r\|_{q,s+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right).

(6.55)

Thus, the notation (
6.24
) and the smallness condition (
6.25
) imply that

N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}\delta_{0}(s_{h})\leqslant C\varepsilon_{0}.

(6.56)

In addition, by (
6.4
) and (
3.3
), we deduce that
f_{0}
satisfies the following symmetry condition

f_{0}(\lambda,-\varphi,-\theta)=f_{0}(\lambda,\varphi,\theta).

(6.57)

We set
\mathcal{O}_{0}^{\gamma}=\mathcal{O}
and consider
N_{0}\geqslant 2.
Our next task is to check that the assumptions (
6.44
) and (
6.49
) are satisfied by
V_{0}
and
f_{0}.
First recall that
V_{0}
is defined by

V_{0}(\lambda)=\Omega+I_{1}(\lambda)K_{1}(\lambda).

Using the smooth regularity of (
A.9
), we obtain

\|V_{0}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant C.

(6.58)

Therefore, the required boundedness property (
6.44
) is satisfied with
V=V_{0}.
Now by (
6.24
), we have

\mu_{2}\geqslant\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+2.

(6.59)

Hence, using (
6.56
), we obtain

\displaystyle\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1}\|f_{0}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle=N_{0}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1}\delta_{0}(s_{0})

\displaystyle\leqslant N_{0}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1-\mu_{2}}N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}\delta_{0}(s_{h})

\displaystyle\leqslant C\varepsilon_{0}N_{0}^{-1}.

By taking
N_{0}
large enough we get

CN_{0}^{-1}\leqslant 1,

(6.60)

so that

\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1}\|f_{0}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant\varepsilon_{0}.

Hence, the assumption (
6.49
) is satisfied for
f=f_{0}.
This ends the initialization step.

➢
Iteration.
let us now assume that we have constructed
V_{m}
and
f_{m}
enjoying the properties (
6.52
), (
6.53
) and (
6.54
). We shall see how to construct
V_{m+1}
and
f_{m+1}
. According to the KAM step, we consider a symplectic quasi-periodic change of variables
\mathscr{G}_{m}
taking the form

\displaystyle\mathscr{G}_{m}\rho(\mu,\varphi,\theta)

\displaystyle:=\big(1+\partial_{\theta}g_{m}(\mu,\varphi,\theta)\big)\mathcal{G}_{m}\rho(\mu,\varphi,\theta)

\displaystyle=\big(1+\partial_{\theta}g_{m}(\mu,\varphi,\theta)\big)\rho\big(\mu,\varphi,\theta+g_{m}(\mu,\varphi,\theta)\big),

with

g_{m}(\mu,\varphi,\theta):={\rm i}\sum_{(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d+1}\setminus\{0\}\atop\langle l,j\rangle\leqslant N_{m}}\tfrac{\chi\big((\omega\cdot l+jV_{m}(\mu))(\gamma^{\upsilon}\langle j\rangle)^{-1}\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}\big)}{\omega\cdot l+jV_{m}(\mu)}(f_{m})_{l,j}(\mu)\,e^{i(l\cdot\varphi+j\theta)},

(6.61)

where
\chi
is the cut-off function introduced in (
5.81
) and
N_{m}
is defined in (
5.82
). As explained in the KAM step,
g_{m}
is well-defined on the whole set of parameters
\mathcal{O}
and solves the
homological equation

\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}g_{m}+V_{m}\partial_{\theta}g_{m}+\Pi_{N_{m}}f_{m}=\langle f_{m}\rangle_{\varphi,\theta}

when restricted to the Cantor set

\mathcal{O}_{m+1}^{\gamma}:=\bigcap_{\underset{\langle l,j\rangle\leqslant N_{m}}{(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d+1}\setminus\{0\}}}\left\{\mu=(\lambda,\omega)\in\mathcal{O}_{m}^{\gamma}\quad\textnormal{s.t.}\quad\big|\omega\cdot l+jV_{m}(\mu)\big|>\tfrac{\gamma^{\upsilon}\langle j\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}\right\}.

(6.62)

Hence, in the Cantor set
\mathcal{O}_{m+1}^{\gamma},
the following reduction holds

\mathscr{G}_{m}^{-1}\Big(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+\partial_{\theta}(V_{m}+f_{m})\Big)\mathscr{G}_{m}=\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+\partial_{\theta}(V_{m+1}+f_{m+1}),

with
V_{m+1}
and
f_{m+1}
defined by

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}V_{m+1}=V_{m}+\langle f_{m}\rangle_{\varphi,\theta}\\
f_{m+1}=\mathcal{G}_{m}^{-1}\Big(\Pi_{N_{m}}^{\perp}f_{m}+f_{m}\partial_{\theta}g_{m}\Big).\end{array}\right.

(6.63)

In view of (
6.54
), the function
f_{m}
is even and therefore
g_{m}
is odd. Consequently, we deduce through elementary manipulations that
f_{m+1}
is also even. This allows us to follow the symmetry persistence along the scheme.
Besides, in a similar way to (
6.42
), one obtains

g_{m}\in\bigcap_{s\geqslant 0}W^{q,\infty,\gamma}(\mathcal{O},H_{\mbox{\tiny{odd}}}^{s}).

(6.64)

Now, we set

\mathscr{B}_{-1}=\mathscr{G}_{-1}=\textnormal{Id}\quad\textnormal{ and }\quad\forall m\in\mathbb{N},\,\,\mathscr{B}_{m}=\mathscr{G}_{0}\circ\mathscr{G}_{1}\circ...\circ\mathscr{G}_{m}.

One easily finds that

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}_{m}\rho(\mu,\varphi,\theta)

\displaystyle=\big(1+\partial_{\theta}\beta_{m}(\mu,\varphi,\theta)\big)\mathcal{B}_{m}\rho(\mu,\varphi,\theta)

\displaystyle=\big(1+\partial_{\theta}\beta_{m}(\mu,\varphi,\theta)\big)\rho\big(\mu,\varphi,\theta+\beta_{m}(\mu,\varphi,\theta)\big),

where the sequence
(\beta_{m})_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
is defined by
\beta_{-1}=g_{-1}=0
and

\beta_{0}=g_{0}\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\beta_{m}(\mu,\varphi,\theta)=\beta_{m-1}(\mu,\varphi,\theta)+g_{m}\big(\mu,\varphi,\theta+\beta_{m-1}(\mu,\varphi,\theta)\big).

(6.65)

A trivial induction based on (
6.64
) yields

\beta_{m}\in\bigcap_{s\geqslant 0}W^{q,\infty,\gamma}(\mathcal{O},H_{\mbox{\tiny{odd}}}^{s}).

(6.66)

According to Sobolev embeddings, (
6.63
) and the induction assumption (
6.52
), we infer

\displaystyle\|V_{m}-V_{m-1}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle=\|\langle f_{m-1}\rangle_{\varphi,\theta}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|f_{m-1}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle=\gamma\delta_{m-1}(s_{0})

\displaystyle\leqslant\gamma\delta_{0}(s_{h})N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}N_{m-1}^{-\mu_{2}}.

(6.67)

As a consequence, by using the triangle inequality, (
6.56
) and choosing
\varepsilon_{0}
small enough we deduce

\displaystyle\|V_{m}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|V_{m-1}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\gamma\delta_{0}(s_{h})N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}N_{m-1}^{-\mu_{2}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\displaystyle\|V_{0}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\gamma\delta_{0}(s_{h})N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}\left(\sum_{k=0}^{m-1}N_{k}^{-\mu_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\displaystyle\|V_{0}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}N_{k}^{-\mu_{2}}.

Now, remark that (
6.24
) implies in particular

\mu_{2}\geqslant\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+2.

Hence, by the induction hypothesis (
6.52
), (
6.56
), (
6.59
) and (
6.60
), we have

\displaystyle\delta_{m}(s_{0})N_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1}

\displaystyle\leqslant\delta_{0}(s_{h})N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}N_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1-\mu_{2}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\varepsilon_{0}N_{0}^{-1}

\displaystyle\leqslant\varepsilon_{0}.

(6.68)

Using (
6.58
) and the previous estimate, we deduce that

\sup_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\|V_{m}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant C\quad\textnormal{and}\quad\delta_{m}(s_{0})N_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1}\leqslant\varepsilon_{0}.

(6.69)

Thus, the KAM step applies and, in particular, the estimate (
6.51
) becomes

\delta_{m+1}(s)\leqslant N_{m}^{s-\overline{s}}\delta_{m}(\overline{s})+CN_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1}\delta_{m}(s)\delta_{m}(s_{0}).

(6.70)

If we apply (
6.70
) with
s=s_{l}
and
\overline{s}=s_{h},
we obtain

\delta_{m+1}(s_{l})\leqslant N_{m}^{s_{l}-s_{h}}\delta_{m}(s_{h})+CN_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1}\delta_{m}(s_{l})\delta_{m}(s_{0}).

Using the induction assumption (
6.52
) and the fact that
s_{l}\geqslant s_{0}
yields

\displaystyle\delta_{m+1}(s_{l})

\displaystyle\leqslant N_{m}^{s_{l}-s_{h}}\delta_{m}(s_{h})+CN_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1}(\delta_{m}(s_{l}))^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\left(2-\tfrac{1}{m+1}\right)N_{m}^{s_{l}-s_{h}}\delta_{0}(s_{h})+CN_{0}^{2\mu_{2}}N_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1-2\mu_{2}}(\delta_{0}(s_{h}))^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant 2N_{m}^{s_{l}-s_{h}}\delta_{0}(s_{h})+CN_{0}^{2\mu_{2}}N_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1-2\mu_{2}}(\delta_{0}(s_{h}))^{2}.

The conditions (
6.24
) imply

s_{h}\geqslant\tfrac{3}{2}\mu_{2}+s_{l}+1,\quad\textnormal{and}\quad\mu_{2}\geqslant 2(\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1)+1.

Also, using the fact that
N_{0}\geqslant 2
and choosing
\varepsilon_{0}
small enough, we get in view of (
6.56
),

4N_{0}^{-\mu_{2}}\leqslant 1\quad\textnormal{and}\quad 2C\delta_{0}(s_{h})N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}\leqslant 1.

As a consequence, one has

N_{m}^{s_{l}-s_{h}}\leqslant\tfrac{1}{4}N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}N_{m+1}^{-\mu_{2}}\quad\textnormal{and}\quad CN_{0}^{2\mu_{2}}N_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1-2\mu_{2}}\delta_{0}(s_{h})\leqslant\tfrac{1}{2}N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}N_{m+1}^{-\mu_{2}},

(6.71)

which implies in turn

\delta_{m+1}(s_{l})\leqslant\delta_{0}(s_{h})N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}N_{m+1}^{-\mu_{2}}.

This proves the first statement of the induction in (
6.52
) and we now turn to the proof of the second statement. Applying (
6.70
) with
s=\overline{s}=s_{h}
and and using the induction (
6.52
), we get

\displaystyle\delta_{m+1}(s_{h})

\displaystyle\leqslant\delta_{m}(s_{h})\left(1+CN_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1}\delta_{m}(s_{0})\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\left(2-\tfrac{1}{m+1}\right)\delta_{0}(s_{h})\left(1+CN_{0}^{\mu_{2}}N_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1-\mu_{2}}\delta_{0}(s_{h})\right).

Notice that if the condition

\left(2-\tfrac{1}{m+1}\right)\left(1+CN_{0}^{\mu_{2}}N_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1-\mu_{2}}\delta_{0}(s_{h})\right)\leqslant 2-\tfrac{1}{m+2}

(6.72)

holds true, then

\delta_{m+1}(s_{h})\leqslant\left(2-\tfrac{1}{m+2}\right)\delta_{0}(s_{h}),

which achieves the induction argument of (
6.52
). Notice that (
6.72
) is equivalent to

\left(2-\tfrac{1}{m+1}\right)CN_{0}^{\mu_{2}}N_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1-\mu_{2}}\delta_{0}(s_{h})\leqslant\tfrac{1}{(m+1)(m+2)}\cdot

Using (
6.59
), the preceding condition holds true if

CN_{0}^{\mu_{2}}N_{m}^{-1}\delta_{0}(s_{h})\leqslant\tfrac{1}{(m+1)(m+2)}\cdot

(6.73)

Since
N_{0}\geqslant 2
, then in view of (
5.82
) there exists a small enough constant
c_{0}>0
such that

\forall m\in\mathbb{N},\quad c_{0}N_{m}^{-1}\leqslant\tfrac{1}{(m+1)(m+2)}\cdot

Consequently, (
6.73
) is ensured provided that

CN_{0}^{\mu_{2}}\delta_{0}(s_{h})\leqslant c_{0}.

(6.74)

Choosing
\varepsilon_{0}
small enough and making use of (
6.56
), we obtain

\displaystyle CN_{0}^{\mu_{2}}\delta_{0}(s_{h})

\displaystyle\leqslant C\varepsilon_{0}

\displaystyle\leqslant c_{0}.

Hence, the condition (
6.74
) is satisfied and the proof of (
6.52
) is now achieved.

➢
Persistence of the regularity.
Putting together (
6.70
), applied with
\overline{s}=s\in[s_{0},S]
, (
6.52
) and (
6.59
), we infer

\displaystyle\delta_{m+1}(s)

\displaystyle\leqslant\delta_{m}(s)\left(1+CN_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1}\delta_{m}(s_{0})\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\delta_{m}(s)\left(1+C\delta_{0}(s_{h})N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}N_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1-\mu_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\delta_{m}(s)\left(1+CN_{m}^{-1}\right).

Gathering this estimate with (
6.2.1
), implies, up to a trivial induction,

\displaystyle\delta_{m}(s)

\displaystyle\leqslant\delta_{0}(s)\prod_{k=0}^{\infty}\left(1+CN_{k}^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\delta_{0}(s)

(6.75)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right).

Then, (
6.48
), interpolation inequality in Lemma
4.1
and (
6.52
) give

\displaystyle\|g_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\delta_{m}(s+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1})

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left(\delta_{m}(s_{0})\right)^{\overline{\theta}(s)}\big(\delta_{m}(s+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1)\big)^{1-\overline{\theta}(s)}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\delta_{0}^{\overline{\theta}(s)}(s_{h})\delta_{0}^{1-\overline{\theta}(s)}(s+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1)N_{0}^{\overline{\theta}(s)\mu_{2}}N_{m}^{-\overline{\theta}(s)\mu_{2}},

with
\overline{\theta}(s):=\frac{1}{s+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1-s_{0}}.

From (
6.2.1
), (
6.25
) and (
6.2.1
), we deduce

\displaystyle\|g_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s_{h}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)N_{0}^{\overline{\theta}(s)\mu_{2}}N_{m}^{-\overline{\theta}(s)\mu_{2}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)N_{0}^{\overline{\theta}(s)\mu_{2}}N_{m}^{-\overline{\theta}(s)\mu_{2}}.

(6.76)

Using (
6.65
) and (
6.18
), we get for all
s\in[s_{0},S]

\|\beta_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant\|\beta_{m-1}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\left(1+C\|g_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)+C\left(1+\|\beta_{m-1}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)\|g_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.77)

If we apply this estimate with
s=s_{0}
and use Sobolev embeddings, we deduce

\|\beta_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant\|\beta_{m-1}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\left(1+C\|g_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)+C\|g_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

The previous two expressions make appear recurrent relation for the weighted norms of the sequence
(\beta_{m})_{m}.
To get good estimate for
\beta_{m}
, we shall make use the following result which is quite easy to prove by induction : Given three positive sequences
(a_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}},(b_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
and
(c_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
satisfying

\forall\,n\in\mathbb{N},\quad a_{n+1}\leqslant b_{n}a_{n}+c_{n},

we have

\displaystyle\forall\,n\geqslant 2,\quad a_{n}

\displaystyle\leqslant a_{0}\prod_{i=0}^{n-1}b_{i}+\sum_{k=0}^{n-2}c_{k}\prod_{i=k+1}^{n-1}b_{i}+c_{n-1}

\displaystyle\leqslant\Big(a_{0}+\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}c_{k}\Big)\prod_{i=0}^{n-1}b_{i}.

(6.78)

In particular, if
\displaystyle\prod_{n=0}^{\infty}b_{n}
and
\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}c_{n}
converge then

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}a_{n}

\displaystyle\leqslant\Big(a_{0}+\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}c_{n}\Big)\prod_{n=0}^{\infty}b_{i}.

(6.79)

Since the conditions (
6.24
) and (
6.23
) imply

s_{0}+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+2\leqslant s_{h}+\sigma_{1}\quad\textnormal{and}\quad\overline{\theta}(s_{0})\mu_{2}\geqslant 1,

(6.80)

then, from (
6.2.1
) and (
6.25
), we deduce

\displaystyle\|g_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s_{0}+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)N_{m}^{-\overline{\theta}(s_{0})\mu_{2}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\varepsilon_{0}N_{m}^{-1}.

Choosing
\varepsilon_{0}
small enough to ensure
C\varepsilon_{0}\leqslant 1
,
N_{0}
sufficiently large to ensure
\displaystyle\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}N_{m}^{-1}<\infty
and we can apply (
6.79
) together with the fact that
\beta_{0}=g_{0}
to obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\|\beta_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\left(\|\beta_{0}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+C\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\|g_{k}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)\prod_{k=0}^{\infty}\left(1+C\|g_{k}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\left(1+C\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}N_{k}^{-1}\right)\prod_{k=0}^{\infty}\left(1+N_{k}^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant C.

(6.81)

Hence the sequence
\left(\|\beta_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
is bounded and inserting this information in (
6.77
) gives for all
s\in[s_{0},S]

\|\beta_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant\|\beta_{m-1}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\left(1+C\|g_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)+C\|g_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Similarly to what preceeds, if we apply (
6.79
) and (
6.2.1
), we infer

\displaystyle\sup_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\|\beta_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\left(\|\beta_{0}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+C\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\|g_{k}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)\prod_{k=0}^{\infty}\left(1+C\|g_{k}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)\left(1+N_{0}^{\overline{\theta}(s)\mu_{2}}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}N_{k}^{-\overline{\theta}(s)\mu_{2}}\right).

From Lemma
A.1
we get

\forall s\in[s_{0},S],\quad N_{0}^{\overline{\theta}(s)\mu_{2}}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}N_{k}^{-\overline{\theta}(s)\mu_{2}}\lesssim 1

which implies in turn

\forall s\in[s_{0},S],\quad\sup_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\|\beta_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant C\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right).

(6.82)

From the condition (
6.3
) we have

s_{l}=s_{0}+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+2,

and consequently we deduce from (
6.18
), (
6.2.1
), (
6.48
) and (
6.52
),

\displaystyle\|\beta_{m}-\beta_{m-1}\|_{q,s_{0}+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\|g_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\left(1+\|\beta_{m-1}\|_{q,s_{0}+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\|g_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant C\delta_{m}(s_{l})

\displaystyle\leqslant CN_{0}^{\mu_{2}}N_{m}^{-\mu_{2}}\delta_{0}(s_{h}).

(6.83)

Applying once again Lemma
A.1
, we deduce that

\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\|\beta_{m}-\beta_{m-1}\|_{q,s_{0}+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant C\delta_{0}(s_{h}).

Hence there exists
\beta\in W^{q,\infty,\gamma}(\mathcal{O},H^{s_{0}+2})
such that

\beta_{m}\underset{m\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}\beta\quad\textnormal{(strongly) in}\quad W^{q,\infty,\gamma}(\mathcal{O},H^{s_{0}+2}).

By (
6.82
) the sequence
\left(\beta_{m}\right)_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
is bounded in
W^{q,\infty,\gamma}(\mathcal{O},H^{s})
, then by a weak-compactness argument we find that
\beta\in W^{q,\infty,\gamma}(\mathcal{O},H^{s})
.
Using (
6.82
), we obtain

\displaystyle\forall s\in[s_{0},S],\quad\|\beta\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\liminf_{m\rightarrow\infty}\|\beta_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right).

(6.84)

We then can consider the quasi-periodic symplectic change of variables
\mathscr{B}
associated with
\beta
and defined by

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}\rho(\lambda,\omega,\varphi,\theta)

\displaystyle=\left(1+\partial_{\theta}\beta(\lambda,\omega,\varphi,\theta)\right)\mathcal{B}\rho(\lambda,\omega,\varphi,\theta)

\displaystyle=\left(1+\partial_{\theta}\beta(\lambda,\omega,\varphi,\theta)\right)\rho(\lambda,\omega,\varphi,\theta+\beta(\lambda,\omega,\varphi,\theta)).

By (
6.2.1
), (
6.80
) and (
6.25
), we have

\|\beta\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s_{0}+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}.

(6.85)

Proceeding as for (
6.50
), using interpolation (
6.2.1
), (
6.85
), (
6.25
) and the fact that
2s_{0}+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+3\leqslant s_{h}+\sigma_{1}
, one obtains

\|\beta\|_{q,2s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}.

Therefore, choosing
\varepsilon_{0}
small enough, we deduce in view of Lemma
6.2
that
\mathscr{B}
is an invertible operator. Moreover, by (
6.19
) and (
6.2.1
), we get

\|\mathscr{B}^{\pm 1}\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\|\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+3}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.86)

In addition, by (
6.66
), and Sobolev embeddings (to get pointwise convergence), we find

\beta\in\bigcap_{s\in[s_{0},S]}W^{q,\infty,\gamma}(\mathcal{O},H_{\mbox{\tiny{odd}}}^{s}).

We also have an estimate of the rate of convergence for the sequence
(\beta_{m})_{m}
towards
\beta
,

\displaystyle\|\beta-\beta_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{k=m}^{\infty}\|\beta_{k+1}-\beta_{k}\|_{q,s_{0}+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma\delta_{0}(s_{h})N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}\sum_{k=m+1}^{\infty}N_{k}^{-\mu_{2}}.

(6.87)

From Lemma
A.1
, one obtains

\sum_{k=m}^{\infty}N_{k}^{-\mu_{2}}\underset{m\rightarrow\infty}{=}O\left(N_{m}^{-\mu_{2}}\right).

(6.88)

Gathering (
6.88
), (
6.2.1
) and (
6.2.1
), we get

\displaystyle\|\beta-\beta_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma\delta_{0}(s_{h})N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}N_{m+1}^{-\mu_{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}N_{m+1}^{-\mu_{2}}.

(6.89)

\blacktriangleright

KAM conclusion

By (
6.2.1
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\|V_{m+1}-V_{m}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\gamma\delta_{0}(s_{h})N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}N_{m}^{-\mu_{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma\delta_{0}(s_{h}).

We deduce that the sequence
(V_{m})_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
is convergent in
W^{q,\infty,\gamma}(\mathcal{O},\mathbb{C})
and let us denote by
c_{i_{0}}
its limit. Moreover, we have by (
6.88
), (
6.2.1
) and (
6.25
)

\displaystyle\|c_{i_{0}}-V_{0}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\|V_{m+1}-V_{m}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma\delta_{0}(s_{h})\lesssim\varepsilon\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s_{h}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon.

Now, we introduce the truncated Cantor set

\mathcal{O}_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0})=\bigcap_{(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{(0,0\}\atop|l|\leqslant N_{n}}\left\{\mu:=(\lambda,\omega)\in\mathcal{O}\quad\textnormal{s.t.}\quad\big|\omega\cdot l+jc_{i_{0}}(\mu)\big|>\tfrac{4\gamma^{\upsilon}\langle j\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}\right\}.

In what follows, we shall prove that the Cantor set
\mathcal{O}_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0})
satisfies the inclusion

\mathcal{O}_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0})\subset\bigcap_{m=0}^{n+1}\mathcal{O}_{m}^{\gamma}=\mathcal{O}_{n+1}^{\gamma},

where the intermediate Cantor sets are defined in (
6.62
). For this aim, we shall argue by induction. We first remark that by construction
\mathcal{O}_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0})\subset\mathcal{O}=:\mathcal{O}_{0}^{\gamma}.
Now assume that
\mathcal{O}_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0})\subset\mathcal{O}_{m}^{\gamma}
for
m\leqslant n
and let us check that

\mathcal{O}_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0})\subset\mathcal{O}_{m+1}^{\gamma}.

(6.90)

Putting together (
6.2.1
) and (
6.88
) we infer

\displaystyle\|V_{m}-c_{i_{0}}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{l=m}^{\infty}\|V_{l+1}-V_{l}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\gamma\delta_{0}(s_{h})N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}\sum_{l=m}^{\infty}N_{l}^{-\mu_{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma\delta_{0}(s_{h})N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}N_{m}^{-\mu_{2}}.

(6.91)

Given
\mu\in\mathcal{O}_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0})
and
(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
such that
0\leqslant|l|\leqslant N_{m},
we have then
|l|\leqslant N_{n}
and by triangle inequality,

\displaystyle|\omega\cdot l+jV_{m}(\mu)|

\displaystyle\geqslant|\omega\cdot l+jc_{i_{0}}(\mu)|-|j||V_{m}(\mu)-c_{i_{0}}(\mu)|

\displaystyle\geqslant\displaystyle\tfrac{4\gamma^{\upsilon}\langle j\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}-C\langle j\rangle\gamma\delta_{0}(s_{h})N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}N_{m}^{-\mu_{2}}

\displaystyle\geqslant\displaystyle\tfrac{4\gamma^{\upsilon}\langle j\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}-C\langle j\rangle\gamma^{\upsilon}\varepsilon_{0}\langle l\rangle^{-\mu_{2}}.

Since (
6.24
) implies
\mu_{2}\geqslant\tau_{1}
, then taking
\varepsilon_{0}\leqslant\frac{1}{C}
, we deduce from the previous estimate

|\omega\cdot l+jV_{m}(\mu)|>\displaystyle\tfrac{\gamma^{\upsilon}\langle j\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}.

Consequently,
\mu\in\mathcal{O}_{m+1}^{\gamma}
and the inclusion (
6.90
) holds.

(iii)
We can write for all
n\in\mathbb{N},

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}^{-1}\Big(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+\partial_{\theta}\left(V_{0}+f_{0}\right)\Big)\mathscr{B}

\displaystyle=\left(\mathscr{B}^{-1}-\mathscr{B}_{n}^{-1}\right)\Big(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+\partial_{\theta}\left(V_{0}+f_{0}\right)\Big)\mathscr{B}

\displaystyle+\mathscr{B}_{n}^{-1}\Big(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+\partial_{\theta}\left(V_{0}+f_{0}\right)\Big)\left(\mathscr{B}-\mathscr{B}_{n}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathscr{B}_{n}^{-1}\Big(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+\partial_{\theta}\left(V_{0}+f_{0}\right)\Big)\mathscr{B}_{n}.

In view of (
6.90
) and the definition of
\mathscr{B}_{n}
, we have in the Cantor set
\mathcal{O}_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0})

\mathscr{B}_{n}^{-1}\Big(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+\partial_{\theta}\left(V_{0}+f_{0}\right)\Big)\mathscr{B}_{n}=\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+\partial_{\theta}\left(V_{n+1}+f_{n+1}\right).

Therefore, in the Cantor set
\mathcal{O}_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0}),
the following decomposition holds

\mathscr{B}^{-1}\Big(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+\partial_{\theta}\left(V_{0}+f_{0}\right)\Big)\mathscr{B}=\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+c_{i_{0}}\partial_{\theta}+\mathtt{E}_{n}^{0}(i_{0}),

where

\displaystyle\mathtt{E}_{n}^{0}(i_{0})

\displaystyle:=\left(V_{n+1}-c_{i_{0}}\right)\partial_{\theta}+\partial_{\theta}\left(f_{n+1}\cdot\right)+\left(\mathscr{B}^{-1}-\mathscr{B}_{n}^{-1}\right)\Big(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+\partial_{\theta}\left(V_{0}+f_{0}\right)\Big)\mathscr{B}

\displaystyle\quad+\mathscr{B}^{-1}\Big(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+\partial_{\theta}\left(V_{0}+f_{0}\right)\Big)\left(\mathscr{B}-\mathscr{B}_{n}\right)

\displaystyle:=\mathtt{E}_{n,1}^{0}+\mathtt{E}_{n,2}^{0}+\mathtt{E}_{n,3}^{0}+\mathtt{E}_{n,4}^{0}.

By the law products in Lemma
4.1
, (
6.2.1
) and (
6.2.1
) we have

\displaystyle\|\mathtt{E}_{n,1}^{0}\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|V_{n+1}-c_{i_{0}}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma\delta_{0}(s_{h})N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}N_{n+1}^{-\mu_{2}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}N_{n+1}^{-\mu_{2}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.92)

From (
6.52
) and since (
6.3
) implies in particular
s_{l}\geqslant s_{0}+1,
we obtain

\displaystyle\|\mathtt{E}_{n,2}^{0}\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma\delta_{n+1}(s_{0}+1)\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma\delta_{0}(s_{h})N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}N_{n+1}^{-\mu_{2}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}N_{n+1}^{-\mu_{2}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.93)

We now turn to the estimate of
\mathtt{E}_{n,4}^{0}.
First remark that by the law products in Lemma
4.1
, we have

\displaystyle\|\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}\rho+\partial_{\theta}\left(V_{\varepsilon r}\rho\right)\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\partial_{\theta}\left(V_{\varepsilon r}\rho\right)\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\left(1+\|V_{\varepsilon r}\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right).

But combining (
6.33
), (
6.58
), (
6.2.1
) and (
6.25
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|V_{\varepsilon r}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|V_{0}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|f_{0}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C+C\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C.

Therefore, we get

\|\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}\rho+\partial_{\theta}\left(V_{\varepsilon r}\rho\right)\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.94)

Putting together (
6.94
), (
6.86
) and (
6.25
), gives

\displaystyle\|\mathtt{E}_{n,4}^{0}\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(\mathscr{B}-\mathscr{B}_{n})\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.95)

Applying Taylor Formula, we may write

\displaystyle(\mathscr{B}-\mathscr{B}_{n})\rho(\theta)

\displaystyle=(1+\partial_{\theta}\beta(\theta))\rho(\theta+\beta(\theta))-(1+\partial_{\theta}\beta_{n}(\theta))\rho(\theta+h_{n}(\theta))

\displaystyle=(1+\partial_{\theta}\beta(\theta))\left[\rho(\theta+\beta(\theta))-\rho(\theta+\beta_{n}(\theta))\right]+\partial_{\theta}\left(\beta-\beta_{n}\right)(\theta)\rho(\theta+\beta_{n}(\theta))

\displaystyle:=(1+\partial_{\theta}\beta(\theta))(\beta-\beta_{n})(\theta)\mathscr{I}_{n}(\theta)+\partial_{\theta}(\beta-\beta_{n})(\theta)\mathcal{B}_{n}\rho(\theta),

where

\mathscr{I}_{n}\rho(\theta):=\int_{0}^{1}(\partial_{\theta}\rho)\left(\theta+\beta_{n}(\theta)+t(\beta(\theta)-\beta_{n}(\theta))\right)dt.

Hence, we get by the law products,
6.18
and (
6.2.1
)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\theta}\left(\beta-\beta_{n}\right)\mathcal{B}_{n}\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\beta-\beta_{n}\|_{q,s_{0}+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+1}\left(1+\|\beta_{n}\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}N_{n+1}^{-\mu_{2}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Using the law products together with (
6.2.1
), (
6.2.1
) and (
6.85
) we find

\displaystyle\Big\|(1+\partial_{\theta}\beta)\left(\beta-\beta_{n}\right)\mathscr{I}_{n}\rho\Big\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(1+\|\beta\|_{q,s_{0}+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)\|\beta-\beta_{n}\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\left(1+\|\beta_{n}\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\beta-\beta_{n}\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}N_{n+1}^{-\mu_{2}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Gathering the foregoing estimates leads to

\displaystyle\|(\mathscr{B}-\mathscr{B}_{n})\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}N_{n+1}^{-\mu_{2}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.96)

Plugging (
6.96
) into (
6.95
) gives

\displaystyle\|\mathtt{E}_{n,4}^{0}\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}N_{n+1}^{-\mu_{2}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.97)

Proceeding in a similar way as before using in particular the identity (
6.16
) and (
6.20
) we find

\displaystyle\|\mathtt{E}_{n,3}^{0}\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}N_{n+1}^{-\mu_{2}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.98)

Putting together (
6.2.1
), (
6.2.1
), (
6.97
), (
6.98
) allows to get

\|\mathtt{E}_{n}^{0}\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}N_{n+1}^{-\mu_{2}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(iv)

\blacktriangleright

Estimate of
\Delta_{12}\beta
.

First notice that, since
\beta_{-1}=0
, then

\Delta_{12}\beta=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\Delta_{12}(\beta_{m}-\beta_{m-1}).

(6.99)

The triangle inequality allows us to write
