\|\Delta_{12}\beta\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\|\Delta_{12}(\beta_{m}-\beta_{m-1})\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.100)

According to Taylor Formula and (
6.65
), we infer

\displaystyle\Delta_{12}\beta_{m}(\theta)

\displaystyle=\Delta_{12}\beta_{m-1}(\theta)+(\mathcal{B}_{m-1})_{r_{1}}(\Delta_{12}g_{m})(\theta)

\displaystyle\quad+\Delta_{12}\beta_{m-1}(\theta)\int_{0}^{1}(\partial_{\theta}(g_{m})_{r_{2}})\big(\theta+(\beta_{m-1})_{r_{2}}(\theta)+t\Delta_{12}\beta_{m-1}(\theta)\big)dt.

Thus,

\displaystyle\Delta_{12}(\beta_{m}-\beta_{m-1})(\theta)

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{B}_{m-1})_{r_{1}}(\Delta_{12}g_{m})(\theta)

\displaystyle\quad+\Delta_{12}\beta_{m-1}(\theta)\int_{0}^{1}(\partial_{\theta}(g_{m})_{r_{2}})\big(\theta+(\beta_{m-1})_{r_{2}}(\theta)+t\Delta_{12}\beta_{m-1}(\theta)\big)dt.

Consequently, using the law product in Lemma
4.1
, Lemma
6.2
and Sobolev embeddings we obtain

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}(\beta_{m}-\beta_{m-1})\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\Delta_{12}g_{m}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\left(1+C\|(\beta_{m-1})_{r_{1}}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)+\|\Delta_{12}g_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|(\beta_{m-1})_{r_{1}}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle+C\|\Delta_{12}\beta_{m-1}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|(g_{m})_{r_{2}}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\left(1+\|(\beta_{m-1})_{r_{2}}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\Delta_{12}\beta_{m-1}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)

\displaystyle+C\|\Delta_{12}\beta_{m-1}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|(g_{m})_{r_{2}}\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\left(\|(\beta_{m-1})_{r_{2}}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\Delta_{12}\beta_{m-1}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)

\displaystyle+C\|\Delta_{12}\beta_{m-1}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|(g_{m})_{r_{2}}\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\left(1+\|(\beta_{m-1})_{r_{2}}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\Delta_{12}\beta_{m-1}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)

and for all
s\in[s_{0},\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}]

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}\beta_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\Delta_{12}g_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\left(1+C\|(\beta_{m-1})_{r_{1}}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)+\|\Delta_{12}g_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|(\beta_{m-1})_{r_{1}}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\quad+C\|\Delta_{12}\beta_{m-1}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|(g_{m})_{r_{2}}\|_{q,s+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\left(1+\|(\beta_{m-1})_{r_{2}}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\Delta_{12}\beta_{m-1}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+C\|\Delta_{12}\beta_{m-1}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|(g_{m})_{r_{2}}\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\left(\|(\beta_{m-1})_{r_{2}}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\Delta_{12}\beta_{m-1}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\|\Delta_{12}\beta_{m-1}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\left(1+C\|(g_{m})_{r_{2}}\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\left(1+\|(\beta_{m-1})_{r_{2}}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\Delta_{12}\beta_{m-1}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)\right).

Notice that (
6.24
) implies in particular
\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+3\leqslant s_{h}+\sigma_{1}.
Therefore, using (
6.2.1
) and (
6.25
), we get

\displaystyle\sup_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\max_{k\in\{1,2\}}\|(g_{m})_{r_{k}}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\max_{k\in\{1,2\}}\|\mathfrak{I}_{k}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+3}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant C.

(6.101)

Notice that the previous estimate is sufficient to easily get rid of most of terms in the estimates of
\Delta_{12}\beta_{m}
and
\Delta_{12}(\beta_{m}-\beta_{m-1})
, but not enough to make the series (
6.99
) convergent. For this purpose, we shall refine the estimates. By (
6.2.1
), (
6.24
) and (
6.25
), we have

\displaystyle\max_{k\in\{1,2\}}\|(g_{m})_{r_{k}}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+1}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{k}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+3}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)N_{0}^{\overline{\theta}\left(\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+1\right)\mu_{2}}N_{m}^{-\overline{\theta}\left(\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+1\right)\mu_{2}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{\overline{\theta}\left(\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+1\right)\mu_{2}}N_{m}^{-\overline{\theta}\left(\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+1\right)\mu_{2}}.

(6.102)

Combining (
6.82
) and (
6.25
)

\displaystyle\sup_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\max_{k\in\{1,2\}}\|(\beta_{m})_{r_{k}}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\max_{k\in\{1,2\}}\|\mathfrak{I}_{k}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+3}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant C.

(6.103)

Hence, using (
6.2.1
), (
6.2.1
) and Sobolev embeddings, the previous two estimates can be reduced to

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}(\beta_{m}-\beta_{m-1})\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left(\|\Delta_{12}g_{m}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\Delta_{12}\beta_{m-1}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|(g_{m})_{r_{2}}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right),

(6.104)

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}\beta_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\|\Delta_{12}g_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\Delta_{12}\beta_{m-1}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\left(1+C\|(g_{m})_{r_{2}}\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)

(6.105)

and

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}\beta_{m}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left(\|\Delta_{12}g_{m}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\Delta_{12}\beta_{m-1}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|(g_{m})_{r_{2}}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+1}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\|\Delta_{12}\beta_{m-1}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\left(1+C\|(g_{m})_{r_{2}}\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right).

(6.106)

From (
6.105
), using (
6.79
) and the fact that
\beta_{0}=g_{0}
, we deduce that

\sup_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\|\Delta_{12}\beta_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant\left(\|\Delta_{12}g_{0}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+C\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\|\Delta_{12}g_{k}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)\prod_{k=0}^{\infty}\left(1+\|(g_{k})_{r_{2}}\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right).

Adding (
6.2.1
), we obtain

\sup_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\|\Delta_{12}\beta_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant C\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\|\Delta_{12}g_{k}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Similarly, (
6.2.1
), (
6.79
), (
6.2.1
) and the previous estimate allow to get

\sup_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\|\Delta_{12}\beta_{m}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant C\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\|\Delta_{12}g_{k}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Putting together the previous bounds, (
6.104
) and (
6.2.1
) gives

\|\Delta_{12}(\beta_{m}-\beta_{m-1})\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\|\Delta_{12}g_{m}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{\overline{\theta}(\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+1)\mu_{2}}N_{m}^{-\overline{\theta}(\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+1)\mu_{2}}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\|\Delta_{12}g_{k}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.107)

Thus, the main delicate point is to estimate
\Delta_{12}g_{m}.
First remark that according to (
6.43
) and (
6.61
), we can make the splitting

\displaystyle g_{m}(\mu,\varphi,\theta)

\displaystyle={\rm i}\sum_{(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d+1}\setminus\{0\}\atop\langle l,j\rangle\leqslant N_{m}}a_{l,j}\widehat{\chi}\big(a_{l,j}(A_{l,j})_{r_{2}}(\mu)\big)(\Delta_{12}f_{m})_{l,j}(\mu)\mathbf{e}_{l,j}

\displaystyle\textnormal{\hskip 8.5359pt}+{\rm i}\sum_{(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d+1}\setminus\{0\}\atop\langle l,j\rangle\leqslant N_{m}}a_{l,j}\Delta_{12}\widehat{\chi}\big(a_{l,j}A_{l,j}(\mu)\big)((f_{m})_{r_{1}})_{l,j}(\mu)\mathbf{e}_{l,j}

\displaystyle:=\mathbf{I}_{1}+\mathbf{I}_{2}.

Similarly to (
6.48
), one obtains

\|\mathbf{I}_{1}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\gamma^{-1}\|\Pi_{N_{m}}\Delta_{12}f_{m}\|_{q,s+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.108)

We shall now estimate the second term. Applying Taylor Formula, we get

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}_{2}

\displaystyle={\rm i}\sum_{(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d+1}\setminus\{0\}\atop\langle l,j\rangle\leqslant N_{m}}a_{l,j}^{2}(\Delta_{12}A_{l,j})\int_{0}^{1}\widehat{\chi}^{\prime}\Big(a_{l,j}\Big[\tau(A_{l,j})_{r_{1}}(\mu)+(1-\tau)(A_{l,j})_{r_{2}}(\mu)\Big]\Big)d\tau((f_{m})_{r_{1}})_{l,j}\mathbf{e}_{l,j}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d+1}\setminus\{0\}\atop\langle l,j\rangle\leqslant N_{m}}\widetilde{h}_{l,j}(\mu)((f_{m})_{r_{1}})_{l,j}(\mu)\mathbf{e}_{l,j}.

(6.109)

Remark that direct computations yield

\forall q^{\prime}\in\llbracket 0,q\rrbracket,\quad\|\Delta_{12}A_{l,j}\|_{q^{\prime}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\langle l,j\rangle\|\Delta_{12}V_{m}\|_{q^{\prime}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.110)

Since that
\widehat{\chi}^{\prime}\in C^{\infty}
with
\widehat{\chi}^{\prime}(0)=0,
then applying Lemma
4.1
-(iv)-(vi) together with (
6.45
) and (
6.110
), we get

\displaystyle\forall q^{\prime}\in\llbracket 0,q\rrbracket,\quad\|\widetilde{h_{l,j}}\|_{q^{\prime}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim a_{l,j}^{3}\|\Delta_{12}A_{l,j}\|_{q^{\prime}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\left(\|(A_{l,j})_{r_{1}}\|_{q^{\prime}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|(A_{l,j})_{r_{2}}\|_{q^{\prime}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)

\displaystyle\textnormal{\hskip 8.5359pt}\times\left(1+a_{l,j}^{q^{\prime}-1}\left(\|(A_{l,j})_{r_{1}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{O})}+\|(A_{l,j})_{r_{2}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{O})}\right)^{q^{\prime}-1}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma^{-\upsilon(q^{\prime}+2)}\langle l,j\rangle^{\tau_{1}q^{\prime}+2\tau_{1}+q^{\prime}+1}\|\Delta_{12}V_{m}\|_{q^{\prime}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

By assumption in Proposition
6.2
, we have

\upsilon\leqslant\tfrac{1}{q+2}

(6.111)

and using Leibniz rule, we deduce that

\|\mathbf{I}_{2}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}V_{m}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\Pi_{N_{m}}(f_{m})_{r_{1}}\|_{q,s+\tau_{1}q+2\tau_{1}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.112)

Putting together (
6.108
) and (
6.112
), we obtain for all
s\geqslant s_{0}

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}g_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma^{-1}\|\Pi_{N_{m}}\Delta_{12}f_{m}\|_{q,s+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}V_{m}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\Pi_{N_{m}}(f_{m})_{r_{1}}\|_{q,s+\tau_{1}q+2\tau_{1}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

(6.113)

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma^{-1}N_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}}\|\Delta_{12}f_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\gamma^{-2}N_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+2\tau_{1}+1}\|\Delta_{12}V_{m}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|(f_{m})_{r_{1}}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Therefore, estimating
\Delta_{12}g_{m}
can be done through the estimate of
\Delta_{12}f_{m}.
To do so, we shall argue by induction. For that purpose, we shall consider a parameter
\widetilde{\mathtt{p}}
(which can depend on the parameter
\mathtt{p}
, see for instance (
6.127
)) satisfying the following constraint

\overline{s}_{h}+\widetilde{\mathtt{p}}+3\leqslant s_{h}+\sigma_{1}.

(6.114)

We denote

u_{m}:=\Pi_{N_{m}}^{\perp}f_{m}+f_{m}\partial_{\theta}g_{m}.

Then, we can write

\Delta_{12}f_{m+1}=(\mathcal{G}_{m}^{-1})_{r_{1}}\Delta_{12}u_{m}+\left(\Delta_{12}\mathcal{G}_{m}^{-1}\right)(u_{m})_{r_{2}},

with

\Delta_{12}u_{m}=\Pi_{N_{m}}^{\perp}\Delta_{12}f_{m}+\Delta_{12}f_{m}\partial_{\theta}(g_{m})_{r_{1}}+(f_{m})_{r_{2}}\partial_{\theta}\Delta_{12}g_{m}.

By the triangle inequality, we have for all
s\geqslant s_{0}

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}f_{m+1}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|(\mathcal{G}_{m}^{-1})_{r_{1}}\Delta_{12}u_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|(\Delta_{12}\mathcal{G}_{m}^{-1})(u_{m})_{r_{2}}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.115)

Therefore, combining (
6.18
), (
6.20
), (
6.48
) and Lemma
4.1
-(ii), we get for all
s\geqslant s_{0}

\displaystyle\|(\mathcal{G}_{m}^{-1})_{r_{1}}\Delta_{12}u_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\Delta_{12}u_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\left(1+C\|(\widehat{g_{m}})_{r_{1}}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)+C\|(\widehat{g_{m}})_{r_{1}}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\Delta_{12}u_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\Delta_{12}u_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\left(1+C\|(g_{m})_{r_{1}}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)+C\|(g_{m})_{r_{1}}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\Delta_{12}u_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\Delta_{12}u_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\left(1+C\gamma^{-1}N_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}}\max_{k\in\{1,2\}}\|(f_{m})_{r_{k}}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+C\gamma^{-1}N_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}}\max_{k\in\{1,2\}}\|(f_{m})_{r_{k}}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\Delta_{12}u_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Using (
6.2.1
),(
6.114
) and (
6.25
), one gets

\displaystyle\gamma^{-1}\sup_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\max_{k\in\{1,2\}}\|(f_{m})_{r_{k}}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\widetilde{\mathtt{p}}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\max_{k\in\{1,2\}}\|\mathfrak{I}_{k}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\widetilde{\mathtt{p}}+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant C.

(6.116)

Therefore, from (
6.52
) and (
6.2.1
), we get for all
s\in[s_{0},\overline{s}_{h}]

\|(\mathcal{G}_{m}^{-1})_{r_{1}}\Delta_{12}u_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant\|\Delta_{12}u_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\left(1+CN_{0}^{\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}-\overline{\mu}_{2}}\right)+CN_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}}\|\Delta_{12}u_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

At this level we need to give a suitable estimate for
\Delta_{12}u_{m}.
For this aim, we apply the law products in Lemma
4.1
, ensuring that for all
s\geqslant s_{0}

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}u_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\Pi_{N_{m}}^{\perp}\Delta_{12}f_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+C\|\Delta_{12}f_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\partial_{\theta}(g_{m})_{r_{1}}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+C\|\Delta_{12}f_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\partial_{\theta}(g_{m})_{r_{1}}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\quad+C\|(f_{m})_{r_{2}}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\Delta_{12}g_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+C\|(f_{m})_{r_{2}}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\Delta_{12}g_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Hence we deduce by (
6.48
) and Lemma
4.1
-(ii),

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}u_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\Pi_{N_{m}}^{\perp}\Delta_{12}f_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+C\gamma^{-1}N_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1}\|\Delta_{12}f_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\max_{k\in\{1,2\}}\|(f_{m})_{r_{k}}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\quad+C\gamma^{-1}N_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1}\|\Delta_{12}f_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\max_{k\in\{1,2\}}\|(f_{m})_{r_{k}}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\quad+C\max_{k\in\{1,2\}}\|(f_{m})_{r_{k}}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\Delta_{12}g_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+C\max_{k\in\{1,2\}}\|(f_{m})_{r_{k}}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\Delta_{12}g_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Added to (
6.113
), we finally obtain for all
s\geqslant s_{0}

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}u_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\Pi_{N_{m}}^{\perp}\Delta_{12}f_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+C\gamma^{-1}N_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1}\|\Delta_{12}f_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\max_{k\in\{1,2\}}\|(f_{m})_{r_{k}}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\quad+C\gamma^{-1}N_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1}\|\Delta_{12}f_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\max_{k\in\{1,2\}}\|(f_{m})_{r_{k}}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\quad+C\gamma^{-2}N_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+2\tau_{1}+1}\max_{k\in\{1,2\}}\|(f_{m})_{r_{k}}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\max_{k\in\{1,2\}}\|(f_{m})_{r_{k}}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\Delta_{12}V_{m}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Consequently, we find from (
6.52
), Lemma
4.1
-(ii) and (
6.2.1
),

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}u_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant N_{m}^{s_{0}-\overline{s}_{h}-\widetilde{\mathtt{p}}}\|\Delta_{12}f_{m}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\widetilde{\mathtt{p}}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+CN_{0}^{\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1-\overline{\mu}_{2}}\delta_{0}^{1,2}(\overline{s}_{h})\|\Delta_{12}f_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\quad+CN_{0}^{2\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+2\tau_{1}+1-2\overline{\mu}_{2}}\delta_{0}^{1,2}(\overline{s}_{h})\|\Delta_{12}V_{m}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

and

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}u_{m}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\widetilde{\mathtt{p}}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\Delta_{12}f_{m}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\widetilde{\mathtt{p}}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\left(1+CN_{0}^{\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1-\overline{\mu}_{2}}\delta_{0}^{1,2}(\overline{s}_{h})\right)+CN_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1}\delta_{0}^{1,2}(\overline{s}_{h})\|\Delta_{12}f_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\quad+CN_{0}^{\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+2\tau_{1}+1-\overline{\mu}_{2}}\delta_{0}^{1,2}(\overline{s}_{h})\|\Delta_{12}V_{m}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}},

where we use the notation

\delta_{0}^{1,2}(s):=\gamma^{-1}\max_{k\in\{1,2\}}\|(f_{0})_{r_{k}}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

It follows from the preceding estimates that,

\displaystyle\|(\mathcal{G}_{m}^{-1})_{r_{1}}\Delta_{12}u_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant CN_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}}\|\Delta_{12}u_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant CN_{m}^{s_{0}+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}-\overline{s}_{h}-\widetilde{\mathtt{p}}}\|\Delta_{12}f_{m}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\widetilde{\mathtt{p}}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+CN_{0}^{\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{m}^{2(\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1})+1-\overline{\mu}_{2}}\delta_{0}^{1,2}(\overline{s}_{h})\|\Delta_{12}f_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\quad+CN_{0}^{2\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{m}^{2\tau_{1}q+3\tau_{1}+1-2\overline{\mu}_{2}}\delta_{0}^{1,2}(\overline{s}_{h})\|\Delta_{12}V_{m}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.117)

In a similar way, direct computations yield

\displaystyle\|(\mathcal{G}_{m}^{-1})_{r_{1}}\Delta_{12}u_{m}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\widetilde{\mathtt{p}}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\Delta_{12}f_{m}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\widetilde{\mathtt{p}}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\left(1+N_{m}^{s_{0}+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}-\overline{s}_{h}-\widetilde{\mathtt{p}}}+CN_{0}^{\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1-\overline{\mu}_{2}}\delta_{0}^{1,2}(\overline{s}_{h})\right)

\displaystyle\quad+C\left(N_{0}^{\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{m}^{2(\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1})+1-\overline{\mu}_{2}}+N_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1}\right)\delta_{0}^{1,2}(\overline{s}_{h})\|\Delta_{12}f_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\quad+CN_{0}^{\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{m}^{2\tau_{1}q+3\tau_{1}+1-\overline{\mu}_{2}}\delta_{0}^{1,2}(\overline{s}_{h})\|\Delta_{12}V_{m}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.118)

By a new use of Taylor Formula, we can write

(\Delta_{12}\mathcal{G}_{m}^{-1})(u_{m})_{r_{2}}(\theta)=\Delta_{12}\widehat{g_{m}}(\theta)\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{\theta}(u_{m})_{r_{2}}\Big(\theta+(\widehat{g_{m}})_{r_{2}}(\theta)+t\Delta_{12}\widehat{g_{m}}(\theta)\Big)dt.

Applying Lemma
4.1
and (
6.48
), we deduce for all
s\geqslant s_{0}

\displaystyle\|u_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\Pi_{N_{m}}^{\perp}f_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+C\|f_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\partial_{\theta}g_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+C\|f_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\partial_{\theta}g_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|f_{m}\|_{q,s}\left(1+CN_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1}\|f_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\|f_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.119)

Using once again the law products in Lemma
4.1
combined with (
6.18
) yield for all
s\geqslant s_{0}

\displaystyle\|(\Delta_{12}\mathcal{G}_{m}^{-1})(u_{m})_{r_{2}}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\|\Delta_{12}\widehat{g_{m}}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|(u_{m})_{r_{2}}\|_{q,s_{0}+1}\left(1+\|(\widehat{g_{m}})_{r_{2}}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\Delta_{12}\widehat{g_{m}}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+C\|\Delta_{12}\widehat{g_{m}}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|(u_{m})_{r_{2}}\|_{q,s+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\left(1+\|(\widehat{g_{m}})_{r_{2}}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\Delta_{12}\widehat{g_{m}}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+C\|\Delta_{12}\widehat{g_{m}}\|_{q,s_{0}}\|(u_{m})_{r_{2}}\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\left(\|(\widehat{g_{m}})_{r_{2}}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\Delta_{12}\widehat{g_{m}}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right).

In view of (
6.21
), (
6.2.1
) and Sobolev embeddings, one gets for all
s\in[s_{0},\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}]

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}\widehat{g_{m}}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left(\|\Delta_{12}g_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\Delta_{12}g_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\max_{k\in\{1,2\}}\|(g_{m})_{r_{k}}\|_{q,s+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\|\Delta_{12}g_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Putting together the previous estimates, (
6.2.1
) and (
6.20
) gives for all
s\in[s_{0},\overline{s}_{h}]

\displaystyle\|(\Delta_{12}\mathcal{G}_{m}^{-1})(u_{m})_{r_{2}}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\|\Delta_{12}g_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|(u_{m})_{r_{2}}\|_{q,s_{0}+1}+C\|\Delta_{12}g_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|(u_{m})_{r_{2}}\|_{q,s+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Thus, by virtue of (
6.113
), (
6.2.1
), we get for all
s\in[s_{0},\overline{s}_{h}]

\displaystyle\|(\Delta_{12}\mathcal{G}_{m}^{-1})(u_{m})_{r_{2}}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\gamma^{-1}N_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}}\|\Delta_{12}f_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\max_{k\in\{1,2\}}\|(f_{m})_{r_{k}}\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\quad+C\gamma^{-1}N_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}}\|\Delta_{12}f_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\max_{k\in\{1,2\}}\|(f_{m})_{r_{k}}\|_{q,s+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\quad+C\gamma^{-2}N_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+2\tau_{1}+1}\|\Delta_{12}V_{m}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\max_{k\in\{1,2\}}\|(f_{m})_{r_{k}}\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\max_{k\in\{1,2\}}\|(f_{m})_{r_{k}}\|_{q,s+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Hence, (
6.52
), (
6.2.1
) and (
6.2.1
) allow to get (since
s_{l}\geqslant s_{0}+1
)

\displaystyle\|(\Delta_{12}\mathcal{G}_{m}^{-1})(u_{m})_{r_{2}}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant CN_{0}^{\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}-\overline{\mu}_{2}}\delta_{0}^{1,2}(\overline{s}_{h})\|\Delta_{12}f_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\quad+CN_{0}^{2\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+2\tau_{1}+1-2\overline{\mu}_{2}}\delta_{0}^{1,2}(\overline{s}_{h})\|\Delta_{12}V_{m}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

(6.120)

and

\displaystyle\|(\Delta_{12}\mathcal{G}_{m}^{-1})(u_{m})_{r_{2}}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\widetilde{\mathtt{p}}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant CN_{0}^{\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}-\overline{\mu}_{2}}\delta_{0}^{1,2}(\overline{s}_{h})\|\Delta_{12}f_{m}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\widetilde{\mathtt{p}}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\quad+CN_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}}\delta_{0}^{1,2}(\overline{s}_{h}+\widetilde{\mathtt{p}}+1)\|\Delta_{12}f_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\quad+CN_{0}^{\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+2\tau_{1}+1-\overline{\mu}_{2}}\delta_{0}^{1,2}(\overline{s}_{h}+\widetilde{\mathtt{p}}+1)\|\Delta_{12}V_{m}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.121)

Gathering (
6.115
), (
6.2.1
) and (
6.2.1
) implies (since
N_{m}^{-\widetilde{\mathtt{p}}}\leqslant 1
)

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}f_{m+1}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant N_{m}^{s_{0}+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}-\overline{s}_{h}}\|\Delta_{12}f_{m}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\widetilde{\mathtt{p}}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\quad+CN_{0}^{\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{m}^{2(\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1})+1-\overline{\mu}_{2}}\delta_{0}^{1,2}(\overline{s}_{h})\|\Delta_{12}f_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\quad+CN_{0}^{2\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{m}^{2\tau_{1}q+3\tau_{1}+1-2\overline{\mu}_{2}}\delta_{0}^{1,2}(\overline{s}_{h})\|\Delta_{12}V_{m}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.122)

In a similar war, we get in view of (
6.115
), (
6.2.1
) and (
6.2.1
)

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}f_{m+1}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\widetilde{\mathtt{p}}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\Delta_{12}f_{m}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\widetilde{\mathtt{p}}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\left(1+N_{m}^{s_{0}+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}-\overline{s}_{h}-\widetilde{\mathtt{p}}}+CN_{0}^{\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1-\overline{\mu}_{2}}\delta_{0}^{1,2}(\overline{s}_{h})\right)

\displaystyle\quad+C\left(N_{0}^{\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{m}^{2(\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1})+1-\overline{\mu}_{2}}+N_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1}\right)\delta_{0}^{1,2}(\overline{s}_{h}+\widetilde{\mathtt{p}}+1)\|\Delta_{12}f_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\quad+CN_{0}^{\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{m}^{2\tau_{1}q+3\tau_{1}+1-\overline{\mu}_{2}}\delta_{0}^{1,2}(\overline{s}_{h}+\widetilde{\mathtt{p}}+1)\|\Delta_{12}V_{m}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.123)

In the sequel, we shall use the following notations

\overline{\delta}_{m}(s)=\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}f_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\quad\textnormal{and}\quad\varkappa_{m}=\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}V_{m}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Notice that

\Delta_{12}V_{m+1}=\Delta_{12}V_{m}+\langle\Delta_{12}f_{m}\rangle_{\varphi,\theta}\quad\textnormal{and}\Delta_{12}V_{0}=0.

Then, by using Sobolev embeddings, we obtain

\varkappa_{m}\leqslant\sum_{k=0}^{m-1}\overline{\delta}_{k}(s_{0}).

(6.124)

We shall now prove by induction that, for all
\widetilde{\mathtt{p}}
satisfying the condition (
6.114
), we have

\forall k\leqslant m,\quad\overline{\delta}_{k}(s_{0})\leqslant N_{0}^{\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{k}^{-\overline{\mu}_{2}}\nu(\overline{s}_{h}+\widetilde{\mathtt{p}})\quad\textnormal{and}\quad\overline{\delta}_{k}(\overline{s}_{h}+\widetilde{\mathtt{p}})\leqslant\left(2-\tfrac{1}{k+1}\right)\nu(\overline{s}_{h}+\widetilde{\mathtt{p}}),

(6.125)

with

\nu(s):=\overline{\delta}_{0}(s)+\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{s_{0}+2}.

First remark that the property (
6.125
) is trivially satisfied for
m=0
according to Sobolev embeddings. We now assume that (
6.125
) is true at the order
m
and let us check it at the next order. By the induction assumption (
6.125
) and (
6.124
), one obtains the following estimate

\sup_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\varkappa_{m}\leqslant C\nu(\overline{s}_{h}+\widetilde{\mathtt{p}}).

(6.126)

Using (
6.2.1
), (
6.126
) and hypothesis of induction (
6.125
), we find

\displaystyle\overline{\delta}_{m+1}(s_{0})

\displaystyle\leqslant N_{m}^{s_{0}+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}-\overline{s}_{h}}\overline{\delta}_{m}(\overline{s}_{h}+\widetilde{\mathtt{p}})+CN_{0}^{\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{m}^{2(\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1})+1-\overline{\mu}_{2}}\delta_{0}^{1,2}(\overline{s}_{h})\overline{\delta}_{m}(s_{0})

\displaystyle\quad+CN_{0}^{2\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{m}^{2\tau_{1}q+3\tau_{1}+1-2\overline{\mu}_{2}}\delta_{0}^{1,2}(\overline{s}_{h})\varkappa_{m}

\displaystyle\leqslant\left[2N_{m}^{s_{0}+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}-\overline{s}_{h}}+CN_{0}^{2\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{m}^{2\tau_{1}q+3\tau_{1}+1-2\overline{\mu}_{2}}\delta_{0}^{1,2}(\overline{s}_{h})\right]\nu(\overline{s}_{h}+\widetilde{\mathtt{p}}).

Then, in view of (
6.24
), we infer

\displaystyle 2N_{m}^{s_{0}+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}-\overline{s}_{h}}

\displaystyle=2N_{m}^{-\frac{3}{2}\overline{\mu}_{2}-3}=2N_{m}^{-3}N_{m+1}^{-\overline{\mu}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leqslant 2N_{0}^{-3}N_{m+1}^{-\overline{\mu}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{2}N_{0}^{\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{m+1}^{-\overline{\mu}_{2}}.

To prove the last inequality, we remark that since
N_{0}\geqslant 2
and
\overline{\mu}_{2}\geqslant 0
(according to (
6.3
)), then

4\leqslant N_{0}^{\overline{\mu}_{2}+3}.

Similarly, from the expression of
\overline{\mu}_{2}
in (
6.24
)
and using (
5.82
) one obtains

\displaystyle CN_{0}^{2\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{m}^{2\tau_{1}q+3\tau_{1}+1-2\overline{\mu}_{2}}\delta_{0}^{1,2}(\overline{s}_{h})

\displaystyle\leqslant C\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{m}^{2\tau_{1}q+3\tau_{1}+1-\frac{1}{2}\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{0}^{\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{m+1}^{-\overline{\mu}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{2\tau_{1}q+3\tau_{1}+1+\frac{1}{2}\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{0}^{\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{m+1}^{-\overline{\mu}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{0}^{\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{m+1}^{-\overline{\mu}_{2}}.

Hence, choosing
\varepsilon_{0}
small enough and using (
6.25
) we deduce that

CN_{0}^{2\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{m}^{2\tau_{1}q+3\tau_{1}+1-2\overline{\mu}_{2}}\delta_{0}^{1,2}(\overline{s}_{h})\leqslant\frac{1}{2}N_{0}^{\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{m+1}^{-\overline{\mu}_{2}}.

Gathering the preceding estimates gives

\overline{\delta}_{m+1}(s_{0})\leqslant N_{0}^{\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{m+1}^{-\overline{\mu}_{2}}\nu(\overline{s}_{h}+\widetilde{\mathtt{p}}).

This ends the proof of the first statement in (
6.125
). As to the second one, we shall first write in view of (
6.2.1
),

\displaystyle\overline{\delta}_{m+1}(\overline{s}_{h}+\widetilde{\mathtt{p}})

\displaystyle\leqslant\overline{\delta}_{m}(\overline{s}_{h}+\widetilde{\mathtt{p}})\left(1+N_{m}^{s_{0}+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}-\overline{s}_{h}}+CN_{0}^{\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1-\overline{\mu}_{2}}\delta_{0}^{1,2}(\overline{s}_{h})\right)

\displaystyle\quad+C\left(N_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1}+N_{0}^{\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{m}^{2(\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1})+1-\overline{\mu}_{2}}\right)\delta_{0}^{1,2}(\overline{s}_{h}+\widetilde{\mathtt{p}}+1)\overline{\delta}_{m}(s_{0})

\displaystyle\quad+CN_{0}^{\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{m}^{2\tau_{1}q+3\tau_{1}+1-\overline{\mu}_{2}}\delta_{0}^{1,2}(\overline{s}_{h}+\widetilde{\mathtt{p}}+1)\varkappa_{m}.

Notice that since
\overline{s}_{h}+\widetilde{\mathtt{p}}+2\leqslant s_{h}+\sigma_{1}
, then by (
6.2.1
) and (
6.25
), one has

\displaystyle\delta_{0}^{1,2}(\overline{s}_{h}+\widetilde{\mathtt{p}}+1)

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\max_{k\in\{1,2\}}\|\mathfrak{I}_{k}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\widetilde{\mathtt{p}}+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}.

It follows from (
6.125
) and (
6.126
),

\displaystyle\overline{\delta}_{m+1}(\overline{s}_{h}+\widetilde{\mathtt{p}})

\displaystyle\leqslant\left(2-\tfrac{1}{m+1}\right)\left(1+N_{m}^{s_{0}+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}-\overline{s}_{h}}+CN_{0}^{\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1-\overline{\mu}_{2}}\right)\nu(\overline{s}_{h}+\widetilde{\mathtt{p}})

\displaystyle\quad+C\left(N_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1}+N_{0}^{\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{m}^{2\tau_{1}q+3\tau_{1}+1-\overline{\mu}_{2}}\right)N_{0}^{\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{m}^{-\overline{\mu}_{2}}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\nu(\overline{s}_{h}+\widetilde{\mathtt{p}}).

Proceeding as for (
6.72
), taking
\varepsilon_{0}
small enough and thanks to (
6.24
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left(2-\tfrac{1}{m+1}\right)\left(1+N_{m}^{s_{0}+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}-\overline{s}_{h}}+CN_{0}^{\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1-\overline{\mu}_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+C\left(N_{m}^{\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1}+N_{0}^{\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{m}^{2\tau_{1}q+3\tau_{1}+1-\overline{\mu}_{2}}\right)N_{0}^{\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{m}^{-\overline{\mu}_{2}}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}

\displaystyle\leqslant 2-\tfrac{1}{m+2},

so that

\overline{\delta}_{m+1}(\overline{s}_{h}+\widetilde{\mathtt{p}})\leqslant\left(2-\tfrac{1}{m+2}\right)\nu(\overline{s}_{h}+\widetilde{\mathtt{p}}).

This completes the proof of the second statement in (
6.125
).

➢
Conclusion.

From (
6.113
), we get for
s=s_{0}.

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}g_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\overline{\delta}_{m}(s_{0}+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1})+\varkappa_{m}\delta_{m}(s_{0}+\tau_{1}q+2\tau_{1}+1).

By interpolation inequality in Lemma
4.1
, (
6.52
) applied with
\mu_{2}=\overline{\mu}_{2}
, (
6.125
) applied with
\widetilde{\mathtt{p}}=0
and Sobolev embeddings, we have for some
\overline{\theta}\in(0,1)

\displaystyle\overline{\delta}_{m}(s_{0}+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1})

\displaystyle\leqslant\overline{\delta}_{m}(s_{0}+\tau_{1}q+2\tau_{1}+1)

\displaystyle\lesssim\overline{\delta}_{m}(s_{0})^{\overline{\theta}}\overline{\delta}_{m}(\overline{s}_{h})^{1-\overline{\theta}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{0}^{\overline{\theta}\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{m}^{-\overline{\theta}\overline{\mu}_{2}}\nu(\overline{s}_{h})

and

\displaystyle\delta_{m}(s_{0}+\tau_{1}q+2\tau_{1}+1)

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{m}(s_{0})^{\overline{\theta}}\delta_{m}(\overline{s}_{h})^{1-\overline{\theta}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{0}^{\overline{\theta}\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{m}^{-\overline{\theta}\overline{\mu}_{2}}\delta_{0}(\overline{s}_{h})

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{0}^{\overline{\theta}\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{m}^{-\overline{\theta}\overline{\mu}_{2}}.

Therefore

\|\Delta_{12}g_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim N_{0}^{\overline{\theta}\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{m}^{-\overline{\theta}\overline{\mu}_{2}}\nu(\overline{s}_{h}).

Now from (
6.113
), we have

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}g_{m}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\overline{\delta}_{m}(\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1)+\varkappa_{m}\delta_{m}(\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\tau_{1}q+2\tau_{1}+2).

Applying (
6.125
) with

\widetilde{\mathtt{p}}=\mathtt{p}+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1,

(6.127)

which is possible since from (
6.3
), (
6.24
) and (
6.23
), one has
\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+4\leqslant s_{h}+\sigma_{1},

we find

\displaystyle\overline{\delta}_{m}(\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1)

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\nu(\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1)

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\overline{\delta}_{0}(\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1)+2\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,s_{0}+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Implementing a similar proof to (
5.48
) based on the kernel decomposition (
5.38
), the composition laws
and (
6.8
),
we find

\displaystyle\forall s\geqslant s_{0},\quad\overline{\delta}_{0}(s)

\displaystyle=\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}V_{\varepsilon r}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\Big(\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,s+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\max_{\ell=1,2}\|r_{\ell}\|_{q,s+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\Big).

On the other hand, since

\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\tau_{1}q+2\tau_{1}+3\leqslant s_{h}+\sigma_{1},

one may obtain through combining (
6.2.1
) and (
6.25
)

\displaystyle\delta_{m}(\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\tau_{1}q+2\tau_{1}+2)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\tau_{1}q+2\tau_{1}+3}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}.

Thus, by interpolation inequality in Lemma
4.1
, we finally obtain for some
\overline{\theta}\in(0,1)

\|\Delta_{12}g_{m}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim N_{0}^{\overline{\theta}\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{m}^{-\overline{\theta}\overline{\mu}_{2}}\nu(\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1).

(6.128)

Choosing
N_{0}
sufficiently large, then the composition law in Lemma
4.1
allows to get

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\|\Delta_{12}g_{k}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\nu(\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+1)N_{0}^{\overline{\theta}\overline{\mu}_{2}}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}N_{m}^{-\overline{\theta}\overline{\mu}_{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.129)

Finally, gathering (
6.100
), (
6.107
), (
6.128
) and (
6.2.1
), we get

\|\Delta_{12}\beta\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+2}.

Putting together this estimate, (
6.21
) and (
6.2.1
) yields

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}\widehat{\beta}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Delta_{12}\beta\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+2}.

\blacktriangleright

Estimate on
\Delta_{12}c_{i}.
Since
V_{0}=\Omega+I_{1}K_{1}
is independent of
r
, then

\Delta_{12}c_{i}=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\Delta_{12}(V_{m+1}-V_{m}).

Therefore we obtain in view of (
6.63
), Sobolev embeddings and (
6.125
) applied with
\widetilde{\mathtt{p}}=0
,

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}(V_{m+1}-V_{m})\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle=\|\langle\Delta_{12}f_{m}\rangle_{\varphi,\theta}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\gamma\overline{\delta}_{m}(s_{0})

\displaystyle\leqslant C\gamma N_{0}^{\overline{\mu}_{2}}N_{m}^{-\overline{\mu}_{2}}\nu(\overline{s}_{h}).

Hence by the composition law in Lemma
4.1
, Lemma
A.1
and (
6.8
) one may find

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}c_{i}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\|\Delta_{12}(V_{m+1}-V_{m})\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\gamma\nu(\overline{s}_{h})N_{0}^{\overline{\mu}_{2}}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}N_{m}^{-\overline{\mu}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\varepsilon\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

This achieves the proof of Proposition
6.2
.
∎

6.2.2
Action on the non-local term

In this section, we shall analyze the conjugation action by
\mathscr{B}
on the nonlocal term appearing in the linearized operator
\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon r}
described in Proposition
6.1
. The main result reads as follows.

Proposition 6.3
.

Let
(\gamma,q,d,\tau_{1},s_{0},\overline{s}_{h},\sigma_{1},S)
satisfy (
4.1
), (
4.2
), (
6.3
) and (
6.23
). We set

\sigma_{2}=s_{0}+\sigma_{1}+3.

(6.130)

For any
(\mu_{2},\mathtt{p},s_{h})
satisfying the condition (
6.24
), there exists
\varepsilon_{0}>0
such that if

\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}\leqslant\varepsilon_{0}\quad\textnormal{and}\quad\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s_{h}+\sigma_{2}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant 1,

(6.131)

then in the Cantor set
\mathcal{O}_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0})
, we have

\mathfrak{L}_{\varepsilon r}:=\mathscr{B}^{-1}\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon r}\mathscr{B}=\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+c_{i_{0}}\partial_{\theta}-\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{K}_{\lambda}\ast\cdot+\partial_{\theta}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}+\mathtt{E}_{n}^{0},

where
\mathcal{K}_{\lambda}
is defined in (
3.12
),
\mathtt{E}_{n}^{0}
is introduced in Proposition
6.2
and
\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}
is a real and reversibility preserving self-adjoint integral operator satisfying

\forall s\in[s_{0},S],\quad\max_{k\in\{0,1,2\}}\|\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{2}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right).

(6.132)

In addition, if
i_{1}
and
i_{2}
are two tori satisfying the smallness property (
6.131
), then

\max_{k\in\{0,1\}}\|\Delta_{12}\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\sigma_{2}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.133)

Proof.

We recall from Proposition
6.1
and Lemma
3.1
. that

\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon r}=\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+\partial_{\theta}\left(V_{\varepsilon r}\cdot\right)-\partial_{\theta}\mathbf{L}_{\varepsilon r},

where
\mathbf{L}_{\varepsilon r}
is a non-local operator defined by

\mathbf{L}_{\varepsilon r}(\rho)(\varphi,\theta)=\int_{\mathbb{T}}\rho(\varphi,\eta)K_{0}(\lambda A_{\varepsilon r}(\varphi,\theta,\eta))d\eta,

with

A_{\varepsilon r}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)=\left(\big(R(\varphi,\eta)-R(\varphi,\theta)\big)^{2}+4R(\varphi,\eta)R(\varphi,\theta)\sin^{2}\left(\tfrac{\eta-\theta}{2}\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

and

R(\varphi,\theta)=\big(1+2\varepsilon r(\varphi,\theta)\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Notice that we have removed the dependance in
(\lambda,\omega)
from the functions in order to alleviate the notation. Hence by Proposition
6.2
, Lemma
6.1
-(i) and (
5.46
), we have in the Cantor set
\mathcal{O}_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0})

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}_{\varepsilon r}

\displaystyle:=\mathscr{B}^{-1}\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon r}\mathscr{B}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{B}^{-1}\big(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+\partial_{\theta}\left(V_{\varepsilon r}\cdot\right)\big)\mathscr{B}-\mathscr{B}^{-1}\partial_{\theta}\mathbf{L}_{\varepsilon r}\mathscr{B}

\displaystyle=\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+c_{i_{0}}\partial_{\theta}-\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{B}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{\varepsilon r}\mathscr{B}+\mathtt{E}_{n}^{0}

\displaystyle=\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+c_{i_{0}}\partial_{\theta}-\partial_{\theta}\Big(\mathcal{B}^{-1}\left(\mathcal{K}_{\lambda}\ast\cdot\right)\mathscr{B}+\mathcal{B}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{\varepsilon r,1}\mathscr{B}\Big)+\mathtt{E}_{n}^{0}.

(6.134)

From a direct computation using (
3.12
) combined with (
6.16
) and (
6.15
), we find

\mathcal{B}^{-1}\big(\mathcal{K}_{\lambda}\ast\mathscr{B}\rho\big)(\varphi,\theta)=\int_{\mathbb{T}}\rho(\varphi,\eta)K_{0}\big(\lambda\mathscr{A}_{\widehat{\beta}}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)\big)d\eta,

where

\mathscr{A}_{\widehat{\beta}}(\varphi,\theta,\eta):=2\left|\sin\left(\tfrac{\eta-\theta}{2}+\widehat{h}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)\right)\right|,

with

\widehat{h}(\varphi,\theta,\eta):=\tfrac{\widehat{\beta}(\varphi,\eta)-\widehat{\beta}(\varphi,\theta)}{2}\cdot

Using elementary trigonometric identities, we can write

\displaystyle\mathscr{A}_{\widehat{\beta}}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)=2\left|\sin\left(\tfrac{\eta-\theta}{2}\right)\right|v_{\widehat{\beta},2}(\varphi,\theta,\eta),

(6.135)

with

v_{\widehat{\beta},2}(\varphi,\theta,\eta):=\cos\left(\widehat{h}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)\right)+\tfrac{\sin\left(\widehat{h}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)\right)}{\tan\left(\frac{\eta-\theta}{2}\right)}\cdot

Notice that
v_{0,2}=1
and one may write

v_{\widehat{\beta},2}(\theta,\eta)=1+\big(\cos\big(\widehat{h}(\theta,\eta)\big)-1\big)+\tfrac{\widehat{h}(\theta,\eta)}{\tan\left(\frac{\eta-\theta}{2}\right)}+\left(\tfrac{\sin(\widehat{h}(\theta,\eta))}{\widehat{h}(\theta,\eta)}-1\right)\tfrac{\widehat{h}(\theta,\eta)}{\tan\left(\frac{\eta-\theta}{2}\right)}

and then using Lemma
4.1
-(iv)-(v), Lemma
4.2
and (
6.28
), we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{\eta\in\mathbb{T}}\big\|v_{\widehat{\beta},2}(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot,\eta+\centerdot)-1\big\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\widehat{\beta}\|_{q,s+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{1}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right),

\displaystyle\forall k\in\mathbb{N}^{*},\,\sup_{\eta\in\mathbb{T}}\big\|(\partial_{\theta}^{k}v_{\widehat{\beta},2})(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot,\eta+\centerdot)\big\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\widehat{\beta}\|_{q,s+k+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{1}+1+k}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right).

(6.136)

Proceeding as for (
5.38
), one obtains the decomposition

K_{0}(\lambda\mathscr{A}_{\widehat{\beta}}(\lambda,\omega,\varphi,\theta,\eta))=K_{0}\left(2\lambda\left|\sin\left(\tfrac{\eta-\theta}{2}\right)\right|\right)+\mathscr{K}(\eta-\theta)\mathscr{K}_{\widehat{\beta},2}^{1}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)+\mathscr{K}_{\widehat{\beta},2}^{2}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)

with similar estimates to (
5.34
) and (
5.37
), that is, for all
k\in{\mathbb{N}},

\displaystyle\sup_{\eta\in\mathbb{T}}\left(\big\|(\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathscr{K}_{\widehat{\beta},2}^{1})(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot,\eta+\centerdot)\big\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\big\|(\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathscr{K}_{\widehat{\beta},2}^{2})(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot,\eta+\centerdot)\big\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\widehat{\beta}\|_{q,s+1+k}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{1}+1+k}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right),

(6.137)

where the symbols
\ast,\cdot,\centerdot
stand for
(\lambda,\omega),\varphi,\theta
, respectively.
Now we shall denote by
\mathbf{L}_{\varepsilon r,2}
the integral operator with the kernel
\mathbb{K}_{\varepsilon r,2}
defined by

\mathbb{K}_{\varepsilon r,2}(\varphi,\theta,\eta):=\mathscr{K}(\eta-\theta)\mathscr{K}_{\widehat{\beta},2}^{1}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)+\mathscr{K}_{\widehat{\beta},2}^{2}(\varphi,\theta,\eta).

(6.138)

Then we find the decomposition

\mathcal{B}^{-1}\left(\mathcal{K}_{\lambda}\ast\cdot\right)\mathscr{B}=\mathcal{K}_{\lambda}\ast\cdot+\mathbf{L}_{\varepsilon r,2}.

Inserting this identity into (
6.2.2
) allows to get

\mathfrak{L}_{\varepsilon r}=\mathscr{B}^{-1}\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon r}\mathscr{B}=\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+c_{i_{0}}\partial_{\theta}-\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{K}_{\lambda}\ast\cdot+\partial_{\theta}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}+\mathtt{E}_{n}^{0},

with

\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}=-\mathbf{L}_{\varepsilon r,2}-\mathcal{B}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{\varepsilon r,1}\mathscr{B}.

(6.139)

Observe that by (
6.4
) and (
6.32
) we can easily check that the kernel
\mathbb{K}_{\varepsilon r,2}
satisfies the following symmetry property

\mathbb{K}_{\varepsilon r,2}(-\varphi,-\theta,-\eta)=\mathbb{K}_{\varepsilon r,2}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)\in\mathbb{R},

(6.140)

which implies in turn, according to Lemma
4.4
, that
\mathbf{L}_{\varepsilon r,2}
is a real and reversibility preserving operator. Moreover, one obtains from (
6.2.2
)

\displaystyle\max_{k\in\{0,1,2\}}\,\big\|(\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathbb{K}_{\varepsilon r,2})(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot,\eta+\centerdot)\big\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{1}+3}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)\,\left(1-\log\left|\sin\left(\tfrac{\eta}{2}\right)\right|\right).

(6.141)

Our next purpose is to highlight some properties of the operator
\mathcal{B}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{\varepsilon r,1}\mathscr{B}
which takes the integral form

\displaystyle\big(\mathcal{B}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{\varepsilon r,1}\mathscr{B}\big)\rho(\varphi,\theta)=\int_{\mathbb{T}}\rho(\varphi,\eta)\widehat{\mathbb{K}}_{\varepsilon r,1}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)d\eta,

(6.142)

where the kernel
\widehat{\mathbb{K}}_{\varepsilon r,1}
is related to the kernel
\mathbb{K}_{\varepsilon r,1}
defined in (
5.41
) through the formula,

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbb{K}}_{\varepsilon r,1}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)=\mathbb{K}_{\varepsilon r,1}\big(\varphi,\theta+\widehat{\beta}(\varphi,\theta),\eta+\widehat{\beta}(\varphi,\eta)\big).

(6.143)

It is quite easy to check from (
5.43
) and (
6.32
), that

\widehat{\mathbb{K}}_{\varepsilon r,1}(-\varphi,-\theta,-\eta)=\widehat{\mathbb{K}}_{\varepsilon r,1}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)\in\mathbb{R}.

(6.144)

According to
(
5.41
), one gets the decomposition

\widehat{\mathbb{K}}_{\varepsilon r,1}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)=\widehat{\mathscr{K}}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)\widehat{\mathscr{K}}_{\varepsilon r,1}^{\,\,1}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)+\widehat{\mathscr{K}}_{\varepsilon r,1}^{\,\,2}(\varphi,\theta,\eta),

(6.145)

with

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathscr{K}}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)

\displaystyle:=\mathscr{K}\big(\eta-\theta+\widehat{\beta}(\varphi,\eta)-\widehat{\beta}(\varphi,\theta)\big),

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathscr{K}}_{\varepsilon r,1}^{\,\,1}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)

\displaystyle:=\mathscr{K}_{\varepsilon r,1}^{1}\big(\varphi,\theta+\widehat{\beta}(\varphi,\theta),\eta+\widehat{\beta}(\varphi,\eta)\big),

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathscr{K}}_{\varepsilon r,1}^{\,\,2}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)

\displaystyle:=\mathscr{K}_{\varepsilon r,1}^{2}\big(\varphi,\theta+\widehat{\beta}(\varphi,\theta),\eta+\widehat{\beta}(\varphi,\eta)\big).

Coming back to
(
5.39
) and using the morphism property of the logarithm, combined with (
6.135
) we deduce that

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathscr{K}}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)

\displaystyle=\sin^{2}\left(\tfrac{\eta-\theta}{2}\right)v_{\widehat{\beta},2}^{2}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)\left(\log\Big|\sin\Big(\tfrac{\eta-\theta}{2}\Big)\Big|+\log\big|v_{\widehat{\beta},2}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)\big|\right)

\displaystyle=\mathscr{K}\big(\eta-\theta\big)+\mathscr{K}\big(\eta-\theta\big)\Big(v_{\widehat{\beta},2}^{2}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)-1\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\sin^{2}\left(\tfrac{\eta-\theta}{2}\right)v_{\widehat{\beta},2}^{2}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)\log\big|v_{\widehat{\beta},2}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)\big|.

Combining Lemma
4.1
-(iv)-(v), (
6.2.2
), (
6.28
) gives for any
\eta\in\mathbb{T}

\displaystyle\max_{k\in\{0,1,2\}}\big\|(\partial_{\theta}^{k}\widehat{\mathscr{K}})\big(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot,

\displaystyle\eta+\centerdot\big)\big\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\,\big\|\widehat{\beta}\|_{q,s+3}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\,\left(1-\log\left|\sin\left(\frac{\eta}{2}\right)\right|\right){-\log\left|\sin\left(\tfrac{\eta}{2}\right)\right|}+1

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{2}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)\left(1-\log\left|\sin\left(\frac{\eta}{2}\right)\right|\right){-\log\left|\sin\left(\tfrac{\eta}{2}\right)\right|}+1.

(6.146)

The next goal is to prove that

\displaystyle\max_{k\in\{0,1,2\}}\sup_{\eta\in\mathbb{T}}\big\|(\partial_{\theta}^{k}\widehat{\mathscr{K}}_{\varepsilon r,1}^{\,\,1})\big(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot,\eta+\centerdot\big)\big\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\,\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{2}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right).

(6.147)

For this aim we first write from (
5.35
) and (
A.2
)

\displaystyle\mathscr{K}_{\varepsilon r,1}^{1}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)

\displaystyle=4\lambda^{2}\big(1-v_{\varepsilon r,1}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)\big)\tilde{I}_{\lambda}(\eta-\theta)

\displaystyle\quad-4\lambda^{2}(v_{\varepsilon r,1}(\varphi,\theta)-1)^{2}\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)I_{0}^{\prime\prime}\left(2\lambda\sin\left(\tfrac{\eta-\theta}{2}\right)(1-t+tv_{\varepsilon r,1}(\varphi,\theta,\eta))\right)dt

\displaystyle:=4\lambda^{2}\big(1-v_{\varepsilon r,1}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)\big)\tilde{I}_{\lambda}(\eta-\theta)+G(\varphi,\theta,\eta),

(6.148)

with

\displaystyle\tilde{I}_{\lambda}(\eta)

\displaystyle:=\frac{I_{0}^{\prime}\left(2\lambda\left|\sin\left(\frac{\eta}{2}\right)\right|\right)}{2\lambda\left|\sin\left(\frac{\eta}{2}\right)\right|}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{\lambda^{2m}\sin^{2m}\left(\frac{\eta}{2}\right)}{m!(m+1)!}\cdot

Then we get the decomposition

\widehat{\mathscr{K}}_{\varepsilon r,1}^{\,\,1}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)=4\lambda^{2}\big[1-\widehat{v}_{\varepsilon r,1}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)\big]\widehat{I}_{\lambda}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)+\widehat{G}(\varphi,\theta,\eta),

with

\displaystyle\widehat{v}_{\varepsilon r,1}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)

\displaystyle:=v_{\varepsilon r,1}\big(\varphi,\theta+\widehat{\beta}(\varphi,\theta),\eta+\widehat{\beta}(\varphi,\eta)\big),

\displaystyle\widehat{I}_{\lambda}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)

\displaystyle:=\tilde{I}_{\lambda}\big(\eta+\widehat{\beta}(\varphi,\eta)-\theta-\widehat{\beta}(\varphi,\theta)\big),

\displaystyle\widehat{G}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)

\displaystyle:=G\big(\varphi,\theta+\widehat{\beta}(\varphi,\theta),\eta+\widehat{\beta}(\varphi,\eta)\big).

It follows that

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathscr{K}}_{\varepsilon r,1}^{\,\,1}(\varphi,\theta,\theta+\eta)=

\displaystyle 4\lambda^{2}\big[1-v_{\varepsilon r,1}\big(\varphi,\theta+\widehat{\beta}(\varphi,\theta),\theta+\eta+\widehat{\beta}(\varphi,\theta+\eta)\big)\big]\tilde{I}_{\lambda}\big(\eta+\widehat{\beta}(\varphi,\theta+\eta)-\widehat{\beta}(\varphi,\theta)\big)

\displaystyle+G\big(\varphi,\theta+\widehat{\beta}(\varphi,\theta),\eta+\theta+\widehat{\beta}(\varphi,\eta+\theta)\big).

(6.149)

Notice that
(\lambda,\eta)\mapsto\tilde{I}_{\lambda}(\eta)
is
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}
, then using Lemma
4.1
-(v) and (
6.28
) yields for any
k\in\mathbb{N}

\displaystyle\sup_{\eta\in\mathbb{T}}\|(\partial_{\theta}^{k}\widehat{I}_{\lambda})(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot,\eta+\centerdot)\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 1+\|\widehat{\beta}\|_{q,s+k}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 1+\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{1}+k}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Now using (
5.2
), Lemma
4.1
-(v), (
6.27
), (
6.7
), (
6.2.2
) and proceeding as in (
5.2
) we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{\eta\in\mathbb{T}}\|\widehat{v}_{\varepsilon r,1}\big(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot,\eta+\centerdot\big)-1\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\|r\|_{q,s+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\varepsilon^{2}\gamma^{-1}\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{1}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|r\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{1}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)

and

\displaystyle\max_{k\in\{1,2\}}\sup_{\eta\in\mathbb{T}}\|(\partial_{\theta}^{k}\widehat{v}_{\varepsilon r,1})\big(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot,\eta+\centerdot\big)\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\|r\|_{q,s+3}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\varepsilon^{2}\gamma^{-1}\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{1}+3}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|r\|_{q,s_{0}+3}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{1}+3}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right).

Arguing as above using the structure of
G
detailed in (
6.2.2
) allows to get

\displaystyle\sup_{\eta\in\mathbb{T}}\|\widehat{G}\big(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot,\eta+\centerdot\big)-1\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{1}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)

and

\displaystyle\max_{k\in\{1,2\}}\sup_{\eta\in\mathbb{T}}\|(\partial_{\theta}^{k}\widehat{G}\big(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot,\eta+\centerdot\big)\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{1}+3}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right).

Thus applying the law products in Lemma
4.1
and using the preceding estimates combined with (
6.2.2
) imply

\displaystyle\max_{k\in\{0,1,2\}}\sup_{\eta\in\mathbb{T}}\|\big(\partial_{\theta}^{k}\widehat{\mathscr{K}}_{\varepsilon r,1}^{\,\,1}\big)(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot,\eta+\centerdot)\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{1}+3}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right),

(6.150)

which gives in particular (
6.147
).
The estimate of the last term
\mathscr{K}_{\varepsilon r,1}^{2}
in (
6.145
), which is connected to (
5.2
), can be treated in a similar way to the estimate (
6.150
) and one finds

\displaystyle\max_{k\in\{0,1,2\}}\sup_{\eta\in\mathbb{T}}\big\|(\partial_{\theta}^{k}\widehat{\mathscr{K}}_{\varepsilon r,1}^{\,\,2})\big(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot,\eta+\centerdot\big)\big\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\,\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{1}+3}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right).

(6.151)

Consequently, putting together (
6.145
),(
6.2.2
), (
6.147
) and (
6.151
) yields

\displaystyle\max_{k\in\{0,1,2\}}\big\|(\partial_{\theta}^{k}\widehat{\mathbb{K}}_{\varepsilon r,1})\big(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot,\eta+\centerdot\big)\big\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{1}+3}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)\left(1-\log\left|\sin\left(\tfrac{\eta}{2}\right)\right|\right).

(6.152)

By (
6.139
) we infer that
\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}
is an integral operator of kernel
\mathbb{K}_{\varepsilon r}
given by

\mathbb{K}_{\varepsilon r}=-\widehat{\mathbb{K}}_{\varepsilon r,1}-\mathbb{K}_{\varepsilon r,2}.

Therefore, by virtue of Lemma
4.4
combined with (
6.141
) and (
6.152
) we find, taking
\sigma_{2}=s_{0}+\sigma_{1}+3,

\displaystyle\max_{k\in\{0,1,2\}}\|\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\max_{k\in\{0,1,2\}}\mathop{\text{\large$\int_{\text{\normalsize$\scriptstyle\mathbb{T}$}}$}}\nolimits\left(\|(\partial_{\theta}^{k}\widehat{\mathbb{K}}_{\varepsilon r,1})(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot,\eta+\centerdot)\|_{q,s+s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|(\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathbb{K}_{\varepsilon r,2})(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot,\eta+\centerdot)\|_{q,s+s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)d\eta

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+s_{0}+\sigma_{1}+3}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)\int_{\mathbb{T}}\left(1-\log\left|\sin\left(\tfrac{\eta}{2}\right)\right|\right)d\eta

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{2}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right).

Notice that by (
6.144
), (
6.140
), the kernel
\mathbb{K}_{\varepsilon r}
satisfies the following symmetry property

\mathbb{K}_{\varepsilon r}(-\varphi,-\theta,-\eta)=\mathbb{K}_{\varepsilon r}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)\in\mathbb{R},

(6.153)

which implies in view of Lemma
4.4
that
\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}
is a real and reversibility preserving Toeplitz in time integral operator. It remains to estimate the quantity
\displaystyle\max_{k\in\{0,1\}}\|\Delta_{12}\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}
. This is will be implemented as before and we shall here sketch the main ideas. First we observe that for
k\in\{0,1\}
the kernel of
\Delta_{12}\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}
is given by

\Delta_{12}\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathbb{K}_{\varepsilon r}=-\Delta_{12}\partial_{\theta}^{k}\widehat{\mathbb{K}}_{\varepsilon r,1}-\Delta_{12}\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathbb{K}_{\varepsilon r,2}.

To estimate
\Delta_{12}\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathbb{K}_{\varepsilon r,2}
we shall use (
6.138
) leading to

\displaystyle\Delta_{12}\mathbb{K}_{\varepsilon r,2}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)=\mathscr{K}(\theta-\eta)\Delta_{12}\mathscr{K}_{\widehat{\beta},2}^{1}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)+\Delta_{12}\mathscr{K}_{\widehat{\beta},2}^{2}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)

(6.154)

and

\displaystyle\Delta_{12}\partial_{\theta}\mathbb{K}_{\varepsilon r,2}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)

\displaystyle=\mathscr{K}(\theta-\eta)\Delta_{12}\partial_{\theta}\mathscr{K}_{\widehat{\beta},2}^{1}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)+\mathscr{K}^{\prime}(\theta-\eta)\Delta_{12}\mathscr{K}_{\widehat{\beta},2}^{1}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\Delta_{12}\partial_{\theta}\mathscr{K}_{\widehat{\beta},2}^{2}(\varphi,\theta,\eta).

(6.155)

Observe from (
6.135
) that the preceding kernels can be expressed with respect to
\widehat{\beta}
. Then proceeding in a similar way to (
5.53
) we obtain

\forall\,i\in\{1,2\},\quad\max_{k\in\{0,1\}}\sup_{\eta\in\mathbb{T}}\|d_{\widehat{\beta}}\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathscr{K}_{\widehat{\beta},2}^{i}[\rho](\ast,\cdot,\centerdot,\eta+\centerdot)\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\|\rho\|_{q,s+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\widehat{\beta}\|_{q,s+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.156)

Applying Taylor Formula yields for all
i\in\{1,2\}
and for all
k\in\{0,1\},

\displaystyle\Delta_{12}\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathscr{K}_{\widehat{\beta},2}^{i}(\varphi,\theta,\theta+\eta)=\mathop{\text{\large$\int_{\text{\normalsize$\scriptstyle\kern-1.22911pt0$}}^{\text{\normalsize$\scriptstyle 1$}}$}}\nolimits d_{\widehat{\beta}}\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathscr{K}_{(1-\tau)\widehat{\beta}_{2}+\tau\widehat{\beta}_{1},2}^{i}[\widehat{\beta}_{1}-\widehat{\beta}_{2}](\varphi,\theta,\theta+\eta)\,d\tau.

It follows from (
6.156
) that for all
i\in\{1,2\}
and for all
k\in\{0,1\}

\displaystyle\big\|\Delta_{12}\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathscr{K}_{\widehat{\beta},2}^{i}(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot,\eta+\centerdot)\big\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\|\widehat{\beta}_{2}-\widehat{\beta}_{1}\|_{q,s+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\widehat{\beta}_{2}-\widehat{\beta}_{1}\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\mathop{\text{\large$\int_{\text{\normalsize$\scriptstyle\kern-1.22911pt0$}}^{\text{\normalsize$\scriptstyle 1$}}$}}\nolimits\|(1-\tau)\widehat{\beta}_{2}+\tau\widehat{\beta}_{1}\|_{q,s+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\,d\tau.

Therefore, by our previous choice of
\sigma_{2}
, we obtain in view of (
6.28
), (
6.31
) (applied with
\mathtt{p}
replaced by
\mathtt{p}+s_{0}
) and the smallness condition (
6.131
),

\displaystyle\forall i\in\{1,2\},\quad\max_{k\in\{0,1\}}\big\|\Delta_{12}\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathscr{K}_{\widehat{\beta},2}^{i}(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot,\eta+\centerdot)\big\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\sigma_{2}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\Big(1+\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\big(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\sigma_{2}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\sigma_{2}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Inserting this estimate into (
6.154
) and (
6.2.2
) yields

\displaystyle\max_{k\in\{0,1\}}\sup_{\eta\in\mathbb{T}}\big\|\Delta_{12}\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathbb{K}_{\varepsilon r,2}(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot,\eta+\centerdot)\big\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\sigma_{2}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.157)

Using similar techniques based on Taylor Formula, one can estimate
\Delta_{12}\partial_{\theta}\widehat{\mathbb{K}}_{\varepsilon r,1}
. We use in particular the identity (
6.145
) combined with (
5.53
), (
6.28
), (
6.31
) and the smallness condition (
6.131
) allowing to get

\max_{k\in\{0,1\}}\sup_{\eta\in\mathbb{T}}\big\|\Delta_{12}\partial_{\theta}^{k}\widehat{\mathbb{K}}_{\varepsilon r,1}(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot,\eta+\centerdot)\big\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\sigma_{2}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.158)

Putting together (
6.157
) and (
6.158
) gives

\max_{k\in\{0,1\}}\sup_{\eta\in\mathbb{T}}\big\|\Delta_{12}\partial_{\theta}^{k}{\mathbb{K}}_{\varepsilon r}(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot,\eta+\centerdot)\big\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\sigma_{2}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Comibining this estimate with Lemma
4.4
yields

\displaystyle\max_{k\in\{0,1\}}\|\Delta_{12}\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\max_{k\in\{0,1\}}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\|\Delta_{12}\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathbb{K}_{\varepsilon r}(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot,\eta+\centerdot)\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}d\eta

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\sigma_{2}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

This completes the proof of the Proposition
6.3
.
∎

6.3
Diagonalization up to small errors

The main goal of this section is to diagonalize, up to small errors, the operator
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\omega}
discussed in Proposition
6.1

and given by

\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\omega}=\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon r}-\varepsilon\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{R})\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}.

This will be performed in two main steps. First, we shall explore the effect of the frequency localization in the normal direction on the transport reduction discussed in Section
6.2
. We essentially get the same structure up to a small perturbation of finite-dimensional rank. Then, in the second step we shall implement a KAM reducibility scheme in order to reduce the remainder to a diagonal one modulo small fast decaying operators. This will be performed through the use of a suitable strong topology on continuous operators given by (
4.10
). With this topology one has tame estimates and the Toeplitz structure of the remainder is very important in this part. The reduction will be conducted by assuming non resonance conditions stemming from the second order Melnikov conditions needed in the resolution of adequate homological equations during the scheme.

6.3.1
Projection in the normal directions

In this section, we study the effects of the reduction of the transport part when the linearized operator is localized in the normal directions. Notice that the change of coordinates does not stabilize the normal subspace and as we shall see the defect of the commutation can be modeled by projectors of finite ranks. Let us define

\mathscr{B}_{\perp}:=\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{B}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp},

where the transformation
\mathscr{B}
is introduced in (
6.15
) and constructed in Proposition
6.2
. Recall that the projection
\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}
and the Soboloev space
H_{\perp}^{s}
were respectively defined in (
5.10
) and (
5.9
). We also recall the following notations

\mathbf{e}_{l,j}(\varphi,\theta)=e^{{\rm i}(l\cdot\varphi+j\theta)}\quad\textnormal{and}\quad e_{m}(\theta)=e^{{\rm i}m\theta}.

The first main result of this section reads as follows.

Lemma 6.3
.

Let
\mathscr{B}
the transformation constructed in Proposition
\ref{reduction of the transport part},
then under the condition (
6.131
) and (
6.24
), the following assertions hold.

(i)

For all
s\in[s_{0},S]
, the operator
\mathscr{B}_{\perp}:W^{q,\infty,\gamma}\big(\mathcal{O},H_{\perp}^{s}\big)\to W^{q,\infty,\gamma}\big(\mathcal{O},H_{\perp}^{s}\big)
is continuous and invertible, with

\|\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{\pm 1}\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\|\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.159)

In addition, we have the representations

\mathscr{B}_{\perp}\rho=\mathscr{B}\rho-\sum_{m\in\mathbb{S}_{0}}\big\langle\rho,\big(\mathcal{B}^{-1}-\textnormal{Id}\big)e_{m}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{\theta}(\mathbb{T})}e_{m}

and

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\rho=\mathscr{B}^{-1}\rho-\sum_{m\in\mathbb{S}_{0}}\big\langle\rho,\big(\mathcal{B}-\textnormal{Id}\big)g_{m}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{\theta}(\mathbb{T})}\mathscr{B}^{-1}e_{m},

where

\quad\mathbf{A}(\varphi)=\Big(\big\langle e_{m},\mathcal{B}e_{k}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{\theta}(\mathbb{T})}\Big)_{m\in\mathbb{S}_{0}\atop k\in\mathbb{S}_{0}},\quad\mathbf{A}^{-1}(\varphi)=\Big(\alpha_{k,m}\Big)_{m\in\mathbb{S}_{0}\atop k\in\mathbb{S}_{0}},\quad g_{m}(\varphi,\theta)=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{S}_{0}}\alpha_{k,m}(\varphi)e_{k}(\theta),

with the estimate

\displaystyle\sup_{k,m\in\mathbb{S}_{0}}\|\alpha_{k,m}-\delta_{km}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{1}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right).

(ii)

Given two tori
i_{1}
and
i_{2}
satisfying the smallness condition (
6.131
), one has

\max_{m\in\mathbb{S}_{0}}\|\Delta_{12}g_{m}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\sigma_{1}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.160)

Proof.

(i)

The first estimate concerning
\mathscr{B}_{\perp}
follows easily from the continuity of the orthogonal projector
\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}
on Sobolev spaces
H^{s}_{\perp},
combined with (
6.27
).
For the representation of
\mathscr{B}_{\perp},
take
\rho\in W^{q,\infty,\gamma}(\mathcal{O},H_{\perp}^{s})
and set

\mathscr{B}_{\perp}\rho=\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{B}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\rho=\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{B}\rho:=g.

Next, we write the following splitting

\mathscr{B}\rho=g+h\quad\hbox{with}\quad\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}h=h.

(6.161)

Notice that the projector
\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}
is defined by

\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}\rho=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{S}_{0}}\rho_{j}e_{j}=\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}}\rho+\langle\rho\rangle_{\theta},

where
\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}}
is defined in (
5.10
) and
\langle\cdot\rangle_{\theta}
denotes the avarage in the variable
\theta.

Therefore

\displaystyle{h(\varphi,\theta)=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{S}_{0}}h_{m}(\varphi)e_{m}(\theta)},

supplemented with the orthogonal conditions

\forall\,k\in\mathbb{S}_{0},\quad\langle\mathscr{B}\rho-h,e_{k}\rangle_{L^{2}_{\theta}(\mathbb{T})}=0.

This implies

h(\varphi,\theta)=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{S}_{0}}\langle\mathscr{B}\rho,e_{m}\rangle_{L^{2}_{\theta}(\mathbb{T})}e_{m}(\theta).

Using Lemma
6.1
-(iii) leads to

h(\varphi,\theta)=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{S}_{0}}\langle\rho,\mathcal{B}^{-1}e_{m}\rangle_{L^{2}_{\theta}(\mathbb{T})}e_{m}(\theta).

Inserting this identity into (
6.161
) yields

\mathscr{B}_{\perp}\rho=g=\mathscr{B}\rho-\sum_{m\in\mathbb{S}_{0}}\big\langle\rho,\mathcal{B}^{-1}e_{m}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{\theta}(\mathbb{T})}e_{m}.

Since
\forall m\in\mathbb{S}_{0},\,\big\langle\rho,e_{m}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{\theta}(\mathbb{T})}=0,
then

\mathscr{B}_{\perp}\rho=g=\mathscr{B}\rho-\sum_{m\in\mathbb{S}_{0}}\big\langle\rho,\big(\mathcal{B}^{-1}-\textnormal{Id}\big)e_{m}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{\theta}(\mathbb{T})}e_{m}.

This ensures the desired representation of
\mathscr{B}_{\perp}
.

Next, we intend to establish similar representation for
\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}
. Let
g\in W^{q,\infty,\gamma}(\mathcal{O},H_{\perp}^{s})
and we need to solve the equation

f\in W^{q,\infty,\gamma}(\mathcal{O},H_{\perp}^{s}),\quad\mathscr{B}_{\perp}f=\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{B}f=g.

This is equivalent to

\mathscr{B}f=g+h,\quad\hbox{with}\quad\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}h=h\quad\hbox{and}\quad\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}f=0.

Then we get

\displaystyle f=\mathscr{B}^{-1}\big(g+h\big),\quad\hbox{with}\quad\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}h=h\quad\textnormal{and}\quad\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}f=0.

(6.162)

The condition
\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}f=0
is equivalent to,

\forall k\in\mathbb{S}_{0},\quad\big\langle\mathscr{B}^{-1}\big(g+h\big),e_{k}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{\theta}(\mathbb{T})}=0.

Therefore using Lemma
6.1
-(iii) the latter equation reads

\forall k\in\mathbb{S}_{0},\quad\big\langle g+h,\widehat{e}_{k}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{\theta}(\mathbb{T})}=0\quad\hbox{with}\quad\widehat{e}_{k}(\varphi,\theta):=\mathcal{B}e_{k}(\varphi,\theta)=e^{{\rm i}k(\theta+\beta(\varphi,\theta))},

which will fix
h
. Indeed, by expanding
\displaystyle h(\varphi,\theta)=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{S}_{0}}a_{m}(\varphi)e_{m}(\theta)
, we can transform the preceding system into

\displaystyle\forall k\in\mathbb{S}_{0},\quad\sum_{m\in\mathbb{S}_{0}}a_{m}(\varphi)\big\langle e_{m},\widehat{e}_{k}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{\theta}(\mathbb{T})}=-\big\langle g,\widehat{e}_{k}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{\theta}(\mathbb{T})}.

(6.163)

Define the matrix

\mathbf{A}(\varphi)=\big(c_{m,k}(\varphi)\big)_{(m,k)\in\mathbb{S}_{0}^{2}},\quad c_{m,k}(\varphi)=\langle e_{m},\widehat{e}_{k}\rangle_{L^{2}_{\theta}(\mathbb{T})}=\int_{\mathbb{T}}e^{{\rm i}((m-k)\theta-k\beta(\varphi,\theta))}d\theta.

(6.164)

Notice that according to (
6.32
) and the change of variables
\theta\mapsto-\theta
, one obtains

\forall(m,k)\in\mathbb{S}_{0}^{2},\quad\forall\varphi\in\mathbb{T}^{d},\quad c_{m,k}(-\varphi)=c_{-m,-k}(\varphi)=\overline{c_{m,k}(\varphi,\theta)}.

(6.165)

One can check by slight adaptation of the composition law in Lemma
4.1
and using the smallness condition (
6.131
) and (
6.28
)

\displaystyle\|c_{m,m}-1\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\mathop{\text{\large$\int_{\text{\normalsize$\scriptstyle\mathbb{T}$}}$}}\nolimits\|e^{-{\rm i}m\beta(\cdot,\theta))}-1\|_{q,H_{\varphi}^{s}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}d\theta

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\beta\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{1}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right).

(6.166)

For
k\neq m\in\mathbb{S}_{0}
we use integration by parts,

c_{m,k}(\varphi)=\tfrac{k}{{\rm i}(m-k)}\int_{\mathbb{T}}e^{{\rm i}((m-k)\theta-k\beta(\varphi,\theta))}\partial_{\theta}\beta(\varphi,\theta)d\theta.

Then using law products and composition laws in Lemma
4.1
combined with (
6.28
) yield

\displaystyle\sup_{(m,k)\in\mathbb{S}_{0}^{2}\atop m\neq k}\|c_{m,k}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\beta\|_{q,s+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{1}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right).

Finally, we get that

\mathbf{A}(\varphi)=\textnormal{Id}+\mathbf{R}(\varphi)\quad\hbox{with}\quad\|\mathbf{R}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\|\beta\|_{q,s+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.167)

Hence under the smallness condition
\|\beta\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\ll 1
following from (
6.131
), combined with the law products in Lemma
4.1
we get that
\mathbf{A}
is invertible with

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{A}^{-1}-\textnormal{Id}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\beta\|_{q,s+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{1}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right).

(6.168)

Therefore the system (
6.163
) is invertible and one gets a unique solution given by

\displaystyle a_{m}(\varphi)=-\sum_{k\in\mathbb{S}_{0}}\alpha_{m,k}(\varphi)\big\langle g,\widehat{e}_{k}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{\theta}(\mathbb{T})}\quad\hbox{with}\quad\mathbf{A}^{-1}(\varphi):=\Big(\alpha_{m,k}(\varphi)\Big)_{(m,k)\in\mathbb{S}_{0}^{2}}.

(6.169)

We claim that the coefficients of
\mathbf{A}^{-1}
admit the same symmetry conditions as (
6.165
), that is

\forall(m,k)\in\mathbb{S}_{0}^{2},\quad\forall\varphi\in\mathbb{T}^{d},\quad\alpha_{m,k}(-\varphi)=\alpha_{-m,-k}(\varphi)=\overline{\alpha_{m,k}(\varphi)}.

(6.170)

This can be done through the series expansion
\displaystyle{A^{-1}=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}}(-1)^{n}(A-\hbox{Id})^{n}}
together with the fact that the entries of the monomials
(A-\hbox{Id})^{n}
satisfy in turn
(
6.165
)
.
Next, using the law products yields

\sup_{m\in\mathbb{S}_{0}}\|a_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\sup_{k\in\mathbb{S}_{0}}\Big(\|\mathbf{A}^{-1}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\big\langle g,\widehat{e}_{k}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{\theta}(\mathbb{T})}\|_{q,H^{s_{0}}_{\varphi}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\mathbf{A}^{-1}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\big\langle g,\widehat{e}_{k}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{\theta}(\mathbb{T})}\|_{q,H^{s}_{\varphi}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\Big).

(6.171)

Notice that one gets from (
6.3.1
)

\displaystyle\sup_{(m,k)\in\mathbb{S}_{0}^{2}}\|\alpha_{k,m}-\delta_{km}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{1}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right),

where
\delta_{km}
denotes the Kronecker symbol.
Let us now move to the estimate of the partial scalar product containing
g
in (
6.171
). Using the law products in Lemma
4.1
with Cauchy-Schwarz inequality gives

\displaystyle\|\big\langle g,\widehat{e}_{k}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{\theta}(\mathbb{T})}\|_{q,H^{s}_{\varphi}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathop{\text{\large$\int_{\text{\normalsize$\scriptstyle\mathbb{T}$}}$}}\nolimits\Big(\|g(\cdot,\theta)\|_{q,H^{s}_{\varphi}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|e^{i\beta(\cdot,\theta)}\|_{q,H^{s_{0}}_{\varphi}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|g(\cdot,\theta)\|_{q,H^{s_{0}}_{\varphi}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|e^{i\beta(\cdot,\theta)}\|_{q,H^{s}_{\varphi}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\Big)d\theta

\displaystyle\lesssim\|g\|_{q,L^{2}_{\theta}H^{s}_{\varphi}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|e^{i\beta}\|_{q,L^{2}_{\theta}H^{s_{0}}_{\varphi}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|g\|_{q,L^{2}_{\theta}H^{s_{0}}_{\varphi}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|e^{i\beta}\|_{q,L^{2}_{\theta}H^{s}_{\varphi}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|g\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|e^{i\beta}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|g\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|e^{i\beta}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Then applying the composition law as in (
6.3.1
) combined with with (
6.28
) and the smallness condition (
6.131
) gives

\displaystyle\|\big\langle g,\widehat{e}_{k}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{\theta}(\mathbb{T})}\|_{q,H^{s}_{\varphi}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|g\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|g\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\beta\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|g\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{1}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)\|g\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Plugging this estimate into (
6.171
) and using (
6.28
), (
6.3.1
) combined with the smallness condition (
6.131
) and Sobolev embeddings implies

\displaystyle\sup_{m\in\mathbb{S}_{0}}\|a_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|g\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{1}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)\|g\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|g\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{1}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|g\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Therefore we obtain

\displaystyle\|h\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{m\in\mathbb{S}_{0}}\|a_{m}\|_{q,H^{s}_{\varphi}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|g\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{1}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|g\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Coming back to (
6.162
) and using (
6.27
), we get

\displaystyle\|f\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|g+h\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{1}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|g+h\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|g\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{1}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|g\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

It follows that

\displaystyle\|\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}g\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|g\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{1}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|g\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

In addition from (
6.169
) and (
6.162
) we deduce the formula

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}g(\varphi,\theta)

\displaystyle=\mathscr{B}^{-1}g(\varphi,\theta)-\sum_{m\in\mathbb{S}_{0}\atop k\in\mathbb{S}_{0}}\alpha_{m,k}(\varphi)\big\langle g,\mathcal{B}{e}_{k}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{\theta}(\mathbb{T})}\mathscr{B}^{-1}e_{m}(\theta)

\displaystyle=\mathscr{B}^{-1}g(\varphi,\theta)-\sum_{m\in\mathbb{S}_{0}}\big\langle g,\mathcal{B}g_{m}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{\theta}(\mathbb{T})}\big(\mathscr{B}^{-1}e_{m}\big)(\varphi,\theta),

(6.172)

with

g_{m}(\varphi,\theta):=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{S}_{0}}\alpha_{m,k}(\varphi){e}_{k}(\theta).

(6.173)

From (
6.170
) and the symmetry of
\mathbb{S}_{0}
, we infer

\forall m\in\mathbb{S}_{0},\quad\forall(\varphi,\theta)\in\mathbb{T}^{d+1},\quad g_{m}(-\varphi,-\theta)=g_{-m}(\varphi,\theta)=\overline{g_{m}(\varphi,\theta)}.

(6.174)

Since
\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}g=g
and
\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}g_{m}=0
then
\big\langle g,g_{m}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{\theta}(\mathbb{T})}=0
and therefore
