\big\langle g,\mathcal{B}g_{m}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{\theta}(\mathbb{T})}=\big\langle g,\big(\mathcal{B}-\textnormal{Id}\big)g_{m}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{\theta}(\mathbb{T})}.

Plugging this identity into (
6.3.1
) yields

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}g

\displaystyle=\mathscr{B}^{-1}g-\sum_{m\in\mathbb{S}_{0}}\big\langle g,\big(\mathcal{B}-\textnormal{Id}\big)g_{m}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{\theta}(\mathbb{T})}\mathscr{B}^{-1}e_{m}.

(ii)
Coming back to the definition of
c_{m,k}
in (
6.164
), one can write

\forall(m,k)\in\mathbb{S}_{0}^{2},\quad\Delta_{12}c_{m,k}=\langle e_{m},(\Delta_{12}\mathcal{B})e_{k}\rangle_{L^{2}_{\theta}(\mathbb{T})}.

Hence, using Taylor Formula and (
6.31
), we have

\max_{(m,k)\in\mathbb{S}_{0}^{2}}\|\Delta_{12}c_{m,k}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\sigma_{1}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

From (
6.173
), one has

\Delta_{12}g_{m}=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{S}_{0}}\Delta_{12}\alpha_{m,k}\,e_{k}.

Thus

\max_{m\in\mathbb{S}_{0}}\|\Delta_{12}g_{m}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\max_{(m,k)\in\mathbb{S}_{0}^{2}}\|\Delta_{12}\alpha_{m,k}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.175)

Using Neumann series, we can write

\mathbf{A}^{-1}(\varphi)=\textnormal{Id}+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{n}\mathbf{R}^{n}(\varphi).

Therefore, the law products in Lemma
4.1
combined with (
6.167
) and the smallness condition (
6.131
) lead to

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}\mathbf{A}^{-1}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\|\Delta_{12}\mathbf{R}^{n}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Delta_{12}\mathbf{R}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\sigma_{1}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

As a consequence,

\max_{(m,k)\in\mathbb{S}_{0}^{2}}\|\Delta_{12}\alpha_{m,k}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\sigma_{1}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.176)

Gathering (
6.176
) and (
6.175
) finally gives

\max_{m\in\mathbb{S}_{0}}\|\Delta_{12}g_{m}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\sigma_{1}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

This achieves the proof of Lemma
6.3
.
∎

In Lemma
6.3
, the parameter
\mathtt{p}
is subject to the constraint (
6.24
) and from now on, we shall fix it to the value

\mathtt{p}=4\tau_{2}q+4\tau_{2}.

(6.177)

This particular choice is determined through some constraints in the proof of the remainder reduction. More precisely, it appears in (
6.373
).
Next we shall establish the second main result of this section.

Proposition 6.4
.

Let
(\gamma,q,d,\tau_{1},s_{0},s_{h},\overline{s}_{h},\mu_{2},\mathtt{p},\sigma_{2},S)
satisfy the assumptions (
4.1
), (
4.2
), (
6.3
), (
6.24
), (
6.130
) and (
6.177
). Consider the operator
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\omega}
defined in Proposition
\ref{lemma setting for Lomega}.

There exists
\varepsilon_{0}>0
and
\sigma_{3}=\sigma_{3}(\tau_{1},q,d,s_{0})\geqslant\sigma_{2}
such that if

\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}\leqslant\varepsilon_{0}\quad\textnormal{and}\quad\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s_{h}+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant 1,

(6.178)

then the following assertions hold true.

(i)

For any
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*},
in the Cantor set
\mathcal{O}_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0})
introduced in Proposition
\ref{reduction of the transport part},
we have

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\omega}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}

\displaystyle=\big(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+c_{i_{0}}\partial_{\theta}-\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{K}_{\lambda}\ast\cdot\big)\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}+\mathscr{R}_{0}+\mathtt{E}_{n}^{1}

\displaystyle:=\big(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+\mathscr{D}_{0}\big)\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}+\mathscr{R}_{0}+\mathtt{E}_{n}^{1}

\displaystyle:=\mathscr{L}_{0}+\mathtt{E}_{n}^{1},

where
\mathscr{D}_{0}
is a reversible Fourier multiplier given by

\forall(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c},\quad\mathscr{D}_{0}\mathbf{e}_{l,j}={\rm i}\mu_{j}^{0}\,\mathbf{e}_{l,j},

with

\mu_{j}^{0}(\lambda,\omega,i_{0})=\Omega_{j}(\lambda)+jr^{1}(\lambda,\omega,i_{0})\quad\mbox{ and }\quad r^{1}(\lambda,\omega,i_{0})=c_{i_{0}}(\lambda,\omega)-V_{0}(\lambda)

and such that

\|r^{1}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\quad\textnormal{and}\quad\|\Delta_{12}r^{1}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.179)

(ii)

The operator
\mathtt{E}_{n}^{1}
satisfies the following estimate

\|\mathtt{E}_{n}^{1}\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}N_{n+1}^{-\mu_{2}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.180)

(iii)

\mathscr{R}_{0}
is a real and reversible Toeplitz in time operator satisfying
\mathscr{R}_{0}=\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{R}_{0}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}
with

\forall s\in[s_{0},S],\quad\max_{k\in\{0,1\}}\|\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathscr{R}_{0}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)

(6.181)

and

\|\Delta_{12}\mathscr{R}_{0}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.182)

(iv)

The operator
\mathscr{L}_{0}
satisfies

\forall s\in[s_{0},S],\quad\|\mathscr{L}_{0}\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\|\rho\|_{q,s+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.183)

Proof.

(i)
We shall first start with finding a suitable expansion for
\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\omega}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}
. Using the expression of
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\omega}
given in Proposition
6.1
and the decomposition
\textnormal{Id}=\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}+\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}
we write

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\omega}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon r}-\varepsilon\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{R})\mathscr{B}_{\perp}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon r}\mathscr{B}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}-\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon r}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}\mathscr{B}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}-\varepsilon\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{R}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}.

According to the definitions of
\mathfrak{L}_{\varepsilon r}
and
\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon r}
seen in Proposition
6.3
and in Lemma
3.1
and using (
5.46
), one has in the Cantor set
\mathcal{O}_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0})

\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon r}\mathscr{B}=\mathscr{B}\mathfrak{L}_{\varepsilon r}\quad\hbox{and}\quad\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon r}=\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+\partial_{\theta}\left(V_{\varepsilon r}\cdot\right)-\partial_{\theta}\mathbf{L}_{\varepsilon r,1}-\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{K}_{\lambda}\ast\cdot

and therefore

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\omega}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}=

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{B}\mathfrak{L}_{\varepsilon r}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}-\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\left(\partial_{\theta}\left(V_{\varepsilon r}\cdot\right)-\partial_{\theta}\mathbf{L}_{\varepsilon r,1}\right)\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}\mathscr{B}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}-\varepsilon\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{R}\mathscr{B}_{\perp},

where we have used the identities

\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}=\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\quad\hbox{and}\quad[\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp},T]=0=[\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}},T],

for any Fourier multiplier
T
. The structure of
\mathfrak{L}_{\varepsilon r}
is detailed in Proposition
6.3
, and from this we deduce that

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{B}\mathfrak{L}_{\varepsilon r}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}

\displaystyle=\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{B}\big(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+c_{i_{0}}\partial_{\theta}-\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{K}_{\lambda}\ast\cdot+\partial_{\theta}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}+\mathtt{E}_{n}^{0}\big)\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}

\displaystyle=\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{B}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\big(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+c_{i_{0}}\partial_{\theta}-\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{K}_{\lambda}\ast\cdot\big)+\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{B}\partial_{\theta}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}+\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{B}\mathtt{E}_{n}^{0}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{B}_{\perp}\big(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+c_{i_{0}}\partial_{\theta}-\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{K}_{\lambda}\ast\cdot\big)+\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{B}\partial_{\theta}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}+\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{B}\mathtt{E}_{n}^{0}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}.

It follows that

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{B}\mathfrak{L}_{\varepsilon r}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}

\displaystyle=\big(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+c_{i_{0}}\partial_{\theta}-\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{K}_{\lambda}\ast\cdot\big)\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}+\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{B}\partial_{\theta}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}+\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{B}\mathtt{E}_{n}^{0}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}

\displaystyle=\big(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+c_{i_{0}}\partial_{\theta}-\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{K}_{\lambda}\ast\cdot\big)\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}+\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\partial_{\theta}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}+\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\mathscr{B}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}\partial_{\theta}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}

\displaystyle\quad+\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{B}\mathtt{E}_{n}^{0}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}.

Consequently, in the Cantor set
\mathcal{O}_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0})
, one has the following reduction

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\omega}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}=

\displaystyle\big(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+c_{i_{0}}\partial_{\theta}-\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{K}_{\lambda}\ast\cdot\big)\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}+\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\partial_{\theta}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}+\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\mathscr{B}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}\partial_{\theta}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}

\displaystyle-\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\left(\partial_{\theta}\left(V_{\varepsilon r}\cdot\right)-\partial_{\theta}\mathbf{L}_{\varepsilon r,1}\right)\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}\mathscr{B}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}-\varepsilon\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{R}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}+\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{B}\mathtt{E}_{n}^{0}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\big(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+c_{i_{0}}\partial_{\theta}-\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{K}_{\lambda}\ast\cdot\big)\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}+\mathscr{R}_{0}+\mathtt{E}_{n}^{1},

(6.184)

where we set

\mathtt{E}_{n}^{1}:=\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{B}\mathtt{E}_{n}^{0}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}.

Notice that the estimates (
6.179
) are simple reformulations of (
6.26
) and (
6.30
) since
\Delta_{12}r^{1}=\Delta_{12}c_{i}
.

(ii)
By using (
6.159
), (
6.27
), the continuity of the projectors, (
6.29
) and (
6.178
), one obtains

\displaystyle\|\mathtt{E}_{n}^{1}\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathscr{B}\mathtt{E}_{n}^{0}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathtt{E}_{n}^{0}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}N_{n+1}^{-\mu_{2}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(iii)
Now, we shall prove the following estimates,

\max_{k\in\{0,1\}}\|\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathscr{R}_{0}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)

(6.185)

and

\|\Delta_{12}\mathscr{R}_{0}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.186)

To do that, we shall study separately the different terms appearing in (
6.3.1
) in the definition of
\mathscr{R}_{0}.

Notice that in the various estimates below, we use the notation
\sigma_{3}
to denote some loss of regularity. This index depends only on
\tau_{1},q,d,s_{0}
and may change increasingly from one line to another and it is always taken greater than the
\sigma_{2}
introduced in Proposition
6.3
.

\blacktriangleright

Study of the term

\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\partial_{\theta}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}
. One gets easily according to (
6.132
) and (
6.133
)

\displaystyle\max_{k\in\{0,1\}}\|\partial_{\theta}^{k}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\partial_{\theta}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\max_{k\in\{0,1,2\}}\|\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)

(6.187)

and

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}\big(\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\partial_{\theta}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\big)\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Delta_{12}\partial_{\theta}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.188)

\blacktriangleright

Study of the term

\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\mathscr{B}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}\partial_{\theta}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}
. Using the first point of Proposition
6.4
yields

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\mathscr{B}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}\partial_{\theta}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}

\displaystyle=\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}\partial_{\theta}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}-\mathcal{T}_{0}\mathcal{S}_{1},

(6.189)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{0}\rho=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{S}_{0}}\big\langle\rho,\big(\mathcal{B}-\textnormal{Id}\big)g_{m}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{\theta}(\mathbb{T})}\mathscr{B}^{-1}e_{m}\quad\hbox{and}\quad\mathcal{S}_{1}:=\mathscr{B}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}\partial_{\theta}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}.

(6.190)

To estimate the first term, we use Proposition
6.3

\displaystyle\max_{k\in\{0,1\}}\|\partial_{\theta}^{k}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}\partial_{\theta}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\max_{k\in\{0,1,2\}}\|\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right).

(6.191)

As to the second term, we write

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{0}\mathcal{S}_{1}\rho

\displaystyle=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{S}_{0}}\big\langle\mathcal{S}_{1}\rho,\big(\mathcal{B}-\textnormal{Id}\big)g_{m}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{\theta}(\mathbb{T})}\mathscr{B}^{-1}e_{m}

\displaystyle=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{S}_{0}}\big\langle\rho,\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\star}\big(\mathcal{B}-\textnormal{Id}\big)g_{m}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{\theta}(\mathbb{T})}\mathscr{B}^{-1}e_{m},

where
\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\star}
is the
L^{2}_{\theta}(\mathbb{T})
-adjoint of
\mathcal{S}_{1}.
This is an integral operator taking the form

\displaystyle\big(\mathcal{T}_{0}\mathcal{S}_{1}\rho\big)(\varphi,\theta)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}}\mathcal{K}_{1}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)\rho(\varphi,\eta)d\eta,

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{1}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{S}_{0}}\big(\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\star}\big(\mathcal{B}-\textnormal{Id}\big)g_{m}\big)(\varphi,\eta)\big(\mathscr{B}^{-1}e_{m}\big)(\varphi,\theta).

Recall from Proposition
6.3
that
\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}
is self-adjoint and using Lemma
6.1
we have the identities

\mathscr{B}^{\star}=\mathcal{B}^{-1}
and
\mathcal{B}^{\star}=\mathscr{B}^{-1},
then

\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\star}=-\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}\partial_{\theta}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}\mathcal{B}^{-1}.

(6.192)

Therefore, combining (
6.174
), (
6.32
) and (
6.153
) imply

\mathcal{K}_{1}(-\varphi,-\theta,-\eta)=-\mathcal{K}_{1}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)\in\mathbb{R}.

(6.193)

Applying Lemma
4.4
combined with the law products yield for any
k\in\mathbb{N}

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathcal{T}_{0}\mathcal{S}_{1}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathop{\text{\large$\int_{\text{\normalsize$\scriptstyle\mathbb{T}$}}$}}\nolimits\|(\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathcal{K}_{1})(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot,\eta+\centerdot)\|_{q,s+s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}d\eta

(6.194)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{m\in\mathbb{S}_{0}}\Big(\big\|\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\star}\big(\mathcal{B}-\textnormal{Id}\big)g_{m}\big\|_{q,s+s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\big\|\mathscr{B}^{-1}e_{m}\big\|_{q,s_{0}+k}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\big\|\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\star}\big(\mathcal{B}-\textnormal{Id}\big)g_{m}\big\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\big\|\mathscr{B}^{-1}e_{m}\big\|_{q,s+s_{0}+k}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\Big).

Remark that (
6.192
) implies

\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\star}\big(\mathcal{B}-\textnormal{Id}\big)g_{m}=-\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}\partial_{\theta}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}\big(\textnormal{Id}-\mathcal{B}^{-1}\big)g_{m}.

Hence according to Lemma
4.3
combined with Proposition
6.3
we find

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\star}\big(\mathcal{B}-\textnormal{Id}\big)g_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\partial_{\theta}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}\big(\textnormal{Id}-\mathcal{B}^{-1}\big)g_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\partial_{\theta}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}\big(\textnormal{Id}-\mathcal{B}^{-1}\big)g_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)\|\big(\textnormal{Id}-\mathcal{B}^{-1}\big)g_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\quad+\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s_{0}+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)\|\big(\textnormal{Id}-\mathcal{B}^{-1}\big)g_{m}\|_{q,s+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.195)

Using (
6.27
) together with Lemma
6.3
and the smallness condition (
6.178
) leads to

\displaystyle\big\|\big(\textnormal{Id}-\mathcal{B}^{-1}\big)g_{m}\big\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|g_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+{\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}}\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|g_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{k,m\in\mathbb{S}_{0}}\|\alpha_{k,m}\|_{q,H^{s}_{\varphi}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+{\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}}\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\sup_{k,m\in\mathbb{S}_{0}}\|\alpha_{k,m}\|_{q,H^{s_{0}}_{\varphi}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 1+{\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}}\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.196)

Therefore, inserting this estimate into (
6.3.1
) and using (
6.178
) allow to get

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\star}\big(\mathcal{B}-\textnormal{Id}\big)g_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right).

Plugging this estimate into (
6.194
) and using (
6.27
) ensure

\displaystyle\max_{k\in\{0,1\}}\|\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathcal{T}_{0}\mathcal{S}_{1}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right).

(6.197)

Consequently, by combining (
6.189
), (
6.3.1
) and (
6.197
), we find

\max_{k\in\{0,1\}}\|\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\mathscr{B}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}\partial_{\theta}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right).

(6.198)

We now turn to the difference estimate. From (
6.189
), it is obvious that

\Delta_{12}\big(\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\mathscr{B}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}\partial_{\theta}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\big)=\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}\Delta_{12}\partial_{\theta}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}-\Delta_{12}(\mathcal{T}_{0}\mathcal{S}_{1}).

(6.199)

To estimate the first term, we use (
6.133
)

\displaystyle\|\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}\Delta_{12}\partial_{\theta}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Delta_{12}\partial_{\theta}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.200)

As to the second term, we notice that
\Delta_{12}\big(\mathcal{T}_{0}\mathcal{S}_{1}\big)
is an integral operator whose kernel
\Delta_{12}\mathcal{K}_{1}
is

\displaystyle\Delta_{12}\mathcal{K}_{1}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)

\displaystyle=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{S}_{0}}\Delta_{12}\big(\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\star}\big(\mathcal{B}-\textnormal{Id}\big)g_{m}\big)(\varphi,\eta)\big(\mathscr{B}_{r_{1}}e_{m}\big)(\varphi,\theta)

\displaystyle\quad+\big(\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\star}\big(\mathcal{B}-\textnormal{Id}\big)g_{m}\big)_{r_{2}}(\varphi,\eta)\big(\Delta_{12}\mathscr{B}e_{m}\big)(\varphi,\theta).

Hence, using Lemma
4.4
-(ii) together with the law products we deduce that

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}\mathcal{T}_{0}\mathcal{S}_{1}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{T}}\|\Delta_{12}\mathcal{K}_{1}(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot,\eta+\centerdot)\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}d\eta

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{m\in\mathbb{S}_{0}}\|\Delta_{12}\big(\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\star}\big(\mathcal{B}-\textnormal{Id}\big)g_{m}\big)\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\mathscr{B}_{r_{1}}e_{m}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\quad+\|\big(\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\star}\big(\mathcal{B}-\textnormal{Id}\big)g_{m}\big)_{r_{2}}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\Delta_{12}\mathscr{B}e_{m}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Notice that by Taylor Formula and (
6.31
) (applied with
\mathtt{p}
replaced by
\mathtt{p}+s_{0}
), one has

\sup_{m\in\mathbb{S}_{0}}\|\Delta_{12}\mathscr{B}^{-1}e_{m}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\sigma_{3}}.

(6.201)

On the other hand, we have

\Delta_{12}\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\star}=-\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\Delta_{12}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}\partial_{\theta}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}\mathcal{B}_{r_{1}}^{-1}-\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r_{2}}\partial_{\theta}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}\Delta_{12}\mathcal{B}^{-1},

leading to

\displaystyle\Delta_{12}\big(\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\star}\big(\mathcal{B}-\textnormal{Id}\big)g_{m}\big)=

\displaystyle-\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\Delta_{12}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}\partial_{\theta}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}\big(\textnormal{Id}-\mathcal{B}_{r_{1}}^{-1}\big)g_{m,r_{1}}-\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r_{2}}\partial_{\theta}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}\Delta_{12}\mathcal{B}^{-1}\big(\mathcal{B}_{r_{1}}-\textnormal{Id}\big)g_{m,r_{1}}

\displaystyle+\mathcal{S}_{1,r_{2}}^{\star}\big(\Delta_{12}\mathcal{B}\big)g_{m,r_{1}}+\mathcal{S}_{1,r_{2}}^{\star}\big(\mathcal{B}_{r_{2}}-\textnormal{Id}\big)\Delta_{12}g_{m}.

AAccording to Lemma
4.3
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\Delta_{12}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}\partial_{\theta}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}\big(\textnormal{Id}-\mathcal{B}_{r_{1}}^{-1}\big)g_{m,r_{1}}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Delta_{12}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\big(\textnormal{Id}-\mathcal{B}_{r_{1}}^{-1}\big)g_{m,r_{1}}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

From (
6.3.1
), one has

\displaystyle\|\big(\textnormal{Id}-\mathcal{B}_{r_{1}}^{-1}\big)g_{m,r_{1}}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim 1+\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\mathfrak{I}_{1}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Thus, from (
6.133
) and(
6.178
), we infer

\|\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\Delta_{12}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}\partial_{\theta}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}\big(\textnormal{Id}-\mathcal{B}_{r_{1}}^{-1}\big)g_{m,r_{1}}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.202)

Applying Lemma
4.3
, (
6.132
) and (
6.178
) we deduce that

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{S}_{1,r_{2}}^{\star}\big(\Delta_{12}\mathcal{B}\big)g_{m,r_{1}}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r_{2}}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,\overline{s}_{h}+s_{0}}\|\mathcal{B}_{r_{2}}\big(\Delta_{12}\mathcal{B}\big)g_{m,r_{1}}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathcal{B}_{r_{2}}\big(\Delta_{12}\mathcal{B}\big)g_{m,r_{1}}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

To estimate the right hand side member, it suffices to use (
6.27
) and (
6.178
), leading to

\|\mathcal{B}_{r_{2}}\big(\Delta_{12}\mathcal{B}\big)g_{m,r_{1}}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\|\big(\Delta_{12}\mathcal{B}\big)g_{m,r_{1}}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

By Taylor Formula, we may write

\Delta_{12}\mathcal{B}\rho(\theta)=\Delta_{12}\beta(\theta)\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{\theta}\rho\big(\theta+\beta_{2}(\theta)+t\Delta_{12}\beta(\theta)\big)dt.

It follows from the law products in Lemma
4.1
, (
6.31
) and (
6.178
) that

\displaystyle\|\big(\Delta_{12}\mathcal{B}\big)g_{m,r_{1}}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Delta_{12}\beta\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|g_{m,r_{1}}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+s_{0}+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Thus

\|\mathcal{S}_{1,r_{2}}^{\star}\big(\Delta_{12}\mathcal{B}\big)g_{m,r_{1}}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.203)

In the same way, using Taylor Formula together with (
6.31
), we get

\|\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r_{2}}\partial_{\theta}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}\Delta_{12}\mathcal{B}^{-1}\big(\mathcal{B}_{r_{1}}-\textnormal{Id}\big)g_{m,r_{1}}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.204)

By Lemma
4.3
,(
6.132
) and (
6.178
), one finds

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{S}_{1,r_{2}}^{\star}\big(\mathcal{B}_{r_{2}}-\textnormal{Id}\big)\Delta_{12}g_{m}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r_{2}}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\big(\mathcal{B}_{r_{2}}-\textnormal{Id}\big)\Delta_{12}g_{m}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\big(\mathcal{B}_{r_{2}}-\textnormal{Id}\big)\Delta_{12}g_{m}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Applying (
6.27
) and (
6.178
), we obtain

\|\big(\mathcal{B}_{r_{2}}-\textnormal{Id}\big)\Delta_{12}g_{m}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\|\Delta_{12}g_{m}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Using (
6.160
) (applied with
\mathtt{p}=s_{0}+1
), we finally get

\|\mathcal{S}_{1,r_{2}}^{\star}\big(\mathcal{B}_{r_{2}}-\textnormal{Id}\big)\Delta_{12}g_{m}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.205)

Gathering (
6.201
), (
6.202
), (
6.203
), (
6.204
) and (
6.205
) implies

\|\Delta_{12}\big(\mathcal{T}_{0}\mathcal{S}_{1}\big)\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.206)

Putting together (
6.199
), (
6.3.1
) and (
6.206
), one obtains

\|\Delta_{12}\big(\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\mathscr{B}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}\partial_{\theta}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\big)\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.207)

\blacktriangleright

Study of the term

\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\left(\partial_{\theta}\left(V_{\varepsilon r}\cdot\right)-\partial_{\theta}\mathbf{L}_{\varepsilon r,1}\right)\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}\mathscr{B}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}.

We first write,

\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\left(\partial_{\theta}\left(V_{\varepsilon r}\cdot\right)-\partial_{\theta}\mathbf{L}_{\varepsilon r,1}\right)\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}\mathscr{B}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}:=\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{S}_{2}\mathscr{B}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp},

with

\mathcal{S}_{2}=\left(\big(V_{\varepsilon r}-c_{i_{0}}\big)\cdot-\mathbf{L}_{\varepsilon r,1}\right)\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}.

Notice that to get the above identity we have used the identity

\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\partial_{\theta}(c_{i_{0}}\cdot)\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}=0.

Recall from (
5.46
) and (
5.41
) that

\mathbf{L}_{\varepsilon r,1}\rho(\varphi,\theta)=\int_{\mathbb{T}}\mathbb{K}_{\varepsilon r,1}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)\rho(\varphi,\eta)d\eta.

Then from elementary computations we find

\mathcal{S}_{2}\rho(\varphi,\theta)=\int_{\mathbb{T}}\mathcal{K}_{2}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)\rho(\varphi,\eta)d\eta,

with

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{2}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)

\displaystyle:=\big(V_{\varepsilon r}(\varphi,\theta)-c_{i_{0}}\big)D_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}(\theta-\eta)-\int_{\mathbb{T}}\mathbb{K}_{\varepsilon r,1}(\varphi,\theta,\eta^{\prime})D_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}(\eta^{\prime}-\eta)d\eta^{\prime},

\displaystyle D_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}(\theta)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{S}_{0}}e^{in\theta}.

Combining (
5.43
), (
6.4
), (
3.3
) and the change of variables
\eta^{\prime}\mapsto\eta^{\prime}
, one gets

\mathcal{K}_{2}(-\varphi,-\theta,-\eta)=\mathcal{K}_{2}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)\in\mathbb{R}.

(6.208)

Proceeding as in (
6.189
) we obtain

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{S}_{2}\mathscr{B}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{B}^{-1}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{S}_{2}\mathscr{B}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}-\mathcal{T}_{0}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{S}_{2}\mathscr{B}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}.

(6.209)

It follows that

\displaystyle\big\|\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{S}_{2}\mathscr{B}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\big\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\|\partial_{\theta}^{k+1}\mathcal{B}^{-1}\mathcal{S}_{2}\mathscr{B}\big\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\big\|\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathcal{T}_{0}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{S}_{2}\mathscr{B}\big\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.210)

The expression of the first term is similar to that of (
6.142
), namely, one has

\displaystyle\big(\mathcal{B}^{-1}\mathcal{S}_{2}\mathscr{B}\big)\rho(\varphi,\theta)=\int_{\mathbb{T}}\rho(\varphi,\eta)\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{2}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)d\eta,

with

\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{2}(\varphi,\theta,\eta):=\mathcal{K}_{2}\big(\varphi,\theta+\widehat{\beta}(\varphi,\theta),\eta+\widehat{\beta}(\varphi,\eta)\big).

Combining (
6.208
) and (
6.32
), one gets

\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{2}(-\varphi,-\theta,-\eta)=\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{2}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)\in\mathbb{R}.

(6.211)

Then coming bacl to (
6.143
) and arguing as for (
6.152
), we find

\displaystyle\sup_{k\in\{0,1,2\}}\big\|(\partial_{\theta}^{k}\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{2})\big(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot,\eta+\centerdot\big)\big\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)\Big(1-\log\big|\sin\big({\eta}/{2}\big)\big|\Big).

(6.212)

By virtue of Lemma
4.4
and (
6.212
) we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{k\in\{0,1\}}\big\|\partial_{\theta}^{k+1}\mathcal{B}^{-1}\mathcal{S}_{2}\mathscr{B}\big\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{k\in\{0,1\}}\mathop{\text{\large$\int_{\text{\normalsize$\scriptstyle\mathbb{T}$}}$}}\nolimits\big\|(\partial_{\theta}^{k+1}\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{2})\big(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot,\eta+\centerdot\big)\big\|_{q,s+s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}d\eta

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right).

(6.213)

Notice that from (
6.190
), we can write

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{0}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{S}_{2}\mathscr{B}\rho

\displaystyle=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{S}_{0}}\big\langle\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{S}_{2}\mathscr{B}\rho,\big(\mathcal{B}-\textnormal{Id}\big)g_{m}\big\rangle_{L_{\theta}^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\mathscr{B}^{-1}e_{m}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{m\in\mathbb{S}_{0}}\big\langle\rho,\mathcal{B}^{-1}\mathcal{S}_{2}^{\star}\partial_{\theta}\big(\mathcal{B}-\textnormal{Id}\big)g_{m}\big\rangle_{L_{\theta}^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\mathscr{B}^{-1}e_{m},

where
\mathcal{S}_{2}^{\star}
is the adjoint of
\mathcal{S}_{2}
and is given by

\mathcal{S}_{2}^{\star}=\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}\big(\big(V_{\varepsilon r}-c_{i_{0}}\big)\cdot-\mathbf{L}_{\varepsilon r,1}\big).

(6.214)

This is an integral operator taking the form

\displaystyle\big(\mathcal{T}_{0}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{S}_{2}\mathscr{B}\rho\big)(\varphi,\theta)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}}\mathcal{K}_{3}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)\rho(\varphi,\eta)d\eta,

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{3}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{S}_{0}}\big(\mathcal{B}^{-1}\mathcal{S}_{2}^{\star}\partial_{\theta}\big(\mathcal{B}-\textnormal{Id}\big)g_{m}\big)(\varphi,\eta)\big(\mathscr{B}^{-1}e_{m}\big)(\varphi,\theta).

According to (
6.174
), (
6.32
), (
6.214
), (
6.4
), (
3.3
) and (
5.43
), one gets

\mathcal{K}_{3}(-\varphi,-\theta,-\eta)=-\mathcal{K}_{3}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)\in\mathbb{R}.

(6.215)

On the other hand, applying Lemma
4.4
combined with the law products yield for any
k\in\mathbb{N}

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathcal{T}_{0}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{S}_{2}\mathscr{B}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{T}}\|(\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathcal{K}_{3})(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot,\eta+\centerdot)\|_{q,s+s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}d\eta

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{m\in\mathbb{S}_{0}}\Big(\|\mathcal{B}^{-1}\mathcal{S}_{2}^{\star}\partial_{\theta}\big(\mathcal{B}-\textnormal{Id}\big)g_{m}\|_{q,s+s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\mathscr{B}^{-1}e_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}+k}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\mathcal{B}^{-1}\mathcal{S}_{2}^{\star}\partial_{\theta}\big(\mathcal{B}-\textnormal{Id}\big)g_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\mathscr{B}^{-1}e_{m}\|_{q,s+s_{0}+k}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\Big).

Applying (
6.27
) we find

\|\mathcal{B}^{-1}\mathcal{S}_{2}^{\star}\partial_{\theta}\big(\mathcal{B}-\textnormal{Id}\big)g_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\|\mathcal{S}_{2}^{\star}\partial_{\theta}\big(\mathcal{B}-\textnormal{Id}\big)g_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\mathcal{S}_{2}^{\star}\partial_{\theta}\big(\mathcal{B}-\textnormal{Id}\big)g_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Now, from (
6.214
), the law products and Lemma
4.3
, we find

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{S}_{2}^{\star}\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|V_{\varepsilon r}-c_{i_{0}}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|V_{\varepsilon r}-c_{i_{0}}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle+\|\mathbf{L}_{\varepsilon r,1}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\mathbf{L}_{\varepsilon r,1}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

From the composition law and (
6.26
), one has

\displaystyle\|V_{\varepsilon r}-c_{i_{0}}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|V_{\varepsilon r}-V_{0}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|V_{0}-c_{i_{0}}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right).

According to Lemma
4.4
and (
5.42
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{L}_{\varepsilon r,1}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{T}}\|\mathbb{K}_{\varepsilon r,1}(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot,\eta+\centerdot)\|_{q,s+s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}d\eta

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right).

Using (
6.178
), one gets

\|\mathcal{S}_{2}^{\star}\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\|\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Combining this with (
6.27
) allow to get

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{S}_{2}^{\star}\partial_{\theta}\big(\mathcal{B}-\textnormal{Id}\big)g_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|g_{m}\|_{q,s+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|g_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 1+\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Therefore,

\max_{k\in\{0,1\}}\big\|\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathcal{T}_{0}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{S}_{2}\mathscr{B}\big\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right).

(6.216)

Plugging the estimates (
6.3.1
) and (
6.216
) into (
6.210
) we find

\displaystyle\max_{k\in\{0,1\}}\big\|\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{S}_{2}\mathscr{B}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\big\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\max_{k\in\{0,1\}}\big\|\partial_{\theta}^{k+1}\mathcal{B}^{-1}\mathcal{S}_{2}\mathscr{B}\big\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\max_{k\in\{0,1\}}\big\|\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathcal{T}_{0}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{S}_{2}\mathscr{B}\big\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right).

(6.217)

We now turn to the estimate of the difference. Coming back to (
6.209
), one can write

\Delta_{12}\big(\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{S}_{2}\mathscr{B}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\big)=\Delta_{12}\big(\mathscr{B}^{-1}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{S}_{2}\mathscr{B}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\big)-\Delta_{12}\big(\mathcal{T}_{0}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{S}_{2}\mathscr{B}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\big).

It follows that

\big\|\Delta_{12}\big(\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{S}_{2}\mathscr{B}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\big)\big\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\big\|\Delta_{12}\big(\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{B}^{-1}\mathcal{S}_{2}\mathscr{B}\big)\big\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\big\|\Delta_{12}\big(\mathcal{T}_{0}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{S}_{2}\mathscr{B}\big)\big\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.218)

Arguing as for (
6.158
), one obtains

\|\Delta_{12}(\partial_{\theta}\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{2})(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot,\eta+\centerdot)\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+s_{0}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\left(1-\log\left|\sin\left(\tfrac{\eta}{2}\right)\right|\right).

Then, using Lemma
4.4
implies

\displaystyle\big\|\Delta_{12}\big(\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{B}^{-1}\mathcal{S}_{2}\mathscr{B}\big)\big\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{T}}\|\Delta_{12}(\partial_{\theta}\widehat{\mathcal{K}}_{2})(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot,\eta+\centerdot)\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+s_{0}}d\eta

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.219)

On the other hand, proceeding as for (
6.206
), and using in particular (
6.30
),

\big\|\Delta_{12}\big(\mathcal{T}_{0}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{S}_{2}\mathscr{B}\big)\big\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.220)

Putting together (
6.3.1
), (
6.220
) and (
6.218
), ensures that

\big\|\Delta_{12}\big(\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{S}_{2}\mathscr{B}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\big)\big\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.221)

\blacktriangleright

Study of the term

\varepsilon\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{R}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}.
Using the relation
\textnormal{Id}=\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}+\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp},
we can write

\displaystyle\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}

\displaystyle\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{R}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}=\partial_{\theta}^{k+1}\mathcal{B}^{-1}\mathcal{R}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}-\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathcal{T}_{0}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{R}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}

\displaystyle=\partial_{\theta}^{k+1}\mathcal{B}^{-1}\mathcal{R}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{B}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}-\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathcal{T}_{0}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{R}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{B}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}

\displaystyle=\partial_{\theta}^{k+1}\mathcal{B}^{-1}\mathcal{R}\mathscr{B}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}-\partial_{\theta}^{k+1}\mathcal{B}^{-1}\mathcal{R}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}\mathscr{B}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}-\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathcal{T}_{0}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{R}\mathscr{B}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}+\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathcal{T}_{0}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{R}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}\mathscr{B}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}.

(6.222)

Hence

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{R}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\theta}^{k+1}\mathcal{B}^{-1}\mathcal{R}\mathscr{B}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\partial_{\theta}^{k+1}\mathcal{B}^{-1}\mathcal{R}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}\mathscr{B}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathcal{T}_{0}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{R}\mathscr{B}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathcal{T}_{0}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{R}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}\mathscr{B}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.223)

Recall that from Proposition
6.1
that
\mathcal{R}
is an integral operator of kernel
J
and therefore direct computations give

\big(\mathcal{B}^{-1}\mathcal{R}\mathscr{B}\rho\big)(\varphi,\theta)=\int_{\mathbb{T}}\rho(\varphi,\eta)\widehat{J}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)d\eta,

(6.224)

with

\widehat{J}(\varphi,\theta,\eta):=J\big(\varphi,\theta+\widehat{\beta}(\varphi,\theta),\eta+\widehat{\beta}(\varphi,\eta)\big).

(6.225)

Combining (
6.32
) and (
6.5
), one gets

\widehat{J}(-\varphi,-\theta,-\eta)=\widehat{J}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)\in\mathbb{R}.

(6.226)

Using the composition law and (
6.9
), we obtain

\max_{k\in\{0,1,2\}}\sup_{\eta\in\mathbb{T}}\|(\partial_{\theta}^{k}\widehat{J})\big(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot,\eta+\centerdot\big)\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim 1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Thus, applying Lemma
4.4
-(ii) implies

\displaystyle\max_{k\in\{0,1\}}\|\partial_{\theta}^{k+1}\mathcal{B}^{-1}\mathcal{R}\mathscr{B}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\max_{k\in\{0,1,2\}}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\|(\partial_{\theta}^{k}\widehat{J})\big(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot,\eta+\centerdot\big)\|_{q,s+s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}d\eta

\displaystyle\lesssim 1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.227)

On the other hand we notice from (
6.224
) that we get the structure

\big(\mathcal{B}^{-1}\mathcal{R}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}\mathscr{B}\rho\big)(\varphi,\theta)=\int_{\mathbb{T}}\rho(\varphi,\eta)\widetilde{J}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)d\eta,

(6.228)

with

\widetilde{J}(\varphi,\theta,\eta):=\int_{\mathbb{T}}J\big(\varphi,\theta+\widehat{\beta}(\varphi,\theta),\eta^{\prime}\big)D_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}(\eta^{\prime}-\eta)d\eta^{\prime}.

(6.229)

Combining (
6.32
), (
6.5
) and the change of variables
\eta^{\prime}\mapsto-\eta^{\prime}
, one finds

\widetilde{J}(-\varphi,-\theta,-\eta)=\widetilde{J}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)\in\mathbb{R}.

(6.230)

Using the change of variables
\eta^{\prime}\mapsto\eta^{\prime}+\theta
yields

\widetilde{J}(\varphi,\theta,\eta+\theta):=\int_{\mathbb{T}}J\big(\varphi,\theta+\widehat{\beta}(\varphi,\theta),\eta^{\prime}+\theta\big)D_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}(\eta^{\prime}-\eta)d\eta^{\prime}.

Then by the composition law, we infer

\max_{k\in\{0,1,2\}}\sup_{\eta\in\mathbb{T}}\|(\partial_{\theta}^{k}\widetilde{J})(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot,\eta+\centerdot)\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim 1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Consequently, we find in view of Lemma
4.4

\displaystyle\max_{k\in\{0,1\}}\|\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathcal{B}^{-1}\mathcal{R}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}\mathscr{B}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\max_{k\in\{0,1,2\}}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\|(\partial_{\theta}^{k}\widetilde{J})\big(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot,\eta+\centerdot\big)\|_{q,s+s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}d\eta

\displaystyle\lesssim 1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.231)

If wet set

\mathcal{S}_{3}=\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{R}\mathscr{B}\quad\textnormal{or}\quad\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{R}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}\mathscr{B},

then using (
6.190
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{0}\mathcal{S}_{3}\rho

\displaystyle=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{S}_{0}}\big\langle\mathcal{S}_{3}\rho,\big(\mathcal{B}-\textnormal{Id}\big)g_{m}\big\rangle_{L_{\theta}^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\mathscr{B}^{-1}e_{m}

\displaystyle=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{S}_{0}}\big\langle\rho,\mathcal{S}_{3}^{\star}\big(\mathcal{B}-\textnormal{Id}\big)g_{m}\big\rangle_{L_{\theta}^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\mathscr{B}^{-1}e_{m},

with
\mathcal{S}_{3}^{\star}
is the adjoint of
\mathcal{S}_{3}
given by

\mathcal{S}_{3}^{\star}=-\mathcal{B}^{-1}\mathcal{R}^{\star}\partial_{\theta}\quad\textnormal{or}\quad-\mathcal{B}^{-1}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}\mathcal{R}^{\star}\partial_{\theta},

(6.232)

and
\mathcal{R}^{\star}
the adjoint of
\mathcal{R}
which is an integral operator with kernel

J^{\star}(\varphi,\theta,\eta):=\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{3}\sum_{k=1}^{d}g_{k,k^{\prime}}(\varphi,\theta)\chi_{k,k^{\prime}}(\varphi,\eta),

(6.233)

where we use the notations of the proof of Proposition
6.1
. Notice that similarly to (
6.9
) and (
6.5
), the kernel
J^{\star}
satisfies

\max_{k\in\{0,1,2\}}\sup_{\eta\in\mathbb{T}}\|(\partial_{\theta}^{k}J^{\star})(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot,\eta+\centerdot)\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim 1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

(6.234)

and

J^{\star}(-\varphi,-\theta,-\eta)=J^{\star}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)\in\mathbb{R}.

(6.235)

Now, we have the integral representation

\displaystyle\big(\mathcal{T}_{0}\mathcal{S}_{3}\rho\big)(\varphi,\theta)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}}\mathcal{K}_{4}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)\rho(\varphi,\eta)d\eta,

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{4}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{S}_{0}}\big(\mathcal{S}_{3}^{\star}\big(\mathcal{B}-\textnormal{Id}\big)g_{m}\big)(\varphi,\eta)\big(\mathscr{B}^{-1}e_{m}\big)(\varphi,\theta).

Then by virtue of (
6.174
), (
6.32
), (
6.214
) and (
6.235
) we obtain

\mathcal{K}_{4}(-\varphi,-\theta,-\eta)=-\mathcal{K}_{4}(\varphi,\theta,\eta)\in\mathbb{R}.

(6.236)

Applying Lemma
4.4
combined with the law products, we get for all
k\in\{0,1\}

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathcal{T}_{0}\mathcal{S}_{3}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{T}}\|(\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathcal{K}_{4})(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot,\eta+\centerdot)\|_{q,s+s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}d\eta

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{m\in\mathbb{S}_{0}}\Big(\|\mathcal{S}_{3}^{\star}\big(\mathcal{B}-\textnormal{Id}\big)g_{m}\|_{q,s+s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\mathscr{B}^{-1}e_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}+k}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\mathcal{S}_{3}^{\star}\big(\mathcal{B}-\textnormal{Id}\big)g_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\mathscr{B}^{-1}e_{m}\|_{q,s+s_{0}+k}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\Big).

Consequently, using Lemma
4.4
and (
6.234
), we get

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{R}^{\star}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{T}}\|J^{\star}(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot,\eta+\centerdot)\|_{q,s+s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Applying (
6.27
), Lemma
4.3
and the previous estimate implies

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{S}_{3}^{\star}\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathcal{R}^{\star}\partial_{\theta}\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\mathcal{R}^{\star}\partial_{\theta}\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)+\|\mathcal{R}^{\star}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\mathcal{R}^{\star}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\rho\|_{q,s+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\rho\|_{q,s+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Thus

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{S}_{3}^{\star}\big(\mathcal{B}-\textnormal{Id}\big)g_{m}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|g_{m}\|_{q,s+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|g_{m}\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Hence

\max_{k\in\{0,1\}}\|\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathcal{T}_{0}\mathcal{S}_{3}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim 1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.237)

Putting together (
6.3.1
), (
6.3.1
), (
6.3.1
) and (
6.237
) allows to get

\displaystyle\max_{k\in\{0,1\}}\varepsilon\big\|\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{R}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}\big\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right).

(6.238)

We now move to the estimate of the difference. From (
6.3.1
), one has

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}\big(\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{R}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}\big)

\displaystyle\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant\|\Delta_{12}\big(\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{B}^{-1}\mathcal{R}\mathscr{B}\big)\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\Delta_{12}\big(\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{B}^{-1}\mathcal{R}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}\mathscr{B}\big)\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle+\|\Delta_{12}\big(\mathcal{T}_{0}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{R}\mathscr{B}\big)\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\Delta_{12}\big(\mathcal{T}_{0}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{R}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}\mathscr{B}\big)\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.239)

Combining Lemma
4.4
with Taylor Formula, (
6.224
), (
6.225
), (
6.10
) and (
6.31
) one obtains

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}\big(\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{B}^{-1}\mathcal{R}\mathscr{B}\big)\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{T}}\|\Delta_{12}(\partial_{\theta}\widehat{J})(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot,\eta+\centerdot)\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}d\eta

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.240)

In the same spirit, (
6.228
) and (
6.229
) give

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}\big(\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{B}^{-1}\mathcal{R}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}\mathscr{B}\big)\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{T}}\|\Delta_{12}(\partial_{\theta}\widetilde{J})(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot,\eta+\centerdot)\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}d\eta

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.241)

According to the structure of
J^{\star}
detailed in (
6.233
) one can check that
J^{\star}
satisfies similar estimates as (
6.10
). Then using (
6.31
), one finds in a similar way to (
6.206
),

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}(\mathcal{T}_{0}\mathcal{S}_{3})\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{T}}\|\Delta_{12}\mathcal{K}_{4}(\ast,\cdot,\centerdot,\eta+\centerdot)\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}d\eta

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.242)

Hence, putting together, (
6.3.1
), (
6.3.1
), (
6.3.1
) and (
6.3.1
) gives

\varepsilon\|\Delta_{12}\big(\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{R}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}\big)\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\sigma_{3}}.

(6.243)

On the other hand, gathering (
6.3.1
), (
6.153
), (
6.193
), (
6.211
), (
6.215
), (
6.236
), (
6.226
) and (
6.230
) together with Lemma
4.4
, we find that
\mathscr{R}_{0}
is a real and reversible Toeplitz in time integral operator.

In addition, (
6.3.1
), (
6.3.1
), (
6.198
), (
6.3.1
) and (
6.238
) give (
6.185
).

Furthermore, (
6.3.1
), (
6.3.1
), (
6.207
), (
6.221
) and (
6.243
) imply (
6.186
).

(iv)
Using Lemma
4.3
together with (
6.181
), (
5.26
), (
6.26
) and (
6.178
), one obtains for all
s\in[s_{0},S]

\displaystyle\|\mathscr{L}_{0}\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\big(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+c_{i_{0}}\partial_{\theta}+\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{K}_{\lambda}\ast\cdot)\big)\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\mathscr{R}_{0}\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\rho\|_{q,s+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\mathscr{R}_{0}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\mathscr{R}_{0}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\rho\|_{q,s+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

This ends the proof of Proposition
6.4
.
∎

6.3.2
KAM reduction of the remainder term

The goal of this section is to conjugate
\mathscr{L}_{0}
defined in Proposition
6.4
to a diagonal operator, up to a fast decaying small remainder. This will be achieved through a standard KAM reducibility techniques in the spirit of Proposition
6.2
but well-adapted to the operators setting. This will be implemented by taking advantage of the exterior parameters which are restricted to a suitable Cantor set that prevents the resonances in the second Melnikov assumption. Notice that one gets from this study some estimates on the distribution of the eigenvalues and their stability with respect to the torus parametrization. This is considered as the key step not only to get an approximate inverse but also to achieve Nash-Moser scheme with a final massive Cantor set. The main result of this section reads as follows.

Proposition 6.5
.

Let
(\gamma,q,d,\tau_{1},\tau_{2},s_{0},s_{l},\overline{s}_{l},\overline{\mu}_{2},S)
satisfy (
4.1
), (
4.2
) and (
6.3
). For any
(\mu_{2},s_{h})
satisfying

\displaystyle\mu_{2}\geqslant\overline{\mu}_{2}+2\tau_{2}q+2\tau_{2}\quad\textnormal{and}\quad s_{h}\geqslant\frac{3}{2}\mu_{2}+\overline{s}_{l}+1,

(6.244)

there exist
\varepsilon_{0}\in(0,1)
and
\sigma_{4}=\sigma_{4}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2},q,d)\geqslant\sigma_{3}
, with
\sigma_{3}
defined in Proposition
\ref{projection in the normal directions},
such that if

\varepsilon\gamma^{-2-q}N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}\leqslant\varepsilon_{0}\quad\textnormal{and}\quad\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s_{h}+\sigma_{4}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant 1,

(6.245)

then the following assertions hold true.

(i)

There exists a family of invertible linear operator
\Phi_{\infty}:\mathcal{O}\to\mathcal{L}\big(H_{\perp}^{s}\big)
satisfying the estimates

\forall s\in[s_{0},S],\quad\mbox{ }\|\Phi_{\infty}^{\pm 1}\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\|\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\varepsilon\gamma^{-2}\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{4}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.246)

There exists a diagonal operator
\mathscr{L}_{\infty}=\mathscr{L}_{\infty}(\lambda,\omega,i_{0})
taking the form

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}+\mathscr{D}_{\infty}

where
\mathscr{D}_{\infty}=\mathscr{D}_{\infty}(\lambda,\omega,i_{0})
is a reversible Fourier multiplier operator given by,

\forall(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c},\quad\mathscr{D}_{\infty}\mathbf{e}_{l,j}={\rm i}\mu_{j}^{\infty}\,\mathbf{e}_{l,j},

with

\forall j\in\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c},\quad\mu_{j}^{\infty}(\lambda,\omega,i_{0})=\mu_{j}^{0}(\lambda,\omega,i_{0})+r_{j}^{\infty}(\lambda,\omega,i_{0}),\quad\|r_{j}^{\infty}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}

(6.247)

and

\displaystyle\sup_{j\in\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c}}|j|\|r_{j}^{\infty}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1},

(6.248)

such that in the Cantor set

\displaystyle\mathscr{O}_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}(i_{0}):=\bigcap_{\underset{|l|\leqslant N_{n}}{(l,j,j_{0})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times(\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c})^{2}}\atop(l,j)\neq(0,j_{0})}\Big\{

\displaystyle(\lambda,\omega)\in\mathcal{O}_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0}),\big|\omega\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty}(\lambda,\omega,i_{0})-\mu_{j_{0}}^{\infty}(\lambda,\omega,i_{0})\big|>\tfrac{2\gamma\langle j-j_{0}\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{2}}}\Big\}

we have

\displaystyle\Phi_{\infty}^{-1}\mathscr{L}_{0}\Phi_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{L}_{\infty}+\mathtt{E}^{2}_{n},

and the linear operator
\mathtt{E}_{n}^{2}
satisfies the estimate

\|\mathtt{E}^{2}_{n}\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-2}N_{0}^{{\mu}_{2}}N_{n+1}^{-\mu_{2}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.249)

Notice that the Cantor set
\mathcal{O}_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0})
was introduced in Proposition
\ref{reduction of the transport part},
the operator
\mathscr{L}_{0}
and the frequencies
\big(\mu_{j}^{0}(\lambda,\omega,i_{0})\big)_{j\in\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c}}
were stated in Proposition
\ref{projection in the normal directions}.

(ii)

Given two tori
i_{1}
and
i_{2}
both satisfying (
6.245
), then

\forall j\in\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c},\quad\|\Delta_{12}r_{j}^{\infty}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\sigma_{4}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

(6.250)

and

\forall j\in\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c},\quad\|\Delta_{12}\mu_{j}^{\infty}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}|j|\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\sigma_{4}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.251)

Proof.

(i)

We shall introduce the quantity

\delta_{0}(s):=\gamma^{-1}\|\mathscr{R}_{0}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}},

where
\mathscr{R}_{0}
is the remainder seen in Proposition
6.4
. By applying (
6.181
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\delta_{0}(s)\leqslant C\varepsilon\gamma^{-2}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right).

(6.252)

Therefore with the notation of (
6.244
), (
6.245
) and the fact that
\sigma_{4}\geqslant\sigma_{3}
we obtain

\displaystyle N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}\delta_{0}(s_{h})

\displaystyle\leqslant CN_{0}^{\mu_{2}}\varepsilon\gamma^{-2}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\varepsilon_{0}.

(6.253)

\blacktriangleright

KAM step.
Recall from Proposition
6.4
that in the Cantor set
\mathcal{O}_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0})
one has

\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\omega}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}=\mathscr{L}_{0}+\mathtt{E}_{n}^{1},

where the operator
\mathscr{L}_{0}
has the following structure

\mathscr{L}_{0}=\big(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+\mathscr{D}_{0}\big)\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}+\mathscr{R}_{0},

(6.254)

with
\mathscr{D}_{0}
a diagonal operator of pure imaginary spectrum and
\mathscr{R}_{0}
a real and reversible Toeplitz in time operator of zero order satisfying
\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{R}_{0}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}=\mathscr{R}_{0}.
Similarly to the reduction of the transport part, we shall first expose a typical step of the iteration process of the KAM scheme whose goal is to reduce to a diagonal part
\mathscr{R}_{0}
. Notice that the scheme is flexible and has been used in the literature to deal with various equations. Assume that we have a linear operator
\mathscr{L}
taking the following form in restriction to some Cantor set
\mathscr{O}
one has

\mathscr{L}=\big(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+\mathscr{D}\big)\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}+\mathscr{R},

where
\mathscr{D}
is real and reversible diagonal Toeplitz in time operator, that is,

\displaystyle\mathscr{D}\mathbf{e}_{l,j}=i\mu_{j}(\lambda,\omega)\,\mathbf{e}_{l,j}\quad\hbox{and}\quad\mu_{-j}(\lambda,\omega)=-\mu_{j}(\lambda,\omega).

(6.255)

The operator
\mathscr{R}
is assumed to be a real and reversible Toeplitz in time operator of zero order satisfying
\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{R}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}=\mathscr{R}.

Consider a linear invertible transformation close to the identity

\Phi=\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}+\Psi:\mathcal{O}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}(H_{\perp}^{s}),

where
\Psi
is small and depends on
\mathscr{R}
.
Then straightforward calculus show that in
\mathscr{O}

\displaystyle\Phi^{-1}\mathscr{L}\Phi

\displaystyle=\Phi^{-1}\Big(\Phi\left(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+\mathscr{D}\right)\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}+\left[\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}+\mathscr{D},\Psi\right]+\mathscr{R}+\mathscr{R}\Psi\Big)

\displaystyle=\big(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+\mathscr{D}\big)\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}+\Phi^{-1}\Big(\big[\big(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+\mathscr{D}\big)\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp},\Psi\big]+P_{N}\mathscr{R}+P_{N}^{\perp}\mathscr{R}+\mathscr{R}\Psi\Big),

where the projector
P_{N}
was defined in (
4.11
).
The main idea consists in replacing the remainder
\mathscr{R}
with another quadratic one up to a diagonal part and provided that the parameters
(\lambda,\omega)
belongs to a Cantor set connected to non-resonance conditions associated to the
homological equation
. Iterating this scheme will generate new remainders which become smaller and smaller up to new contributions on the diagonal part and with more extraction on the parameters. Then by passing to the limit we expect to diagonalize completely the operators provided that the parameters belong to a limit Cantor set. Notice that the Cantor set should be truncated in the time mode in order to get a stability form required later in Nash-Moser scheme and during the measure of the final Cantor set. This will induce a diagonalization up to small fast decaying remainders modeled by the operators
\mathtt{E}^{2}_{n}
in
Proposition
6.5
.
Now the first step is to impose the following homological equation,

\big[\big(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+\mathscr{D}\big)\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp},\Psi\big]+P_{N}\mathscr{R}=\lfloor P_{N}\mathscr{R}\rfloor,

(6.256)

where
\lfloor P_{N}\mathscr{R}\rfloor
is the diagonal part of the operator
\mathscr{R}
. We emphasize that the notation
\lfloor\mathcal{R}\rfloor
with a general operator
\mathcal{R}
is defined as follows, for all
(l_{0},j_{0})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c}
,

\mathcal{R}{\bf e}_{l_{0},j_{0}}=\sum_{(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c}}\mathcal{R}_{l_{0},j_{0}}^{l,j}{\bf e}_{l,j}\Longrightarrow\lfloor\mathcal{R}\rfloor{\bf e}_{l_{0},j_{0}}=\mathcal{R}_{l_{0},j_{0}}^{l_{0},j_{0}}{\bf e}_{l_{0},j_{0}}=\big\langle\mathcal{R}{\bf e}_{l_{0},j_{0}},{\bf e}_{l_{0},j_{0}}\big\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d+1})}\,{\bf e}_{l_{0},j_{0}}.

(6.257)

Remind the notation
{\bf e}_{l_{0},j_{0}}(\varphi,\theta)=e^{{\rm i}(l_{0}\cdot\varphi+j_{0}\theta)}
.
The Fourier coefficients of
\Psi
are defined through

\Psi{\bf e}_{l_{0},j_{0}}=\sum_{(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c}}\Psi_{l_{0},j_{0}}^{l,j}{\bf e}_{l,j},\quad\Psi_{l_{0},j_{0}}^{l,j}\in\mathbb{C}.

From direct computations based on the above Fourier decomposition, we infer

\big[\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp},\Psi\big]\mathbf{e}_{l_{0},j_{0}}={\rm i}\sum_{(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c}}\Psi_{l_{0},j_{0}}^{l,j}\,\,\omega\cdot(l-l_{0})\,\,\mathbf{e}_{l,j}

and using the diagonal structure of
\mathscr{D}
,

[\mathscr{D}_{0},\Psi]\mathbf{e}_{l_{0},j_{0}}={\rm i}\sum_{(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c}}\Psi_{l_{0},j_{0}}^{l,j}\left(\mu_{j}(\lambda,\omega)-\mu_{j_{0}}(\lambda,\omega)\right)\mathbf{e}_{l,j}.

By hypothesis,
\mathscr{R}
is a real and reversible Toeplitz in time operator. Hence its Fourier coefficients write in view of Proposition
4.1
,

\mathscr{R}_{l_{0},j_{0}}^{l,j}:={\rm i}\,r_{j_{0}}^{j}(\lambda,\omega,l_{0}-l)\in{\rm i}\,\mathbb{R}\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\mathscr{R}_{-l_{0},-j_{0}}^{-l,-j}=-\mathscr{R}_{l_{0},j_{0}}^{l,j}.

(6.258)

Consequently
\Psi
is a solution of (
6.256
) if and only if

\Psi{\bf e}_{l_{0},j_{0}}=\sum_{|l-l_{0}|\leqslant N\atop|j-j_{0}|\leqslant N}\Psi_{l_{0},j_{0}}^{l,j}{\bf e}_{l,j}

and

\Psi_{l_{0},j_{0}}^{l,j}\Big(\omega\cdot(l-l_{0})+\mu_{j}(\lambda,\omega)-\mu_{j_{0}}(\lambda,\omega)\Big)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}-r_{j_{0}}^{j}(\lambda,\omega,l_{0}-l)&\mbox{if }(l,j)\neq(l_{0},j_{0})\\
0&\mbox{if }(l,j)=(l_{0},j_{0}).\end{array}\right.

In particular, we get that
\Psi
is a Toeplitz in time operator with
\Psi_{j_{0}}^{j}(l_{0}-l):=\Psi_{l_{0},j_{0}}^{l,j}
. Moreover, for
(l,j,j_{0})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times(\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c})^{2}
with
|l|,|j-j_{0}|\leqslant N,
one obtains

\Psi_{j_{0}}^{j}(\lambda,\omega,l)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{-r_{j_{0}}^{j}(\lambda,\omega,l)}{\omega\cdot l+\mu_{j}(\lambda,\omega)-\mu_{j_{0}}(\lambda,\omega)}&\mbox{if }(l,j)\neq(0,j_{0})\\
0&\mbox{if }(l,j)=(0,j_{0}),\end{array}\right.

(6.259)

provided that the denominator is non zero. In addition, from
\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{R}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}=\mathscr{R},
one easily gets

\forall\,l\,\in\mathbb{Z}^{d},\,\forall\,j\,\,\hbox{or}\,\,\,j_{0}\in\mathbb{S}_{0},\quad r_{j_{0}}^{j}(\lambda,\omega,l)=0.

Therfore, we should impose the compatibility condition

\forall\,l\,\in\mathbb{Z}^{d},\,\forall\,j\,\,\hbox{or}\,\,\,j_{0}\in\mathbb{S}_{0},\quad\Psi_{j_{0}}^{j}(\lambda,\omega,l)=0.

This implies that
\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\Psi\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}=\Psi.

To justify the formula given by (
6.259
) we need to avoid resonances and restrict the parameters to the following open set according to the so-called second Melnikov condition,

\mathscr{O}_{+}^{\gamma}=\bigcap_{\underset{|l|\leqslant N}{(l,j,j_{0})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times({\mathbb{S}}_{0}^{c})^{2}}\atop(l,j)\neq(0,j_{0})}\left\{(\lambda,\omega)\in\mathscr{O}\quad\textnormal{s.t.}\quad|\omega\cdot l+\mu_{j}(\lambda,\omega)-\mu_{j_{0}}(\lambda,\omega)|>\tfrac{\gamma\langle j-j_{0}\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{2}}}\right\}.

In view of this restriction, the identity (
6.259
) is well defined and to extend
\Psi
to the whole set
\mathcal{O}
we shall use the cut-off function
\chi
of (
5.81
). We set

\Psi_{j_{0}}^{j}(\lambda,\omega,l)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}-\varrho_{j_{0}}^{j}(\lambda,\omega,l)\,\,r_{j_{0}}^{j}(\lambda,\omega,l),&\mbox{if }\quad(l,j)\neq(0,j_{0})\\
0,&\mbox{if }\quad(l,j)=(0,j_{0}),\end{array}\right.

(6.260)

with

\displaystyle\varrho_{j_{0}}^{j}(\lambda,\omega,l):=\frac{\chi\left((\omega\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{0}(\lambda,\omega)-\mu_{j_{0}}^{0}(\lambda,\omega))(\gamma\langle j-j_{0}\rangle)^{-1}\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{2}}\right)}{\omega\cdot l+\mu_{j}(\lambda,\omega)-\mu_{j_{0}}(\lambda,\omega)}\cdot

(6.261)

To simplify the notation, in the sequel we shall still write
\Psi
to denote this extension. Note that the extension (
6.260
) is smooth and in restriction to the Cantor set
\mathscr{O}_{+}^{\gamma}
coincides with
\Psi.

On the other hand, (
6.258
) and (
6.260
) imply that
\Psi_{j_{0}}^{j}(l)\in\mathbb{R}.
In addition, (
6.261
) combined with (
6.255
) give

\Psi_{-j_{0}}^{-j}(-l)=\Psi_{j_{0}}^{j}(l).

Consequently, in view of Proposition
4.1
, we deduce that
\Psi
is a real and reversibility preserving operator.
Now consider,

\mathscr{D}_{+}=\mathscr{D}+\lfloor P_{N}\mathscr{R}\rfloor,\quad\quad\mathscr{R}_{+}=\Phi^{-1}\big(-\Psi\,\lfloor P_{N}\mathscr{R}\rfloor+P_{N}^{\perp}\mathscr{R}+\mathscr{R}\Psi\big)

(6.262)

and

\mathscr{L}_{+}:=\big(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+\mathscr{D}_{+}+\mathscr{R}_{+}\big)\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}.

Therefore, in restriction to the Cantor set
\mathscr{O}_{+}^{\gamma}
, we can write

\mathscr{L}_{+}=\Phi^{-1}\mathscr{L}\Phi.

Our next task is to estimate
\varrho_{j_{0}}^{j}
defined by (
6.261
). Notice that this quantity can be written in the following form

\displaystyle\varrho_{j_{0}}^{j}(\lambda,\omega,l)=a_{l,j,j_{0}}\widehat{\chi}\big(a_{l,j,j_{0}}A_{l,j,j_{0}}(\lambda,\omega)\big),\quad\widehat{\chi}(x)=\frac{\chi(x)}{x},

\displaystyle A_{l,j,j_{0}}(\lambda,\omega)=\omega\cdot l+\mu_{j}(\lambda,\omega)-\mu_{j_{0}}(\lambda,\omega),\quad a_{l,j,j_{0}}=(\gamma\langle j-j_{0}\rangle)^{-1}\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{2}},

(6.263)

where
\widehat{\chi}(x):=\frac{\chi(x)}{x}
is
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}
with bounded derivatives.
Assume now the following estimate

\displaystyle\forall\,(j,j_{0})\in(\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c})^{2},\quad\max_{|\alpha|\in\llbracket 0,q\rrbracket}\sup_{(\lambda,\omega)\in\mathcal{O}}\left|\partial_{\lambda,\omega}^{\alpha}\left(\mu_{j}(\lambda,\omega)-\mu_{j_{0}}(\lambda,\omega)\right)\right|\leqslant C\,|j-j_{0}|.

(6.264)

Then, we find

\displaystyle\forall\,(l,j,j_{0})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times(\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c})^{2},\quad\max_{q^{\prime}\in\llbracket 0,q\rrbracket}\|A_{l,j,j_{0}}\|_{q^{\prime}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant C\,\langle l,j-j_{0}\rangle.

(6.265)

In a similar way to (
6.46
), using Lemma
4.1
-(vi) and (
6.265
), we obtain

\displaystyle\forall q^{\prime}\in\llbracket 0,q\rrbracket,\quad\|\varrho_{j_{0}}^{j}(\ast,l)\|_{q^{\prime}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant C\gamma^{-(q^{\prime}+1)}\langle l,j-j_{0}\rangle^{\tau_{2}q^{\prime}+\tau_{2}+q^{\prime}}.

(6.266)

Similarly to (
6.48
), using Leibniz rule, we get

\displaystyle\|\Psi\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\gamma^{-1}\|P_{N}\mathscr{R}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s+\tau_{2}q+\tau_{2}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.267)

We also assume that the following smallness condition holds

\displaystyle\gamma^{-1}\|\mathscr{R}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s_{0}+\tau_{2}q+\tau_{2}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant C\varepsilon_{0}.

(6.268)

Hence, by virtue of (
6.267
), we get

\displaystyle\|\Psi\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\gamma^{-1}\|\mathscr{R}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s_{0}+\tau_{2}q+\tau_{2}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\varepsilon_{0}.

(6.269)

As a consequence, up to take
\varepsilon_{0}
small enough, the operator
\Phi
is invertible and

\Phi^{-1}=\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{n}\Psi^{n}:=\textnormal{Id}+\Sigma.

According to the law products in Lemma
4.1
, Lemma
4.3
, (
6.267
) and (
6.3.2
) one gets

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|\Sigma\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\displaystyle\|\Psi\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\left(1+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\left(C\|\Psi\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)^{n}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\displaystyle C\,\gamma^{-1}N^{\tau_{2}q+\tau_{2}}\|\mathscr{R}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.270)

Therefore, we conclude with the assumption (
6.268
) that
\Phi^{-1}
is satisfies the following estimate

\|\Phi^{-1}-\hbox{Id}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}{\leqslant}C\gamma^{-1}N^{\tau_{2}q+\tau_{2}}\|\mathscr{R}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.271)

From (
6.262
), we can write

\mathscr{R}_{+}=P_{N}^{\perp}\mathscr{R}+\Phi^{-1}\mathscr{R}\Psi-\Psi\,\lfloor P_{N}\mathscr{R}\rfloor+\Sigma\big(P_{N}^{\perp}\mathscr{R}-\Psi\,\lfloor P_{N}\mathscr{R}\rfloor\big).

Thus, by virtue of Lemma
4.3
and (
6.271
), we infer

\displaystyle\|\mathscr{R}_{+}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|P_{N}^{\perp}\mathscr{R}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+C\|\Sigma\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\left(\|P_{N}^{\perp}\mathscr{R}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\Psi\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\mathscr{R}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)

\displaystyle+C\left(1+\|\Sigma\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)\left(\|\Psi\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\mathscr{R}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\Psi\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\mathscr{R}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right).

(6.272)

By Lemma
4.3
, (
6.267
),(
6.3.2
) and (
6.271
), we get for all
S\geqslant\overline{s}\geqslant s\geqslant s_{0}
,

\|\mathscr{R}_{+}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant N^{s-\overline{s}}\|\mathscr{R}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,\overline{s}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+C\gamma^{-1}N^{\tau_{2}q+\tau_{2}}\|\mathscr{R}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\mathscr{R}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.273)

\blacktriangleright

Initialization
We shall verify that the assumptions (
6.264
) and (
6.268
) required along the KAM step to get the final form (
6.273
) are satisfied for
\mathscr{L}=\mathscr{L}_{0}
in (
6.254
). Indeed, (
6.264
) is an immediate consequence of Lemma
3.3
-(vi), that is

\displaystyle\exists C>0,\,\,\forall(j,j_{0})\in\mathbb{Z}^{2},\quad\max_{|\alpha|\in\llbracket 0,q\rrbracket}\sup_{\lambda\in(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1})}\left|\partial_{\lambda}^{\alpha}\left(\Omega_{j}(\lambda)-\Omega_{j_{0}}(\lambda)\right)\right|\leqslant C\,|j-j_{0}|.

(6.274)

Thus, applying (
6.179
) we obtain

\displaystyle\exists C>0,\,\,\forall(j,j_{0})\in\mathbb{Z}^{2},\quad\max_{|\alpha|\in\llbracket 0,q\rrbracket}\sup_{(\lambda,\omega)\in\mathcal{O}}\left|\partial_{\lambda,\omega}^{\alpha}\left(\mu_{j}^{0}(\lambda,\omega)-\mu_{j_{0}}^{0}(\lambda,\omega)\right)\right|\leqslant C\,|j-j_{0}|.

Concerning the second assumption (
6.268
), we may combine (
6.181
) and (
6.245
) to find

\displaystyle\gamma^{-1}\|\mathscr{R}_{0}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s_{0}+\tau_{2}q+\tau_{2}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\varepsilon\gamma^{-2}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s_{h}+\sigma_{4}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\varepsilon_{0}.

\blacktriangleright

KAM iteration
. Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
and consider a linear operator

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{m}:=\big(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+\mathscr{D}_{m}+\mathscr{R}_{m}\big)\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}

(6.275)

with
\mathscr{D}_{m}
a diagonal real reversible operator and
\mathscr{R}_{m}
a real and reversible Toeplitz in time operator of zero order satisfying
\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{R}_{m}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}=\mathscr{R}_{m}.
We assume that both assumptions (
6.264
) and (
6.268
) are satisfied for
\mathscr{D}_{m}
and
\mathscr{R}_{m}
. Remark that for
m=0
we take the operator
\mathscr{L}_{0}
defined in (
6.254
). Let
\Phi_{m}=\hbox{Id}+\Psi_{m}
be a linear invertible operator such that

\displaystyle\Phi_{m}^{-1}\mathscr{L}_{m}\Phi_{m}:=\big(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+\mathscr{D}_{m+1}+\mathscr{R}_{m+1}\big)\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp},

(6.276)

with
\Psi_{m}
satisfying the homological equation

\big[\big(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+\mathscr{D}_{m}\big)\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp},\Psi_{m}\big]+P_{N_{m}}\mathscr{R}_{m}=\lfloor P_{N_{m}}\mathscr{R}_{m}\rfloor.

Recall that
N_{m}
was defined in (
5.82
). The diagonal parts
(\mathscr{D}_{m})_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
and the remainders
(\mathscr{R}_{m})_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
