\displaystyle\lesssim N_{n}^{s-\overline{s}}\gamma^{-1}\|\rho\|_{\overline{s}+1+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.380)

(ii)
Let us define

\displaystyle\mathtt{T}_{\omega,n}:=\mathscr{B}_{\perp}\Phi_{\infty}\mathtt{T}_{n}\Phi_{\infty}^{-1}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1},

(6.381)

where the operators
\mathscr{B}_{\perp}
and
\Phi_{\infty}
are defined in Propositions
6.4
and
6.5
respectively. Notice that
\mathtt{T}_{\omega,n}
is defined in the whole range of parameters
\mathcal{O}.
Since the condition (
6.374
) is satisfied, then, both Propositions
6.2
and
6.5
apply and from (
6.159
) we obtain

\forall s\in[s_{0},S],\quad\|\mathtt{T}_{\omega,n}\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\|\Phi_{\infty}\mathtt{T}_{n}\Phi_{\infty}^{-1}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\Phi_{\infty}\mathtt{T}_{n}\Phi_{\infty}^{-1}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

By using (
6.246
) and (
6.374
), one gets

\forall s\in[s_{0},S],\quad\|\Phi_{\infty}\mathtt{T}_{n}\Phi_{\infty}^{-1}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\|\mathtt{T}_{n}\Phi_{\infty}^{-1}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\mathtt{T}_{n}\Phi_{\infty}^{-1}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Thus the point (i) of the current proposition implies

\forall s\geqslant s_{0},\quad\|\mathtt{T}_{n}\Phi_{\infty}^{-1}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\gamma^{-1}\|\Phi_{\infty}^{-1}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\rho\|_{q,s+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Applying (
6.246
) and (
6.159
) with (
6.374
) yields

\displaystyle\forall s\in[s_{0},S],\quad\|\Phi_{\infty}^{-1}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Putting together the preceding three estimates gives (
6.375
). Now combining Propositions
6.4
and
6.5
, we find that in the Cantor set
\mathcal{O}_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0})\cap\mathcal{O}_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}(i_{0})

the following decomposition holds

\displaystyle\Phi^{-1}_{\infty}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\omega}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}\Phi_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\Phi^{-1}_{\infty}\mathscr{L}_{0}\Phi_{\infty}+\Phi^{-1}_{\infty}\mathtt{E}_{n}^{1}\Phi_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{L}_{\infty}+\mathtt{E}_{n}^{2}+\Phi^{-1}_{\infty}\mathtt{E}_{n}^{1}\Phi_{\infty}.

It follows that in the Cantor set
\mathcal{O}_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0})\cap\mathscr{O}_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{2}}(i_{0})\cap\Lambda_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0})
one has by virtue of the identity (
6.4
)

\displaystyle\Phi^{-1}_{\infty}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\omega}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}\Phi_{\infty}\mathtt{T}_{n}

\displaystyle=\textnormal{Id}+\mathtt{E}_{n}^{3}+\mathtt{E}_{n}^{2}\mathtt{T}_{n}+\Phi^{-1}_{\infty}\mathtt{E}_{n}^{1}\Phi_{\infty}\mathtt{T}_{n},

which gives, using (
6.381
), the following identity in
\mathcal{G}_{n}(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2},i_{0})

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\omega}\mathtt{T}_{\omega,n}

\displaystyle=\textnormal{Id}+\mathscr{B}_{\perp}\Phi_{\infty}\big(\mathtt{E}_{n}^{3}+\mathtt{E}_{n}^{2}\mathtt{T}_{n}+\Phi^{-1}_{\infty}\mathtt{E}_{n}^{1}\Phi_{\infty}\mathtt{T}_{n}\big)\Phi^{-1}_{\infty}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}

\displaystyle:=\textnormal{Id}+\mathscr{B}_{\perp}\Phi_{\infty}\mathtt{E}_{n}^{4}\Phi^{-1}_{\infty}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}

\displaystyle:=\textnormal{Id}+\mathtt{E}_{n}.

(6.382)

The estimate of the first term of
\mathtt{E}_{n}^{4}
is given in (
6.4
). For the second term of
\mathtt{E}_{n}^{4}

we use (
6.249
) and (
6.377
) leading to

\displaystyle\|\mathtt{E}^{2}_{n}\mathtt{T}_{n}\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-2}N_{0}^{{\mu}_{2}}N_{n+1}^{-\mu_{2}}\|\mathtt{T}_{n}\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-3}N_{0}^{{\mu}_{2}}N_{n+1}^{-\mu_{2}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+1+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.383)

For the estimate of
\Phi^{-1}_{\infty}\mathtt{E}_{n}^{1}\Phi_{\infty}\mathtt{T}_{n}
, we combine (
6.246
), (
6.180
), (
6.377
) and (
6.374
) to get

\displaystyle\|\Phi^{-1}_{\infty}\mathtt{E}_{n}^{1}\Phi_{\infty}\mathtt{T}_{n}\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathtt{E}_{n}^{1}\Phi_{\infty}\mathtt{T}_{n}\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}N_{n+1}^{-\mu_{2}}\|\Phi_{\infty}\mathtt{T}_{n}\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}N_{n+1}^{-\mu_{2}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+2+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.384)

Putting together (
6.4
) and (
6.4
) and (
6.4
) we find

\displaystyle\|\mathtt{E}^{4}_{n}\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim N_{n}^{s_{0}-s}\gamma^{-1}\|\rho\|_{s+2+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\varepsilon\gamma^{-3}N_{0}^{{\mu}_{2}}N_{n+1}^{-\mu_{2}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+2+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.385)

Set
\Psi=\mathscr{B}_{\perp}\Phi_{\infty}
then from (
6.246
), (
6.159
) and (
6.374
) we deduce that

\displaystyle\forall s\in[s_{0},S],\quad\mbox{ }\|\Psi^{\pm 1}\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\|\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\varepsilon\gamma^{-2}\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.386)

Straightforward computations based on (
6.385
), (
6.386
) and (
6.374
) yields

\displaystyle\|\Psi\mathtt{E}^{4}_{n}\Psi^{-1}\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathtt{E}^{4}_{n}\Psi^{-1}\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{n}^{s_{0}-s}\gamma^{-1}\|\Psi^{-1}\rho\|_{s+2+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\varepsilon\gamma^{-3}N_{0}^{{\mu}_{2}}N_{n+1}^{-\mu_{2}}\|\Psi^{-1}\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+2+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{n}^{s_{0}-s}\gamma^{-1}\big(\|\rho\|_{q,s+2+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\varepsilon\gamma^{-2}\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+\varepsilon\gamma^{-3}N_{0}^{{\mu}_{2}}N_{n+1}^{-\mu_{2}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+2+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Consequently, taking
\sigma
large enough, we get

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\|\mathtt{E}_{n}\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim N_{n}^{s_{0}-s}\gamma^{-1}\big(\|\rho\|_{q,s+\sigma}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\varepsilon\gamma^{-2}\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+\sigma}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\big)+\varepsilon\gamma^{-3}N_{0}^{{\mu}_{2}}N_{n+1}^{-\mu_{2}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+\sigma}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(iii)
According to (
6.4
), one can write in the Cantor set
\mathcal{G}_{n}(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2},i_{0})

\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\omega}=\mathtt{T}_{\omega,n}^{-1}+\mathtt{E}_{n}\mathtt{T}_{\omega,n}^{-1}.

(6.387)

Gathering (
6.381
) and (
6.378
), one obtains in the Cantor set
\mathcal{G}_{n}(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2},i_{0})

\widehat{\mathtt{L}}_{\omega,n}:=\mathtt{T}_{\omega,n}^{-1}=\mathscr{B}_{\perp}\Phi_{\infty}\mathtt{L}_{n}\Phi_{\infty}^{-1}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}=\Psi\mathtt{L}_{n}\Psi^{-1}.

Hence, (
6.387
) can be rewritten

\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\omega}=\widehat{\mathtt{L}}_{\omega,n}+\widehat{\mathtt{R}}_{n}\quad\textnormal{with}\quad\widehat{\mathtt{R}}_{n}:=\mathtt{E}_{n}\widehat{\mathtt{L}}_{\omega,n}.

(6.388)

Putting together (
6.4
), (
6.386
) and (
6.374
), we obtain

\displaystyle\forall s\in[s_{0},S],\quad\|\widehat{\mathtt{L}}_{\omega,n}\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle=\|\Psi\mathtt{L}_{n}\Psi^{-1}\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathtt{L}_{n}\Psi^{-1}\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\varepsilon\gamma^{-2}\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\mathtt{L}_{n}\Psi^{-1}\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Psi^{-1}\rho\|_{q,s+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\varepsilon\gamma^{-2}\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\Psi^{-1}\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\rho\|_{q,s+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\varepsilon\gamma^{-2}\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.389)

Hence combining this estimate with (
6.4
) yields

\displaystyle\forall s\in[s_{0},S],\quad\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\|\widehat{\mathtt{R}}_{n}\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{n}^{s_{0}-s}\gamma^{-1}\left(\|\rho\|_{q,s+\sigma}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\varepsilon\gamma^{-2}\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+\sigma}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon\gamma^{-3}N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}N_{n+1}^{-\mu_{2}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+\sigma}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

This achieves the proof of the third point and the proof of the proposition is now complete.
∎

7
Proof of the main result

This section is devoted to the proof of Theorem
1.1
. For this aim we intend to implement Nash-Moser scheme in order to construct
zeros for the nonlinear functional
\mathcal{F}\big(i,\alpha,\lambda,\omega,\varepsilon\big)
defined in (
5.23
). We shall be able to capture the solutions when the parameters
(\lambda,\omega)
belong to a suitable final Cantor
set
\mathcal{G}_{\infty}^{\gamma}
obtained as the intersection of all the Cantor sets required during the steps of the scheme to invert the linearized operator. More precisely, we get a relatively smooth function

(\lambda,\omega)\in\mathcal{O}\mapsto U_{\infty}(\lambda,\omega)=\big(i_{\infty}(\lambda,\omega),\alpha_{\infty}(\lambda,\omega)\big)
such that

\forall(\lambda,\omega)\in\mathcal{G}_{\infty}^{\gamma},\quad\mathcal{F}\big(U_{\infty}(\lambda,\omega),\lambda,\omega,\varepsilon\big)=0.

To generate solutions to the initial Hamiltonian equation (
5.4
) we should adjust the parameters so that
\alpha_{\infty}(\lambda,\omega)=-\omega_{\textnormal{Eq}}(\lambda)
, where
\omega_{\textnormal{Eq}}
corresponds to the equilibrium frequency vector defined in (
3.32
). As a consequence, nontrivial solutions are constructed when the scalar parameter
\lambda
is selected in the final Cantor set

\mathcal{C}_{\infty}^{\varepsilon}=\Big\{\lambda\in(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1})\quad\textnormal{s.t.}\quad(\lambda,\omega(\lambda,\varepsilon))\in\mathcal{G}_{\infty}^{\gamma}\quad\textnormal{with}\quad\alpha_{\infty}(\lambda,\omega(\lambda,\varepsilon))=-\omega_{\textnormal{Eq}}(\lambda)\Big\}.

The measure of this set will be discussed in Section
7.2
.

7.1
Nash-Moser scheme

In this section we implement the Nash-Moser scheme, which is a modified Newton method implemented with a suitable Banach scales and through a frequency cut-off. Basically, it consists in a recursive construction of approximate solutions to the equation
\mathcal{F}\big(i,\alpha,\lambda,\omega,\varepsilon\big)=0
where the functional
\mathcal{F}
is defined in (
5.23
). At each step of this scheme, we need to construct an approximate inverse of the linearized operator at a state near the equilibrium by applying the reduction procedure developed in Section
6
. This enables to get the result of Theorem
5.1
with the suitable tame estimates associated to the final loss of regularity
\overline{\sigma}
that could be arranged to be large enough. We point out that
\overline{\sigma}
depends only on the shape of the Cantor set through the parameters
\tau_{1},\tau_{2},d
and on the non degeneracy of the equilibrium frequency through
q=1+q_{0}
, where
q_{0}
be defined in Lemma
3.5
. However,
\overline{\sigma}
is independent of the regularity of the solutions that we want to construct. Now, we shall fix the following parameters needed to implement Nash-Moser scheme and related to the geometry of the Cantor sets encoded in
\tau_{1},\tau_{2},d
fixed by (
4.2
) and to the parameter
q=q_{0}+1,

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcl}\overline{a}&=&\tau_{2}+2\\
\mu_{1}&=&3q(\tau_{2}+2)+6\overline{\sigma}+6\\
a_{1}&=&6q(\tau_{2}+2)+12\overline{\sigma}+15\\
a_{2}&=&3q(\tau_{2}+2)+6\overline{\sigma}+9\\
\mu_{2}&=&2q(\tau_{2}+2)+5\overline{\sigma}+7\\
s_{h}&=&s_{0}+4q(\tau_{2}+2)+9\overline{\sigma}+11\\
b_{1}&=&2s_{h}-s_{0}.\end{array}\right.

(7.1)

The numbers
a_{1}
and
a_{2}
will describe the rate of convergence for the regularity
s_{0}
and
s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}
, respectively. They appear in the statements
(\mathcal{P}1)_{n}
and
(\mathcal{P}2)_{n}
in the Proposition
7.1
. The parameter
\mu_{1}
controls the norm inflation at the high regularity index
b_{1}
and appears in
(\mathcal{P}3)_{n}.
As to the parameter
\overline{a}
, it is linked to the thickness of a suitable enlargement of the intermediate Cantor sets, needed to construct classical extensions of our approximate solutions. Finally, the numbers
\mu_{2}
and
s_{h}
corresponds to those already encountered before in the reduction of the linearized operator and are now fixed to their minimal required values. In particular, we recall that
\mu_{2}
corresponds to the rate of convergence of the error terms emerging in the almost reducibility of the linearized operator, for instance we refer to Theorem
5.1
.
We should emphasize that, by taking
\overline{\sigma}
large enough, the choice for
\mu_{2}
and
s_{h}
done in (
7.1
) enables to cover all the required assumptions in (
6.24
) and (
6.244
).
Another assumption that we need to fix is related to
\gamma,N_{0}
and
\varepsilon

0<a<\tfrac{1}{\mu_{2}+q+2},\quad\gamma:=\varepsilon^{a},\quad N_{0}:=\gamma^{-1}.

(7.2)

This constraint is required for the measuring the final Cantor set and to check that it is massive, for more details we refer to Proposition
7.2
.

We shall start with defining the finite dimensional subspaces where the approximate solutions are expected to live with controlled estimates. Consider the space,

\displaystyle E_{n}:=

\displaystyle\Big\{\mathfrak{I}=(\Theta,I,z)\quad\textnormal{s.t.}\quad\Theta=\Pi_{n}\Theta,\quad I=\Pi_{n}I\quad\textnormal{and}\quad z=\Pi_{n}z\Big\},

where
\Pi_{n}
is the projector defined by

f(\varphi,\theta)=\sum_{(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{Z}}f_{l,j}e^{{\rm i}(l\cdot\varphi+j\theta)}\quad\Rightarrow\quad\Pi_{n}f(\varphi,\theta)=\sum_{\langle l,j\rangle\leqslant N_{n}}f_{l,j}e^{{\rm i}(l\cdot\varphi+j\theta)},

where the sequence
(N_{n})
is defined in (
5.82
). We observe that the same definition applies without ambiguity when the functions depend only on
\varphi
such as the action and the angles unknowns. The main result of this section is to prove the following induction statement.

Proposition 7.1

(Nash-Moser)
.

Let
(\tau_{1},\tau_{2},q,d,s_{0})
satisfy (
4.1
) and (
4.2
). Consider the parameters fixed by (
7.1
) and (
7.2
). There exist
C_{\ast}>0
and
\varepsilon_{0}>0
such that for any
\varepsilon\in[0,\varepsilon_{0}]
we get for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
the following properties,

(\mathcal{P}1)_{n}

There exists a
q
-times differentiable function

W_{n}:\begin{array}[t]{rcl}\mathcal{O}&\rightarrow&E_{n-1}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d+1}\\
(\lambda,\omega)&\mapsto&\big(\mathfrak{I}_{n},\alpha_{n}-\omega,0\big)\end{array}

satisfying

W_{0}=0\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\hbox{for}\quad n\geqslant 1,\quad\,\|W_{n}\|_{q,s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant C_{\ast}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{q\overline{a}}.

By setting

U_{0}=\Big((\varphi,0,0),\omega,(\lambda,\omega)\Big)\quad\textnormal{and}\quad\hbox{for}\quad n\in\mathbb{N}^{*},\,U_{n}=U_{0}+W_{n}\quad\textnormal{and}\quad H_{n}=U_{n}-U_{n-1},

then

\forall s\in[s_{0},S],\,\|H_{1}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant\tfrac{1}{2}C_{\ast}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{q\overline{a}}\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\forall\,2\leqslant k\leqslant n,\,\|H_{k}\|_{q,s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant C_{\ast}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{k-1}^{-a_{2}}.

(\mathcal{P}2)_{n}

Define

i_{n}=(\varphi,0,0)+\mathfrak{I}_{n},\quad\gamma_{n}=\gamma(1+2^{-n})\in[\gamma,2\gamma].

The embedded torus
i_{n}
statisfies the reversibility condition

\mathfrak{S}i_{n}(\varphi)=i_{n}(-\varphi),

where the involution
\mathfrak{S}
is defined in (
5.14
).
Introduce

\mathcal{A}_{0}^{\gamma}=\mathcal{O}\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\mathcal{A}_{n+1}^{\gamma}=\mathcal{A}_{n}^{\gamma}\cap\mathcal{G}_{n}(\gamma_{n+1},\tau_{1},\tau_{2},i_{n}),

where
\mathcal{G}_{n}(\gamma_{n+1},\tau_{1},\tau_{2},i_{n})
is described in Proposition
6.6
and consider the open sets

\forall\mathtt{r}>0,\quad\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\mathtt{r}}:=\Big\{(\lambda,\omega)\in\mathcal{O}\quad\textnormal{s.t.}\quad{\mathtt{dist}}\big((\lambda,\omega),\mathcal{A}_{n}^{\gamma}\big)<\mathtt{r}N_{n}^{-\overline{a}}\Big\},

where
\displaystyle{\mathtt{dist}}(x,A)=\inf_{y\in A}\|x-y\|
. Then we have the following estimate

\|\mathcal{F}(U_{n})\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}\leqslant C_{\ast}\varepsilon N_{n-1}^{-a_{1}}.

(\mathcal{P}3)_{n}

\|W_{n}\|_{q,b_{1}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant C_{\ast}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{n-1}^{\mu_{1}}.

Remark 7.1
.

Let
\mathrm{O}
be an open subset of
\mathcal{O}.
Since
\forall n\in\mathbb{N},\gamma_{n}\in[\gamma,2\gamma],
then the norms
\|\cdot\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}}
and
\|\cdot\|_{q,s}^{\gamma_{n},\mathrm{O}}.
are equivalent uniformly in
n.

Proof.

\bullet

Initialization :
By construction,
U_{0}=\Big((\varphi,0,0),\omega,(\lambda,\omega)\Big)
and the flat torus
i_{\textnormal{\tiny{flat}}}(\varphi)=(\varphi,0,0)
satisfies obviously the reversibility condition.
By (
5.23
), we have

\mathcal{F}(U_{0})=\varepsilon\left(\begin{array}[]{c}-\partial_{I}\mathcal{P}_{\varepsilon}((\varphi,0,0))\\
\partial_{\vartheta}\mathcal{P}_{\varepsilon}((\varphi,0,0))\\
-\partial_{\theta}\nabla_{z}\mathcal{P}_{\varepsilon}((\varphi,0,0))\end{array}\right).

Using Lemma
5.2
, we get

\forall s\geqslant 0,\quad\|\mathcal{F}(U_{0})\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant C_{\ast}\varepsilon,

(7.3)

up to take
C_{\ast}
large enough. The properties
(\mathcal{P}1)_{0},

(\mathcal{P}2)_{0}
and
(\mathcal{P}3)_{0}
then follow immediately since
N_{-1}=1
and
\mathrm{O}_{0}^{\gamma}=\mathcal{O}
and by setting
W_{0}=0.

\bullet

Induction step :
Given
n\in\mathbb{N},
assume that
(\mathcal{P}1)_{k},

(\mathcal{P}2)_{k}
and
(\mathcal{P}3)_{k}
are true for all
k\in\llbracket 0,n\rrbracket
and let us check them at the next order
n+1
. Introduce the linearized operator of
\mathcal{F}
at the state
(i_{n},\alpha_{n})

L_{n}:=L_{n}(\lambda,\omega):=d_{i,\alpha}\mathcal{F}(i_{n}\big(\lambda,\omega),\alpha_{n}(\lambda,\omega),(\lambda,\omega)\big).

In order to construct the next approximation
U_{n+1},
we need an approximate right inverse for
L_{n}.
Its construction was performed along the preceding sections and we refer to Theorem
5.1
for a precise statement. To apply this result and get some bounds on
U_{n+1}
we need to establish first some intermediate results connected to the smallness condition and to some Cantor set inclusions.

\blacktriangleright

Smallness/boundedness properties.
First of all, remark that the parameters conditions (
6.3
) are automatically satisfied by (
7.1
). Then, provided that the smallness assumption (
6.374
) is satisfied, Proposition
6.6
applies. It remains to check that (
6.374
) is satisfied. According to the first condition in (
7.2
) and choosing
\varepsilon
small enough, we can ensure

\varepsilon\gamma^{-2-q}N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}=\varepsilon^{1-a(\mu_{2}+q+2)}\leqslant\varepsilon_{0}

(7.4)

for some a priori fixed
\varepsilon_{0}>0.
Therefore the first assumption in (
6.374
) holds. We now turn to the second assumption. Since from (
7.1
)
b_{1}=2s_{h}-s_{0},
then by interpolation inequality in Lemma
4.1
, we have

\|H_{n}\|_{q,s_{h}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\left(\|H_{n}\|_{q,s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\|H_{n}\|_{q,b_{1}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(7.5)

Besides, by using
(\mathcal{P}1)_{n}
, we find

\forall s\in[s_{0},S],\quad\|H_{1}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant\tfrac{1}{2}C_{\ast}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{q\overline{a}}\quad\textnormal{and}\quad\|H_{n}\|_{q,s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant C_{\ast}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{n-1}^{-a_{2}}.

(7.6)

Now
(\mathcal{P}3)_{n}
and
(\mathcal{P}3)_{n-1}
imply

\displaystyle\|H_{n}\|_{q,b_{1}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle=\|U_{n}-U_{n-1}\|_{q,b_{1}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle=\|W_{n}-W_{n-1}\|_{q,b_{1}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|W_{n}\|_{q,b_{1}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|W_{n-1}\|_{q,b_{1}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant 2C_{\ast}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{n-1}^{\mu_{1}}.

Putting together the foregoing estimates into (
7.5
) gives for
n\geqslant 2
,

\|H_{n}\|_{q,s_{h}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant CC_{\ast}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{n-1}^{\frac{1}{2}(\mu_{1}-a_{2})}

(7.7)

and for
n=1
,

\|H_{1}\|_{q,s_{h}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant\tfrac{1}{2}C_{\ast}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{q\overline{a}}.

(7.8)

Now from (
7.1
) we infer

a_{2}\geqslant\mu_{1}+2.

(7.9)

Thus, by (
7.2
) and Lemma
A.1
, we get for small
\varepsilon

\displaystyle\|W_{n}\|_{q,s_{h}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|H_{1}\|_{q,s_{h}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\sum_{k=2}^{n}\|H_{k}\|_{q,s_{h}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\tfrac{1}{2}C_{\ast}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{q\overline{a}}+CC_{\ast}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\sum_{k=0}^{n}N_{k}^{-1}

\displaystyle\leqslant\tfrac{1}{2}C_{\ast}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{q\overline{a}}+CN_{0}^{-1}C_{\ast}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{\ast}\varepsilon^{1-a(1+q\overline{a})}.

One can check from (
7.1
) and (
7.2
) that

a\leqslant\tfrac{1}{2(1+q\overline{a})}

(7.10)

and therefore, by choosing
\varepsilon
small enough and since
\overline{\sigma}\geqslant\sigma
, we get

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{I}_{n}\|_{q,s_{h}+\sigma}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant\|W_{n}\|_{q,s_{h}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{\ast}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leqslant 1.

As we have already mentioned, the parameter
\overline{\sigma}
is the final loss of regularity constructed in
Theorem
5.1
and depending only on the parameters
\tau_{1},\tau_{2},q
and
d
but it is independent of the state and the regularity. Hence it can be selected large enough such that
s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}\geqslant\overline{s}_{h}+\sigma_{4}
where
\overline{s}_{h}
and
\sigma_{4}
are respectively defined in (
6.24
) and Proposition
6.5
. Then using (
7.6
) and Sobolev embeddings, we obtain

\forall n\geqslant 2,\quad\|H_{n}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\sigma_{4}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant C_{\ast}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{n-1}^{-a_{2}}.

(7.11)

\blacktriangleright

Set inclusions.
From the previous point, Propositions
6.2
,
6.5
and
6.6
apply and allow us to perform the reduction of the linearized operator in the normal directions at the current step. Therefore, the sets
\mathcal{A}_{k}^{\gamma}
for all
k\leqslant n+1
are well-defined. We shall now prove the following inclusions needed later to establish suitable estimates for the extensions.

\mathcal{A}_{n+1}^{\gamma}\subset\mathrm{O}_{n+1}^{2\gamma}\subset\left(\mathcal{A}_{n+1}^{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\cap\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}\right).

(7.12)

Notice that the first inclusion is obvious by construction since
\mathrm{O}_{n+1}^{2\gamma}
is an enlargement of
\mathcal{A}_{n+1}^{\gamma}.
It remains to prove the last inclusion.
We have the inclusion

\forall k\in\llbracket 0,n\rrbracket,\quad\mathrm{O}_{k+1}^{2\gamma}\subset\mathrm{O}_{k}^{\gamma}.

(7.13)

Indeed, since by construction
\mathcal{A}_{k+1}^{\gamma}\subset\mathcal{A}_{k}^{\gamma}
then taking
(\lambda,\omega)\in\mathrm{O}_{k+1}^{2\gamma}
we have the following estimates

\displaystyle{\mathtt{dist}}\big((\lambda,\omega),\mathcal{A}_{k}^{\gamma}\big)

\displaystyle\leqslant{\mathtt{dist}}\big((\lambda,\omega),\mathcal{A}_{k+1}^{\gamma}\big)

\displaystyle<2\gamma N_{k+1}^{-\overline{a}}=2\gamma N_{k}^{-\overline{a}}N_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}\overline{a}}

\displaystyle<\gamma N_{k}^{-\overline{a}},

provided that
2N_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}\overline{a}}<1,
which is true up to take
N_{0}
large enough, that is in view of (
7.2
) for
\varepsilon
small enough. We shall now prove by induction in
k
that

\forall k\in\llbracket 0,n+1\rrbracket,\quad\mathrm{O}_{k}^{2\gamma}\subset\mathcal{A}_{k}^{\frac{\gamma}{2}}.

(7.14)

The case
k=0
is trivial since
\mathrm{O}_{0}^{2\gamma}=\mathcal{O}=\mathcal{A}_{0}^{\frac{\gamma}{2}}.
Let us now assume that (
7.14
) is true for the index
k\in\llbracket 0,n\rrbracket
and let us check it at the next order. From (
7.13
) and (
7.14
), we obtain

\mathrm{O}_{k+1}^{2\gamma}\subset\mathrm{O}_{k}^{\gamma}\subset\mathrm{O}_{k}^{2\gamma}\subset\mathcal{A}_{k}^{\frac{\gamma}{2}}.

Therefore, we are left to check that

\mathrm{O}_{k+1}^{2\gamma}\subset\mathcal{G}_{k}\Big(\frac{\gamma_{k+1}}{2},\tau_{1},\tau_{2},i_{k}\Big).

Let
(\lambda,\omega)\in\mathcal{O}_{k+1}^{2\gamma},
then by construction, there exists
(\lambda^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})\in\mathcal{A}_{k+1}^{\gamma}
such that

\mathtt{dist}\left((\lambda,\omega),(\lambda^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})\right)<2\gamma N_{k+1}^{-\overline{a}}.

Hence, for all
(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c}
with
|l|\leqslant N_{k},
we have by left triangle and Cauchy-Schwarz inequalities together with
(\lambda^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})\in\Lambda_{\infty,k}^{\gamma_{k+1},\tau_{1}}(i_{k})

\displaystyle\left|\omega\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty}(\lambda,\omega,i_{k})\right|

\displaystyle\geqslant\left|\omega^{\prime}\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty}(\lambda^{\prime},\omega^{\prime},i_{k})\right|-|\omega-\omega^{\prime}||l|-\left|\mu_{j}^{\infty}(\lambda,\omega,i_{k})-\mu_{j}^{\infty}(\lambda^{\prime},\omega^{\prime},i_{k})\right|

\displaystyle>\tfrac{\gamma_{k+1}\langle j\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}-2\gamma N_{k}N_{k+1}^{-\overline{a}}-\left|\mu_{j}^{\infty}(\lambda,\omega,i_{k})-\mu_{j}^{\infty}(\lambda^{\prime},\omega^{\prime},i_{k})\right|

\displaystyle>\tfrac{\gamma_{k+1}\langle j\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}-2\gamma N_{k+1}^{1-\overline{a}}-\left|\mu_{j}^{\infty}(\lambda,\omega,i_{k})-\mu_{j}^{\infty}(\lambda^{\prime},\omega^{\prime},i_{k})\right|.

Using the Mean Value Theorem and the definition of
\mathrm{O}_{k+1}^{2\gamma}
yields

\displaystyle\left|\mu_{j}^{\infty}(\lambda,\omega,i_{k})-\mu_{j}^{\infty}(\lambda^{\prime},\omega^{\prime},i_{k})\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant|(\lambda,\omega)-(\lambda^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})|\gamma^{-1}\|\mu_{j}^{\infty}(i_{k})\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant 2N_{k+1}^{-\overline{a}}\|\mu_{j}^{\infty}(i_{k})\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

On the other hand,

\forall j\in\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c},\quad\|\mu_{j}^{\infty}(i_{k})\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant\|\mu_{j}^{\infty}(i_{k})-\Omega_{j}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\Omega_{j}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Using the asymptotic (
3.17
) and the smoothness of
\lambda\mapsto I_{j}(\lambda)K_{j}(\lambda)
for all
j\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
, one has

\|\Omega_{j}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant C|j|.

Since (
6.245
) is satisfied by the previous point, we can apply (
6.247
) and obtain

\forall j\in\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c},\quad\|\mu_{j}^{\infty}(i_{k})-\Omega_{j}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant C|j|.

Hence

\forall j\in\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c},\quad\|\mu_{j}^{\infty}(i_{k})\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant C|j|.

It follows that

\left|\mu_{j}^{\infty}(\lambda,\omega,i_{k})-\mu_{j}^{\infty}(\lambda^{\prime},\omega^{\prime},i_{k})\right|\leqslant C\langle j\rangle N_{k+1}^{-\overline{a}}\leqslant C\gamma\langle j\rangle N_{k+1}^{1-\overline{a}}.

Since
|l|\leqslant N_{k}\leqslant N_{k+1}
and
\gamma_{k+1}\geqslant\gamma
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\omega\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty}(\lambda,\omega,i_{k})\right|

\displaystyle\geqslant\tfrac{\gamma_{k+1}\langle j\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}-C\gamma\langle j\rangle N_{k+1}^{1-\overline{a}}

\displaystyle\geqslant\tfrac{\gamma_{k+1}\langle j\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}\left(1-CN_{k+1}^{\tau_{1}+1-\overline{a}}\right).

From (
7.1
) and (
4.2
) we infer

\overline{a}\geqslant\tau_{2}+2\geqslant\tau_{1}+2

(7.15)

and we can take
N_{0}
sufficiently large to ensure

CN_{k+1}^{\tau_{1}+1-\overline{a}}\leqslant CN_{0}^{-1}<\tfrac{1}{2},

allowing to finally get

\left|\omega\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty}(\lambda,\omega,i_{k})\right|>\tfrac{\gamma_{k+1}\langle j\rangle}{2\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}\cdot

This shows that,
(\lambda,\omega)\in\Lambda_{\infty,k}^{\frac{\gamma_{k+1}}{2},\tau_{1}}(i_{k}).
Let us now check that
(\lambda,\omega)\in\mathcal{O}_{\infty,k}^{\frac{\gamma_{k+1}}{2},\tau_{1}}(i_{k}).
For all
(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c}
with
|l|\leqslant N_{k},
we have by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality together with
(\lambda^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})\in\mathcal{O}_{\infty,k}^{\gamma_{k+1},\tau_{1}}(i_{k})

\displaystyle\left|\omega\cdot l+jc_{i_{k}}(\lambda,\omega)\right|

\displaystyle\geqslant\left|\omega^{\prime}\cdot l+jc_{i_{k}}(\lambda^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})\right|-|\omega-\omega^{\prime}||l|-|j|\left|c_{i_{k}}(\lambda,\omega)-c_{i_{k}}(\lambda^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})\right|

\displaystyle>\tfrac{4\gamma_{k+1}^{\upsilon}\langle j\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}-2\gamma N_{k+1}^{1-\overline{a}}-\langle j\rangle\left|c_{i_{k}}(\lambda,\omega)-c_{i_{k}}(\lambda^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})\right|.

Using the Mean Value Theorem and the definition of
\mathrm{O}_{k+1}^{2\gamma}
yields

\left|c_{i_{k}}(\lambda,\omega)-c_{i_{k}}(\lambda^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})\right|\leqslant CN_{k+1}^{-\overline{a}}\|c_{i_{k}}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Since (
6.25
) is satisfied by the previous point, we can apply (
6.26
) leading to

\displaystyle\|c_{i_{k}}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|c_{i_{k}}-V_{0}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|V_{0}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C.

Thus

\left|c_{i_{k}}(\lambda,\omega)-c_{i_{k}}(\lambda^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})\right|\leqslant C\gamma\gamma^{-1}N_{k+1}^{-\overline{a}}\leqslant C\gamma N_{k+1}^{1-\overline{a}}.

Therefore, we obtain from the definition of
\gamma_{k}
and
\upsilon\in(0,1)

\displaystyle\left|\omega\cdot l+c_{i_{k}}(\lambda,\omega)\right|

\displaystyle>\tfrac{4\gamma_{k+1}^{\upsilon}\langle j\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}-C\gamma\langle j\rangle N_{k+1}^{1-\overline{a}}

\displaystyle\geqslant\tfrac{4\gamma_{k+1}^{\upsilon}\langle j\rangle}{2^{\upsilon}\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}\left(2^{\upsilon}-C2^{\upsilon}N_{k+1}^{\tau_{1}+1-\overline{a}}\right).

By the choice of
\overline{a}
made in (
7.15
), we can ensure, up to take
N_{0}
sufficiently large,

CN_{k+1}^{\tau_{1}+1-\overline{a}}\leqslant CN_{0}^{-1}<1-2^{-\upsilon},

so that

\left|\omega\cdot l+jc_{i_{k}}(\lambda,\omega)\right|>\tfrac{4\gamma_{k+1}^{\upsilon}\langle j\rangle}{2^{\upsilon}\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}\cdot

As a consequence,
(\lambda,\omega)\in\mathcal{O}_{\infty,k}^{\frac{\gamma_{k+1}}{2},\tau_{1}}(i_{k}).
Let us now check that
(\lambda,\omega)\in\mathscr{O}_{\infty,k}^{\frac{\gamma_{k+1}}{2},\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}(i_{k}).
For all
(l,j,j_{0})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times(\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c})^{2}
with
|l|\leqslant N_{k},
we have by the triangle and Cauchy-Schwarz inequalities together with
(\lambda^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})\in\mathscr{O}_{\infty,k}^{\gamma_{k+1},\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}(i_{k})

\displaystyle\left|\omega\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty}(\lambda,\omega,i_{k})-\mu_{j_{0}}^{\infty}(\lambda,\omega,i_{k})\right|

\displaystyle\geqslant\left|\omega^{\prime}\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty}(\lambda^{\prime},\omega^{\prime},i_{k})-\mu_{j_{0}}^{\infty}(\lambda^{\prime},\omega^{\prime},i_{k})\right|-|\omega-\omega^{\prime}||l|

\displaystyle\quad-\left|\mu_{j}^{\infty}(\lambda,\omega,i_{k})-\mu_{j_{0}}^{\infty}(\lambda,\omega,i_{k})+\mu_{j_{0}}^{\infty}(\lambda^{\prime},\omega^{\prime},i_{k})-\mu_{j}^{\infty}(\lambda^{\prime},\omega^{\prime},i_{k})\right|

\displaystyle>\tfrac{2\gamma_{k+1}\langle j-j_{0}\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{2}}}-2\gamma N_{k+1}^{1-\overline{a}}

\displaystyle\quad-\left|\mu_{j}^{\infty}(\lambda,\omega,i_{k})-\mu_{j_{0}}^{\infty}(\lambda,\omega,i_{k})+\mu_{j_{0}}^{\infty}(\lambda^{\prime},\omega^{\prime},i_{k})-\mu_{j}^{\infty}(\lambda^{\prime},\omega^{\prime},i_{k})\right|.

We recall by virtue of Proposition
6.5
that

\mu_{j}^{\infty}(\lambda,\omega,i_{k})=\mu_{j}^{0}(\lambda,\omega,i_{k})+r_{j}^{\infty}(\lambda,\omega,i_{k}).

Thus

\displaystyle\left|\mu_{j}^{\infty}(\lambda,\omega,i_{k})-\mu_{j_{0}}^{\infty}(\lambda,\omega,i_{k})+\mu_{j_{0}}^{\infty}(\lambda^{\prime},\omega^{\prime},i_{k})-\mu_{j}^{\infty}(\lambda^{\prime},\omega^{\prime},i_{k})\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant\left|\mu_{j}^{0}(\lambda,\omega,i_{k})-\mu_{j_{0}}^{0}(\lambda,\omega,i_{k})+\mu_{j_{0}}^{0}(\lambda^{\prime},\omega^{\prime},i_{k})-\mu_{j}^{0}(\lambda^{\prime},\omega^{\prime},i_{k})\right|

\displaystyle\quad+\left|r_{j}^{\infty}(\lambda,\omega,i_{k})-r_{j}^{\infty}(\lambda^{\prime},\omega^{\prime},i_{k})\right|+\left|r_{j_{0}}^{\infty}(\lambda,\omega,i_{k})-r_{j_{0}}^{\infty}(\lambda^{\prime},\omega^{\prime},i_{k})\right|.

According to the Mean Value Theorem, (
6.264
) and the definition of
\mathrm{O}_{k+1}^{2\gamma}
we find

\left|\mu_{j}^{0}(\lambda,\omega,i_{k})-\mu_{j_{0}}^{0}(\lambda,\omega,i_{k})+\mu_{j_{0}}^{0}(\lambda^{\prime},\omega^{\prime},i_{k})-\mu_{j}^{0}(\lambda^{\prime},\omega^{\prime},i_{k})\right|\leqslant\gamma CN_{k+1}^{1-\overline{a}}\langle j-j_{0}\rangle.

Applying once again the Mean Value Theorem, (
6.248
), (
7.4
) and the definition of
\mathrm{O}_{n+1}^{2\gamma}
yields

\left|r_{j}^{\infty}(\lambda,\omega,i_{k})-r_{j}^{\infty}(\lambda^{\prime},\omega^{\prime},i_{k})\right|\leqslant C\gamma N_{k+1}^{-\overline{a}}\varepsilon\gamma^{-2}\leqslant\gamma CN_{k+1}^{1-\overline{a}}\langle j-j_{0}\rangle.

Putting together the foregoing estimates and the facts that
|l|\leqslant N_{k}
and
\gamma_{k+1}\geqslant\gamma
we infer

\displaystyle\left|\omega\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty}(\lambda,\omega,i_{k})-\mu_{j_{0}}^{\infty}(\lambda,\omega,i_{k})\right|

\displaystyle\geqslant\tfrac{\gamma_{k+1}\langle j-j_{0}\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{2}}}\left(2-CN_{k+1}^{\tau_{2}+1-\overline{a}}\right).

By virtue of (
7.15
) and taking

N_{0}
sufficiently large we get

CN_{n}^{\tau_{2}+1-\overline{a}}\leqslant CN_{0}^{-1}<1.

This implies

\left|\omega\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty}(\lambda,\omega,i_{k})-\mu_{j_{0}}^{\infty}(\lambda,\omega,i_{k})\right|>\tfrac{2\gamma_{k+1}\langle j-j_{0}\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{2}}}.

As a consequence, we deduce that
(\lambda,\omega)\in\mathscr{O}_{\infty,k}^{\frac{\gamma_{k+1}}{2},\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}(i_{n}).
Finally,
(\lambda,\omega)\in\mathcal{G}_{k}\big(\frac{\gamma_{k+1}}{2},\tau_{1},\tau_{2},i_{k}\big)
and therefore
(\lambda,\omega)\in\mathcal{A}_{k+1}^{\frac{\gamma}{2}}.
This achieves the induction proof of (
7.14
).

\blacktriangleright

Construction of the next approximation.
We are now going to construct the next approximation
U_{n+1}
by using a modified Nash-Moser scheme. The assumption (
6.374
) being satisfied, we can apply Theorem
5.1
with
L_{n}
and obtain the existence of an operator
\mathrm{T}_{n}:=\mathrm{T}_{n}(\lambda,\omega)
well-defined in the whole set of parameters
\mathcal{O}
and satisfying the following estimates

\forall s\in[s_{0},S],\quad\|\mathrm{T}_{n}\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\gamma^{-1}\left(\|\rho\|_{q,s+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\mathfrak{I}_{n}\|_{q,s+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)

(7.16)

and

\|\mathrm{T}_{n}\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\gamma^{-1}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(7.17)

Moreover, when it is restricted to the Cantor set
\mathcal{G}_{n}(\gamma_{n+1},\tau_{1},\tau_{2},i_{n}),

\mathrm{T}_{n}
is an approximate right inverse of
L_{n}
with suitable tame estimates needed later, see Theorem
5.1
.
Next we define,

\widetilde{U}_{n+1}:=U_{n}+\widetilde{H}_{n+1}\quad\mbox{ with }\quad\widetilde{H}_{n+1}:=(\widehat{\mathfrak{I}}_{n+1},\widehat{\alpha}_{n+1},0):=-\mathbf{\Pi}_{n}\mathrm{T}_{n}\Pi_{n}\mathcal{F}(U_{n})\in E_{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d+1},

where
\mathbf{\Pi}_{n}
is defined by

\mathbf{\Pi}_{n}(\mathfrak{I},\alpha,0)=(\Pi_{n}\mathfrak{I},\alpha,0)\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\mathbf{\Pi}_{n}^{\perp}(\mathfrak{I},\alpha,0)=(\Pi_{n}\mathfrak{I},0,0).

(7.18)

Notice that the projectors
\Pi_{n}
are reversibility preserving due to the symmetry with respect to the Fourier modes. Then, using the reversibility of
\mathrm{T}_{n}
together with (
5.24
) and Lemma
2.2
, one deduces from
\mathfrak{S}i_{n}(\varphi)=i_{n}(-\varphi)
that

\mathfrak{S}\widehat{\mathfrak{I}}_{n+1}(\varphi)=\widehat{\mathfrak{I}}_{n+1}(-\varphi).

(7.19)

Note that
U_{n}
is defined in the full set
\mathcal{O}
and so does
\widetilde{U}_{n+1}
. Nevertheless,
we will not be working with this natural extension but rather with a suitable localized version of it around the Cantor set
\mathcal{A}_{n+1}^{\gamma}.
Doing so, we shall get a nice decay property allowing the scheme to converge. Now, introduce the quadratic function

\displaystyle Q_{n}=\mathcal{F}(U_{n}+\widetilde{H}_{n+1})-\mathcal{F}(U_{n})-L_{n}\widetilde{H}_{n+1},

(7.20)

then simple transformations give

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\widetilde{U}_{n+1})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}(U_{n})-L_{n}\mathbf{\Pi}_{n}\mathrm{T}_{n}\Pi_{n}\mathcal{F}(U_{n})+Q_{n}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}(U_{n})-L_{n}\mathrm{T}_{n}\Pi_{n}\mathcal{F}(U_{n})+L_{n}\mathbf{\Pi}_{n}^{\perp}\mathrm{T}_{n}\Pi_{n}\mathcal{F}(U_{n})+Q_{n}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}(U_{n})-\Pi_{n}L_{n}\mathrm{T}_{n}\Pi_{n}\mathcal{F}(U_{n})+(L_{n}\mathbf{\Pi}_{n}^{\perp}-\Pi_{n}^{\perp}L_{n})\mathrm{T}_{n}\Pi_{n}\mathcal{F}(U_{n})+Q_{n}

\displaystyle=\Pi_{n}^{\perp}\mathcal{F}(U_{n})-\Pi_{n}(L_{n}\mathrm{T}_{n}-\textnormal{Id})\Pi_{n}\mathcal{F}(U_{n})+(L_{n}\mathbf{\Pi}_{n}^{\perp}-\Pi_{n}^{\perp}L_{n})\mathrm{T}_{n}\Pi_{n}\mathcal{F}(U_{n})+Q_{n}.

(7.21)

In the sequel we shall prove

\|\mathcal{F}(U_{n+1})\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n+1}^{\gamma}}\leqslant C_{\ast}\varepsilon N_{n}^{-a_{1}},

with
U_{n+1}
a suitable extension of
\widetilde{U}_{n+1}|_{\mathrm{O}_{n+1}^{\gamma}}.

\blacktriangleright

Estimates of
\mathcal{F}(\widetilde{U}_{n+1})
.

We shall now estimate
\mathcal{F}(\widetilde{U}_{n+1})
with the norm
\|\cdot\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n+1}^{2\gamma}}
by using (
7.1
). The localization in
\mathrm{O}_{n+1}^{2\gamma}
is required for the classical extension in the next point, see (
7.48
).

➢
Estimate of
\Pi_{n}^{\perp}\mathcal{F}(U_{n}).

We apply Taylor formula combined with (
5.23
) and Lemma
5.2
together with (
7.3
) and
(\mathcal{P}1)_{n}.
Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle\forall s\geqslant s_{0},\quad\|\mathcal{F}(U_{n})\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\mathcal{F}(U_{0})\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\mathcal{F}(U_{n})-\mathcal{F}(U_{0})\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon+\|W_{n}\|_{q,s+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(7.22)

As a consequence, (
7.2
) and
(\mathcal{P}1)_{n}
imply

\gamma^{-1}\|\mathcal{F}(U_{n})\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}\leqslant 1.

(7.23)

From Lemma
4.1
-(ii) and (
7.1
), we get

\displaystyle\|\Pi_{n}^{\perp}\mathcal{F}(U_{n})\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leqslant N_{n}^{s_{0}-b_{1}}\|\mathcal{F}(U_{n})\|_{q,b_{1}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{n}^{\overline{\sigma}-b_{1}}\left(\varepsilon+\|W_{n}\|_{q,b_{1}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right).

(7.24)

Now,
(\mathcal{P}3)_{n}
together (
5.82
) and (
7.2
) yield

\displaystyle\varepsilon+\|W_{n}\|_{q,b_{1}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\varepsilon\left(1+C_{\ast}\gamma^{-1}N_{n-1}^{\mu_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant 2C_{\ast}\varepsilon N_{n}^{\frac{2}{3}\mu_{1}+1}.

(7.25)

By putting together (
7.1
) and (
7.1
) and by making appeal to (
7.13
), we infer for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\|\Pi_{n}^{\perp}\mathcal{F}(U_{n})\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n+1}^{2\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\Pi_{n}^{\perp}\mathcal{F}(U_{n})\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{\ast}\varepsilon N_{n}^{s_{0}+\frac{2}{3}\mu_{1}+1-b_{1}}.

(7.26)

Remark that one also obtains, combining (
7.1
) and (
7.1
),

\|\mathcal{F}(U_{n})\|_{q,b_{1}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}\leqslant C_{\ast}\varepsilon N_{n}^{\overline{\sigma}+\frac{2}{3}\mu_{1}+1}.

(7.27)

➢
Estimate of
\Pi_{n}(L_{n}\mathrm{T}_{n}-\textnormal{Id})\Pi_{n}\mathcal{F}(U_{n})
.
In view of (
7.14
), one has

\mathrm{O}_{n+1}^{2\gamma}\subset\mathcal{A}_{n+1}^{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\subset\mathcal{G}_{n}\Big(\frac{\gamma_{n+1}}{2},\tau_{1},\tau_{2},i_{n}\Big).

Then, applying Theorem
5.1
, we can write

\Pi_{n}(L_{n}\mathrm{T}_{n}-\textnormal{Id})\Pi_{n}\mathcal{F}(U_{n})=\mathscr{E}_{1,n}+\mathscr{E}_{2,n}+\mathscr{E}_{3,n},

with

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}_{1,n}:=\Pi_{n}{\mathcal{E}_{1}^{(n)}}\Pi_{n}\mathcal{F}(U_{n}),

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}_{2,n}:=\Pi_{n}\mathcal{E}_{2}^{(n)}\Pi_{n}\mathcal{F}(U_{n}),

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}_{3,n}:=\Pi_{n}\mathcal{E}_{3}^{(n)}\Pi_{n}\mathcal{F}(U_{n})

where
\mathcal{E}_{1}^{(n)}
,
\mathcal{E}_{2}^{(n)}
and
\mathcal{E}_{3}^{(n)}
satisfy the estimates (
5.127
), (
5.128
) and (
5.129
) respectively. By (
7.13
), we get

\|\Pi_{n}(L_{n}\mathrm{T}_{n}-\textnormal{Id})\Pi_{n}\mathcal{F}(U_{n})\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n+1}^{2\gamma}}\leqslant\|\mathscr{E}_{1,n}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}+\|\mathscr{E}_{2,n}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}+\|\mathscr{E}_{3,n}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}.

(7.28)

We shall first focus on
\mathscr{E}_{1,n}.
We need the following interpolation-type inequality

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{F}(U_{n})\|_{q,s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\Pi_{n}\mathcal{F}(U_{n})\|_{q,s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}+\|\Pi_{n}^{\perp}\mathcal{F}(U_{n})\|_{q,s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leqslant N_{n}^{\overline{\sigma}}\|\mathcal{F}(U_{n})\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}+N_{n}^{s_{0}-b_{1}}\|\mathcal{F}(U_{n})\|_{q,b_{1}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}.

(7.29)

Combining (
5.127
), (
7.1
),
(\mathcal{P}_{1})_{n}
, (
7.4
) and (
7.27
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|\mathscr{E}_{1,n}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma^{-1}\|\mathcal{F}(U_{n})\|_{q,s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}\|\Pi_{n}\mathcal{F}(U_{n})\|_{q,s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{n}\|_{q,s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma^{-1}N_{n}^{\overline{\sigma}}\left(N_{n}^{\overline{\sigma}}\|\mathcal{F}(U_{n})\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}+N_{n}^{s_{0}-b_{1}}\|\mathcal{F}(U_{n})\|_{q,b_{1}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}\right)\|\mathcal{F}(U_{n})\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}\left(1+\|W_{n}\|_{q,s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{\ast}\varepsilon\left(N_{n}^{2\overline{\sigma}-\frac{4}{3}a_{1}}+N_{n}^{s_{0}+2\overline{\sigma}+\frac{2}{3}\mu_{1}+1-\frac{2}{3}a_{1}-b_{1}}\right).

(7.30)

We now turn to
\mathscr{E}_{2,n}
and
\mathscr{E}_{3,n}.
Applying (
5.128
) with
b=b_{1}-s_{0}
and using (
7.4
),
(\mathcal{P}_{2})_{n}
and
(\mathcal{P}_{3})_{n}
, we get

\displaystyle\|\mathscr{E}_{2,n}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma^{-1}N_{n}^{s_{0}-b_{1}}\left(\|\Pi_{n}\mathcal{F}(U_{n})\|_{q,b_{1}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}+\varepsilon\|\mathfrak{I}_{n}\|_{q,b_{1}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\Pi_{n}\mathcal{F}(U_{n})\|_{q,s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma^{-1}N_{n}^{s_{0}-b_{1}}\left(\|\mathcal{F}(U_{n})\|_{q,b_{1}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}+\varepsilon N_{n}^{\overline{\sigma}}\|W_{n}\|_{q,b_{1}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\mathcal{F}(U_{n})\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{\ast}\varepsilon N_{n}^{s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}+\frac{2}{3}\mu_{1}+2-b_{1}}+C_{\ast}\varepsilon N_{n}^{s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}+\frac{2}{3}\mu_{1}+2-\frac{2}{3}a_{1}-b_{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{\ast}\varepsilon N_{n}^{s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}+\frac{2}{3}\mu_{1}+2-b_{1}}.

(7.31)

Using the same techniques together with (
5.129
), (
5.82
), (
7.2
) and (
7.4
), we infer

\displaystyle\|\mathscr{E}_{3,n}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{n}^{s_{0}-b_{1}}\gamma^{-2}\left(\|\Pi_{n}\mathcal{F}(U_{n})\|_{q,b_{1}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}+\varepsilon\gamma^{-2}\|\mathfrak{I}_{n}\|_{q,b_{1}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\Pi_{n}\mathcal{F}(U_{n})\|_{q,s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\varepsilon\gamma^{-4}N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}N_{n}^{-\mu_{2}}\|\Pi_{n}\mathcal{F}(U_{n})\|_{q,s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{\ast}\varepsilon\left(N_{n}^{s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}+\frac{2}{3}\mu_{1}+2-b_{1}}+N_{n}^{\overline{\sigma}+1-\mu_{2}-\frac{2}{3}a_{1}}\right).

(7.32)

Putting together (
7.28
), (
7.1
), (
7.1
) and (
7.1
), we obtain

\|\Pi_{n}(L_{n}\mathrm{T}_{n}-\textnormal{Id})\Pi_{n}\mathcal{F}(U_{n})\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n+1}^{2\gamma}}\leqslant CC_{\ast}\varepsilon\left(N_{n}^{2\overline{\sigma}-\frac{4}{3}a_{1}}+N_{n}^{s_{0}+2\overline{\sigma}+\frac{2}{3}\mu_{1}+1-b_{1}}+N_{n}^{\overline{\sigma}+1-\mu_{2}-\frac{2}{3}a_{1}}\right).

(7.33)

For
n=0
, we deduce from (
7.3
),(
7.4
) and by slight modifications of the preceding computations

\displaystyle\|\Pi_{0}(L_{0}\mathrm{T}_{0}-\textnormal{Id})\Pi_{0}\mathcal{F}(U_{0})\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{1}^{2\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\mathscr{E}_{1,0}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}+\|\mathscr{E}_{2,0}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}+\|\mathscr{E}_{3,0}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}\gamma^{-1}+\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}+\big(\varepsilon\gamma^{-2}N_{0}^{s_{0}-b_{1}}+\varepsilon^{2}\gamma^{-4}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-2}.

(7.34)

➢
Estimate of
\big(L_{n}\mathbf{\Pi}_{n}^{\perp}-\Pi_{n}^{\perp}L_{n}\big)\mathrm{T}_{n}\Pi_{n}\mathcal{F}(U_{n}).
Combining (
5.60
) and (
5.23
), we get for
H=(\widehat{\mathfrak{I}},\widehat{\alpha})
with
\widehat{\mathfrak{I}}=(\widehat{\Theta},\widehat{I},\widehat{z}),

L_{n}H=\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}\widehat{\mathfrak{I}}-(0,0,\partial_{\theta}\mathrm{L}(\lambda)\widehat{z})-\varepsilon d_{i}X_{\mathcal{P}_{\varepsilon}}(i_{n})\widehat{\mathfrak{I}}-(\widehat{\alpha},0,0).

(7.35)

Using (
7.18
) and the fact that
\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}
and
\partial_{\theta}\mathrm{L}(\lambda)
are diagonal leading to
[\Pi_{n}^{\perp},\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}]=[\Pi_{n}^{\perp},\partial_{\theta}\mathrm{L}(\lambda)]=0
, one has for
H=(\widehat{\mathfrak{I}},\widehat{\alpha}),

\left(L_{n}\mathbf{\Pi}_{n}^{\perp}-\Pi_{n}^{\perp}L_{n}\right)H=-\varepsilon[d_{i}X_{\mathcal{P}_{\varepsilon}}(i_{n}),\Pi_{n}^{\perp}]\widehat{\mathfrak{I}}.

In view of Lemma
5.2
-(ii), Lemma
4.3
, (
7.13
) and
(\mathcal{P}1)_{n}
we get

\left\|\left(L_{n}\mathbf{\Pi}_{n}^{\perp}-\Pi_{n}^{\perp}L_{n}\right)H\right\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n+1}^{2\gamma}}\lesssim\varepsilon N_{n}^{s_{0}-b_{1}}\left(\|\widehat{\mathfrak{I}}\|_{q,b_{1}+1}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}+\|\mathfrak{I}_{n}\|_{q,b_{1}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\widehat{\mathfrak{I}}\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}\right).

Consequently,

\displaystyle\mathtt{N}_{\textnormal{\tiny{com}}}(s_{0}):=\left\|\left(L_{n}\mathbf{\Pi}_{n}^{\perp}-\Pi_{n}^{\perp}L_{n}\right)\mathrm{T}_{n}\Pi_{n}\mathcal{F}(U_{n})\right\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n+1}^{2\gamma}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon N_{n}^{s_{0}-b_{1}}\|\mathrm{T}_{n}\Pi_{n}\mathcal{F}(U_{n})\|_{q,b_{1}+1}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\quad+\varepsilon N_{n}^{s_{0}-b_{1}}\|\mathfrak{I}_{n}\|_{q,b_{1}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\mathrm{T}_{n}\Pi_{n}\mathcal{F}(U_{n})\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}.

Hence, gathering (
7.16
), Lemma
4.1
, Sobolev embeddings, (
7.4
), (
7.2
) and
(\mathcal{P}1)_{n}
yields

\displaystyle\mathtt{N}_{\textnormal{\tiny{com}}}(s_{0})

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{n}^{s_{0}-b_{1}}\|\Pi_{n}\mathcal{F}(U_{n})\|_{q,b_{1}+\overline{\sigma}+1}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}+\|\mathfrak{I}_{n}\|_{q,b_{1}+\overline{\sigma}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\Pi_{n}\mathcal{F}(U_{n})\|_{q,s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\quad+\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{n}^{s_{0}-b_{1}}\|\mathfrak{I}_{n}\|_{q,b_{1}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\left(\|\Pi_{n}\mathcal{F}(U_{n})\|_{q,s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}+1}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}+\|\mathfrak{I}_{n}\|_{q,s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\Pi_{n}\mathcal{F}(U_{n})\|_{q,s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon N_{n}^{s_{0}+2-b_{1}}\left(\|\mathcal{F}(U_{n})\|_{q,b_{1}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}+\|W_{n}\|_{q,b_{1}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\Pi_{n}\mathcal{F}(U_{n})\|_{q,s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}\right).

Applying Lemma
4.1
-(ii),
(\mathcal{P}2)_{n}
and (
5.82
), we infer

\displaystyle\|\Pi_{n}\mathcal{F}(U_{n})\|_{q,s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leqslant N_{n}^{\overline{\sigma}}\|\mathcal{F}(U_{n})\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{\ast}\varepsilon N_{n}^{\overline{\sigma}}N_{n-1}^{-a_{1}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{\ast}\varepsilon N_{n}^{\overline{\sigma}-\frac{2}{3}a_{1}}.

Added to (
7.1
), (
7.27
) and
(\mathcal{P}3)_{n}
, we obtain for
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\|(L_{n}\mathbf{\Pi}_{n}^{\perp}-\Pi_{n}^{\perp}L_{n})\mathrm{T}_{n}\Pi_{n}\mathcal{F}(U_{n})\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n+1}^{2\gamma}}\leqslant CC_{\ast}\varepsilon N_{n}^{s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}+\frac{2}{3}\mu_{1}+3-b_{1}}.

(7.36)

➢
Estimate of
Q_{n}
.
We apply Taylor formula together with (
7.20
) leading to

Q_{n}=\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)d_{i,\alpha}^{2}\mathcal{F}(U_{n}+t\widetilde{H}_{n+1})[\widetilde{H}_{n+1},\widetilde{H}_{n+1}]dt.

Thus, (
7.35
) and Lemma
5.2
-(iii) allow to get

\displaystyle\|Q_{n}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n+1}^{2\gamma}}\lesssim\varepsilon\left(1+\|W_{n}\|_{q,s_{0}+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\widetilde{H}_{n+1}\|_{q,s_{0}+2}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n+1}^{2\gamma}}\right)\left(\|\widetilde{H}_{n+1}\|_{q,s_{0}+2}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n+1}^{2\gamma}}\right)^{2}.

(7.37)

Combining (
7.14
), (
7.16
), (
7.1
) and (
7.23
), we find for all
s\in[s_{0},S]

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{H}_{n+1}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n+1}^{2\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\|\mathbf{\Pi}_{n}\mathrm{T}_{n}\Pi_{n}\mathcal{F}(U_{n})\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n+1}^{2\gamma}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma^{-1}\left(\|\Pi_{n}\mathcal{F}(U_{n})\|_{q,s+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}+\|\mathfrak{I}_{n}\|_{q,s+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\Pi_{n}\mathcal{F}(U_{n})\|_{q,s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma^{-1}\left(N_{n}^{\overline{\sigma}}\|\mathcal{F}(U_{n})\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}+N_{n}^{2\overline{\sigma}}\|\mathfrak{I}_{n}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\mathcal{F}(U_{n})\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma^{-1}N_{n}^{2\overline{\sigma}}\left(\varepsilon+\|W_{n}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right).

(7.38)

In the same way, according to (
7.17
),
(\mathcal{P}1)_{n}
and
(\mathcal{P}2)_{n}
, we infer

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{H}_{n+1}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n+1}^{2\gamma}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma^{-1}N_{n}^{\overline{\sigma}}\|\mathcal{F}(U_{n})\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{2\gamma}}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{\ast}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{n}^{\overline{\sigma}}N_{n-1}^{-a_{1}}.

(7.39)

Choosing
\varepsilon
small enough and using
(\mathcal{P}1)_{n}
and (
7.1
), we find

\displaystyle\|W_{n}\|_{q,s_{0}+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\widetilde{H}_{n+1}\|_{q,s_{0}+2}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n+1}^{2\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{\ast}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}+N_{n}^{2}\|\widetilde{H}_{n+1}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n+1}^{2\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leqslant 1+C\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{n}^{\overline{\sigma}+2}N_{n-1}^{-a_{1}}

\displaystyle\leqslant 1+C\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{n-1}^{3+\frac{3}{2}\overline{\sigma}-a_{1}}.

Now notice that (
7.1
) implies

a_{1}\geqslant 3+\tfrac{3}{2}\overline{\sigma}.

(7.40)

Therefore, we obtain

\|W_{n}\|_{q,s_{0}+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\widetilde{H}_{n+1}\|_{q,s_{0}+2}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n+1}^{2\gamma}}\leqslant 2.

Hence, plugging this estimate and (
7.1
) into (
7.37
) and using (
7.2
) and (
7.4
), we find

\displaystyle\|Q_{n}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n+1}^{2\gamma}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\left(\|\widetilde{H}_{n+1}\|_{q,s_{0}+2}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n+1}^{2\gamma}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\varepsilon N_{n}^{4}\left(\|\widetilde{H}_{n+1}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n+1}^{2\gamma}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon C_{\ast}N_{n}^{2\overline{\sigma}+4}N_{n-1}^{-2a_{1}}.

By using (
5.82
), we deduce when
n\geqslant 1
,

\|Q_{n}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}_{n+1}^{2\gamma}}\leqslant CC_{\ast}\varepsilon N_{n}^{2\overline{\sigma}+4-\frac{4}{3}a_{1}}.

(7.41)

For
n=0
, we come back to (
7.1
) and (
7.3
) to obtain for all
s\in[s_{0},S]

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{H}_{1}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{1}^{2\gamma}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma^{-1}\|\Pi_{0}\mathcal{F}(U_{0})\|_{q,s+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{0}^{2\gamma}}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{\ast}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}.

(7.42)

Finally, the inequality (
7.41
) becomes for
n=0
,

\|Q_{0}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{0}^{2\gamma}}\lesssim C_{\ast}\varepsilon^{3}\gamma^{-2}.

(7.43)

➢
Conclusion.

Inserting (
7.1
), (
7.33
), (
7.36
) and (
7.41
), into (
7.1
) implies for
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
,

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{F}(\widetilde{U}_{n+1})\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n+1}^{2\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leqslant CC_{\ast}\varepsilon\left(N_{n}^{s_{0}+2\overline{\sigma}+\frac{2}{3}\mu_{1}+1-b_{1}}+N_{n}^{\overline{\sigma}+1-\mu_{2}-\frac{2}{3}a_{1}}+N_{n}^{2\overline{\sigma}+4-\frac{4}{3}a_{1}}\right).

The parameters conditions stated in (
7.1
) give

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcl}s_{0}+2\overline{\sigma}+\frac{2}{3}\mu_{1}+2+a_{1}&\leqslant&b_{1}\\
\overline{\sigma}+\frac{1}{3}a_{1}+2&\leqslant&\mu_{2}\\
2\overline{\sigma}+5&\leqslant&\frac{1}{3}a_{1}.\end{array}\right.

(7.44)

Thus, by taking
N_{0}
large enough, that is
\varepsilon
small enough, we obtain for
n\in\mathbb{N},

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcl}CN_{n}^{s_{0}+2\overline{\sigma}+\frac{2}{3}\mu_{1}+1-b_{1}}&\leqslant&\frac{1}{3}N_{n}^{-a_{1}}\\
CN_{n}^{\overline{\sigma}+1-\mu_{2}-\frac{2}{3}a_{1}}&\leqslant&\frac{1}{3}N_{n}^{-a_{1}}\\
CN_{n}^{2\overline{\sigma}+4-\frac{4}{3}a_{1}}&\leqslant&\frac{1}{3}N_{n}^{-a_{1}},\end{array}\right.

(7.45)

which implies in turn
that when
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
,

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{F}(\widetilde{U}_{n+1})\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n+1}^{2\gamma}}\leqslant C_{\ast}\varepsilon N_{n}^{-a_{1}}.

(7.46)

However, when
n=0
, we plug (
7.1
), (
7.1
), (
7.36
) and (
7.43
) into (
7.1
) in order to get

\|\mathcal{F}(\widetilde{U}_{1})\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{1}^{2\gamma}}\leqslant CC_{\ast}\varepsilon\left(N_{0}^{s_{0}+2\overline{\sigma}+\frac{3}{2}\mu_{1}+1-b_{1}}+\varepsilon\gamma^{-2}+\varepsilon^{2}\gamma^{-2}\right).

From (
7.45
), one already has

CN_{0}^{s_{0}+2\overline{\sigma}+\frac{3}{2}\mu_{1}+1-b_{1}}\leqslant\tfrac{1}{3}N_{0}^{-a_{1}}.

Therefore, we need at this level to take
\varepsilon
small enough to ensure

C\left(\varepsilon\gamma^{-2}+\varepsilon^{2}\gamma^{-2}\right)\leqslant\tfrac{2}{3}N_{0}^{-a_{1}}.

This occurs since (
7.2
) and (
7.1
) imply

0<a<\tfrac{1}{2+a_{1}}.

Hence

\|\mathcal{F}(\widetilde{U}_{1})\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{1}^{2\gamma}}\leqslant C_{\ast}\varepsilon N_{0}^{-a_{1}}.

This completes the proof of the estimates in
(\mathcal{P}2)_{n+1}.

\blacktriangleright

Extension and verification of
(\mathcal{P}1)_{n+1}-(\mathcal{P}3)_{n+1}.
We shall now construct an extention of
\widetilde{H}_{n+1}
living in the whole set of parameters and enjoying suitable decay properties. This is done by using the
C^{\infty}
cut-off function
\chi_{n+1}:\mathcal{O}\rightarrow[0,1]
defined by

\chi_{n+1}(\lambda,\omega)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1&\textnormal{in }\mathrm{O}_{n+1}^{\gamma}\\
0&\textnormal{in }\mathcal{O}\setminus\mathrm{O}_{n+1}^{2\gamma}\end{array}\right.

and satisfying the additional growth conditions

\forall\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{d},\quad|\alpha|\in\llbracket 0,q\rrbracket,\quad\|\partial_{\lambda,\omega}^{\alpha}\chi_{n+1}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{O})}\lesssim\left(\gamma^{-1}N_{n}^{\overline{a}}\right)^{|\alpha|}.

(7.47)

Next, we shall deal with the extension
H_{n+1}
of
\widetilde{H}_{n+1}
defined by

H_{n+1}(\lambda,\omega):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\chi_{n+1}(\lambda,\omega)\widetilde{H}_{n+1}(\lambda,\omega)&\quad\textnormal{in }\quad\mathrm{O}_{n+1}^{2\gamma}\\
0&\quad\textnormal{in }\quad\mathcal{O}\setminus\mathrm{O}_{n+1}^{2\gamma}\end{array}\right.

(7.48)

and the extension
U_{n+1}
of
\widetilde{U}_{n+1}
by

U_{n+1}:=U_{n}+H_{n+1}.

(7.49)

We remark that

H_{n+1}=\widetilde{H}_{n+1}\quad\textnormal{and}\quad\mathcal{F}(U_{n+1})=\mathcal{F}(\widetilde{U}_{n+1})\quad\textnormal{in}\quad\mathrm{O}_{n+1}^{\gamma}.

Looking at the first component of (
7.49
), one can write with obvious notations

i_{n+1}=i_{n}+\mathfrak{I}_{n+1}.

By the induction assumption
(\mathcal{P}2)_{n}
, (
7.48
) and (
7.19
), one has

\mathfrak{S}i_{n}(\varphi)=i_{n}(-\varphi)\quad\textnormal{and}\quad\mathfrak{S}\mathfrak{I}_{n+1}(\varphi)=\mathfrak{I}_{n+1}(-\varphi).

Thus

\mathfrak{S}i_{n+1}(\varphi)=i_{n+1}(-\varphi).

(7.50)

Using Lemma
4.1
-(iv) together with (
7.47
) and the fact that
H_{n+1}=0
in
\mathcal{O}\setminus\mathrm{O}_{n+1}^{2\gamma}
, we obtain

\forall s\geqslant s_{0},\quad\|H_{n+1}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim N_{n}^{q\overline{a}}\|\widetilde{H}_{n+1}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n+1}^{2\gamma}}.

(7.51)

Applying (
7.51
) and (
7.1
) we deduce that for
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*},

\displaystyle\|H_{n+1}\|_{q,s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant CN_{n}^{q\overline{a}}\|\widetilde{H}_{n+1}\|_{q,s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n+1}^{2\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leqslant CN_{n}^{q\overline{a}+\overline{\sigma}}\|\widetilde{H}_{n+1}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n+1}^{2\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leqslant CC_{\ast}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{n}^{q\overline{a}+2\overline{\sigma}-\frac{2}{3}a_{1}}.

From (
7.1
), we have

\displaystyle a_{2}=\tfrac{2}{3}a_{1}-q\overline{a}-2\overline{\sigma}-1\geqslant 1.

(7.52)

Therefore, choosing
\varepsilon
small enough, we obtain

\displaystyle\|H_{n+1}\|_{q,s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant CN_{0}^{-1}C_{\ast}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{n}^{-a_{2}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{\ast}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{n}^{-a_{2}}.

(7.53)

As to the case
n=0
, we combine (
7.51
) and (
7.1
) to obtain, up to take
C_{\ast}
large enough,

\|H_{1}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant\tfrac{1}{2}C_{\ast}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{q\overline{a}}.

(7.54)

We now set

W_{n+1}:=W_{n}+H_{n+1},

(7.55)

then by construction, we infer

U_{n+1}=U_{0}+W_{n+1}.

Moreover, applying
(\mathcal{P}1)_{n}
, (
7.54
) and (
7.1
) and Lemma
A.1
, we infer

\displaystyle\|W_{n+1}\|_{q,s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|H_{1}\|_{q,s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\sum_{k=2}^{n+1}\|H_{k}\|_{q,s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\tfrac{1}{2}C_{\ast}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{q\overline{a}}+C_{\ast}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}N_{k}^{-1}

\displaystyle\leqslant\tfrac{1}{2}C_{\ast}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{q\overline{a}}+CN_{0}^{-1}C_{\ast}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{\ast}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{q\overline{a}}.

This completes the proof of
(\mathcal{P}1)_{n+1}.
Now gathering (
7.1
), (
7.51
) and
(\mathcal{P}3)_{n}
allows to write

\displaystyle\|W_{n+1}\|_{q,b_{1}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|W_{n}\|_{q,b_{1}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+CN_{n}^{q\overline{a}}\|H_{n+1}\|_{q,b_{1}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{\ast}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{n-1}^{\mu_{1}}+CC_{\ast}\gamma^{-1}N_{n}^{q\overline{a}+2\overline{\sigma}}\left(\varepsilon+\|W_{n}\|_{q,b_{1}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant CC_{\ast}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{n}^{q\overline{a}+2\overline{\sigma}+1+\frac{2}{3}\mu_{1}}.

Fom (
7.1
), we can ensure the condition

\displaystyle q\overline{a}+2\overline{\sigma}+2=\tfrac{\mu_{1}}{3},

(7.56)

in order to get

\displaystyle\|W_{n+1}\|_{q,b_{1}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}_{n+1}^{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leqslant CN_{0}^{-1}C_{\ast}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{n}^{\mu_{1}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{\ast}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{n}^{\mu_{1}}

by taking
\varepsilon
small enough and using (
7.2
). This proves
(\mathcal{P}3)_{n+1}
and the proof of Proposition
7.1
is now complete.
∎

Once this sequence of approximate solutions is constructed, we may obtain a non-trivial solution by passing to the limit. This is possible due the decay properties given in Proposition
7.1
. Actually, we obtain the following corollary.

Corollary 7.1
.

There exists
\varepsilon_{0}>0
such that for all
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}),
the following assertions hold true.
We consider the Cantor set
\mathcal{G}_{\infty}^{\gamma}
, depending on
\varepsilon
through
\gamma,
and defined by

\mathcal{G}_{\infty}^{\gamma}:=\bigcap_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{A}_{n}^{\gamma}.

There exists a function

U_{\infty}:\begin{array}[t]{rcl}\mathcal{O}&\rightarrow&\left(\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times H_{\mathbb{S}}^{\perp}\right)\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d+1}\\
(\lambda,\omega)&\mapsto&\big(i_{\infty}(\lambda,\omega),\alpha_{\infty}(\lambda,\omega),(\lambda,\omega)\big)\end{array}

such that

\forall(\lambda,\omega)\in\mathcal{G}_{\infty}^{\gamma},\quad\mathcal{F}(U_{\infty}(\lambda,\omega))=0.

In addition,
i_{\infty}
is reversible and
\alpha_{\infty}\in W^{q,\infty,\gamma}(\mathcal{O},\mathbb{R}^{d})
with

\alpha_{\infty}(\lambda,\omega)=\omega+\mathrm{r}_{\varepsilon}(\lambda,\omega)\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\|\mathrm{r}_{\varepsilon}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{q\overline{a}}.

(7.57)

Moreover, there exists a
q
-times differentiable function
\lambda\in(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1})\mapsto\omega(\lambda,\varepsilon)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
with

\omega(\lambda,\varepsilon)=-\omega_{\textnormal{Eq}}(\lambda)+\bar{r}_{\varepsilon}(\lambda),\quad\|\bar{r}_{\varepsilon}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{q\overline{a}}

(7.58)

and

\forall\lambda\in\mathcal{C}_{\infty}^{\varepsilon},\quad\mathcal{F}\Big(U_{\infty}\big(\lambda,\omega(\lambda,\varepsilon)\big)\Big)=0\quad\textnormal{and}\quad\alpha_{\infty}\big(\lambda,\omega(\lambda,\varepsilon)\big)=-\omega_{\textnormal{Eq}}(\lambda),

where the Cantor set
\mathcal{C}_{\infty}^{\varepsilon}
is defined by

\mathcal{C}_{\infty}^{\varepsilon}=\Big\{\lambda\in(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1})\quad\textnormal{s.t.}\quad\big(\lambda,\omega(\lambda,\varepsilon)\big)\in\mathcal{G}_{\infty}^{\gamma}\Big\}.

(7.59)

Proof.

Putting together (
7.55
) and (
7.1
), we infer

\|W_{n+1}-W_{n}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}=\|H_{n+1}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant\|H_{n+1}\|_{q,s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant C_{\ast}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{n}^{-a_{2}}.

Thus, the telescopic series associated with the sequence
\left(W_{n}\right)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is convergent, so the sequence itself converges. We denote its limit

W_{\infty}:=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}W_{n}:=(\mathfrak{I}_{\infty},\alpha_{\infty}-\omega,0,0)

and

U_{\infty}:=\big(i_{\infty},\alpha_{\infty},(\lambda,\omega)\big)=U_{0}+W_{\infty}.

Passing to the limit in (
7.50
), one obtains the reversibility property

\mathfrak{S}i_{\infty}(\varphi)=i_{\infty}(-\varphi).

By the point
(\mathcal{P}2)_{n}
of Proposition
7.1
, we have for small
\varepsilon

\forall(\lambda,\omega)\in\mathcal{G}_{\infty}^{\gamma},\quad\mathcal{F}\Big(i_{\infty}(\lambda,\omega),\alpha_{\infty}(\lambda,\omega),(\lambda,\omega),\varepsilon\Big)=0,

(7.60)

with
\mathcal{F}
the functional defined in (
5.23
). We highlight that the Cantor set
\mathcal{G}_{\infty}^{\gamma}
depends on
\varepsilon
through
\gamma
and (
7.2
). By the point
(\mathcal{P}1)_{n}
of the Proposition
7.1
, we have

\alpha_{\infty}(\lambda,\omega)=\omega+\mathrm{r}_{\varepsilon}(\lambda,\omega)\quad\mbox{ with }\quad\|\mathrm{r}_{\varepsilon}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{q\overline{a}}.

We now prove the second result and check the existence of solutions to the original Hamiltonian equation. First recall that the open set
\mathcal{O}
is defined in (
4.3
) by

\mathcal{O}=(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1})\times\mathscr{U}\quad\textnormal{with}\quad\mathscr{U}=B(0,R_{0})\quad\textnormal{for some large }R_{0}>0,

where the ball
\mathscr{U}
is taken to contain the equilibrium frequency vector
\lambda\mapsto{\omega}_{\textnormal{Eq}}(\lambda).
According to (
7.57
), we deduce that for any
\lambda\in(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}),
the mapping
\omega\mapsto\alpha_{\infty}(\lambda,\omega)
is invertible from
\mathscr{U}
into its image
\alpha_{\infty}(\lambda,\mathscr{U})
and we have

\widehat{\omega}=\alpha_{\infty}(\lambda,\omega)=\omega+\mathrm{r}_{\varepsilon}(\lambda,\omega)\Leftrightarrow\omega=\alpha_{\infty}^{-1}(\lambda,\widehat{\omega})=\widehat{\omega}+\widehat{\mathrm{r}}_{\varepsilon}(\lambda,\widehat{\omega}).

This gives the identity

\widehat{\mathrm{r}}_{\varepsilon}(\lambda,\widehat{\omega})=-\mathrm{r}_{\varepsilon}(\lambda,\omega),

which implies in turn after using successive differentiation and (
7.57
) that
\widehat{\mathrm{r}}_{\varepsilon}
satisfies the estimate

\|\widehat{\mathrm{r}}_{\varepsilon}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{q\overline{a}}.

(7.61)

We now set

{\omega}(\lambda,\varepsilon):=\alpha_{\infty}^{-1}(\lambda,-{\omega}_{\textnormal{Eq}}(\lambda))=-{\omega}_{\textnormal{Eq}}(\lambda)+\overline{\mathrm{r}}_{\varepsilon}(\lambda)\quad\mbox{ with }\quad\overline{\mathrm{r}}_{\varepsilon}(\lambda):=\widehat{\mathrm{r}}_{\varepsilon}\big(\lambda,-{\omega}_{\textnormal{Eq}}(\lambda)\big).

As a consequence of (
7.60
), if we denote

\mathcal{C}_{\infty}^{\varepsilon}:=\Big\{\lambda\in(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1})\quad\textnormal{s.t.}\quad\big(\lambda,\omega(\lambda,\varepsilon)\big)\in\mathcal{G}_{\infty}^{\gamma}\Big\},

then we have

\forall\lambda\in\mathcal{C}_{\infty}^{\varepsilon},\quad\mathcal{F}\Big(U_{\infty}\big(\lambda,\omega(\lambda,\varepsilon)\big)\Big)=0.

This gives a nontrivial reversible solution for the original Hamiltonian equation provided that
\lambda\in\mathcal{C}_{\infty}^{\varepsilon}.

Since all the derivatives up to order
q
of
\omega_{\textnormal{Eq}}
are uniformly bounded on
[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}],
see Lemma
3.3
-(vi), then by chain rule and (
7.61
), we obtain

\|\overline{\mathrm{r}}_{\varepsilon}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{q\overline{a}}\quad\textnormal{and}\quad\|\omega(\cdot,\varepsilon)\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim 1+\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{q\overline{a}}\lesssim 1.

(7.62)

This ends the proof of Corollary
7.1
.
∎

7.2
Measure of the final Cantor set

The purpose of this final section is to give a lower bound of the Lebesgue measure of the Cantor set
\mathcal{C}_{\infty}^{\varepsilon}
constructed in Corollary
7.1
via (
7.59
). We show that this set is massive and asymptotically when
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
it tends to be of full
measure in
(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1})
. Note that Corollary

7.1
allows us to write the Cantor set
\mathcal{C}_{\infty}^{\varepsilon}
in the following form

\mathcal{C}_{\infty}^{\varepsilon}:=\bigcap_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{C}_{n}^{\varepsilon}\quad\mbox{ where }\quad\mathcal{C}_{n}^{\varepsilon}:=\Big\{\lambda\in(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1})\quad\hbox{s.t}\quad\big(\lambda,{\omega}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\big)\in\mathcal{A}_{n}^{\gamma}\Big\}.

(7.63)

The sets
\mathcal{A}_{n}^{\gamma}
and the perturbed frequency vector
\omega(\lambda,\varepsilon)
are respectively defined in Proposition
7.1
and in (
7.57
). The main result of this section reads as follows.

Proposition 7.2
.

Let
q_{0}
be defined as in Lemma
3.5
and assume that (
7.1
) and (
7.2
) hold with
q=q_{0}+1.
Assume the additional conditions

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\tau_{1}>dq_{0}\\
\tau_{2}>\tau_{1}+dq_{0}\\
\upsilon=\frac{1}{q_{0}+3}.\end{array}\right.

(7.64)

Then, there exists
C>0
such that

\big|\mathcal{C}_{\infty}^{\varepsilon}\big|\geqslant(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{0})-C\varepsilon^{\frac{a\upsilon}{q_{0}}}.

In particular,

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\big|\mathcal{C}_{\infty}^{\varepsilon}\big|=\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{0}.

The remainder of this section is devoted to the proof of Proposition
7.2
. We shall begin by giving the proof using some a priori results. These results will be proved later in Lemmas
7.1
,
7.2
and
7.3
. We first give a short insight about the strategy to prove Proposition
7.2
. The idea is to measure the complementary set of
\mathcal{C}_{\infty}^{\varepsilon}
in
(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}).
To proceed with, we write

(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1})\setminus\mathcal{C}_{\infty}^{\varepsilon}=\big((\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1})\setminus\mathcal{C}_{0}^{\varepsilon}\big)\sqcup\bigsqcup_{n=0}^{\infty}\big(\mathcal{C}_{n}^{\varepsilon}\setminus\mathcal{C}_{n+1}^{\varepsilon}\big).

(7.65)

The measure of each set which appears, we estimate it by using Lemma
3.6
. We shall now give the proof of Proposition
7.2
.

Proof.

By choosing
R_{0}
large enough, one can ensure using (
7.58
) that

\forall\lambda\in(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}),\quad\omega(\lambda,\varepsilon)\in\mathscr{U}=B(0,R_{0}).

Indeed,
\mathscr{U}
contains by construction the curve
\lambda\in(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1})\mapsto\pm\omega_{\textnormal{Eq}}(\lambda)
and by (
7.58
) and (
7.2
), one has

\sup_{\lambda\in(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1})}\left|\omega(\lambda,\varepsilon)+\omega_{\textnormal{Eq}}(\lambda)\right|\leqslant\|\overline{\mathrm{r}}_{\varepsilon}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant C\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{q\overline{a}}=C\varepsilon^{1-a(1+q\overline{a})}.

Now, the conditions (
7.1
) and (
7.2
) imply in particular

0<a<\frac{1}{1+q\overline{a}}\cdot

Hence, by taking
\varepsilon
small enough, we find

\sup_{\lambda\in(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1})}\left|\omega(\lambda,\varepsilon)+\omega_{\textnormal{Eq}}(\lambda)\right|\leqslant\|\overline{\mathrm{r}}_{\varepsilon}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant 1.

As a consequence,

\mathcal{C}_{0}^{\varepsilon}=(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}).

By (
7.65
), we can write

\displaystyle\Big|(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1})\setminus\mathcal{C}_{\infty}^{\varepsilon}\Big|\leqslant

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\Big|\mathcal{C}_{n}^{\varepsilon}\setminus\mathcal{C}_{n+1}^{\varepsilon}\Big|

\displaystyle:=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\mathcal{S}_{n}.

(7.66)

According to the notation introduced in Proposition
6.5
and Proposition
6.4
one may write

\displaystyle\mu_{j}^{\infty,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)

\displaystyle:=\mu_{j}^{\infty}\big(\lambda,{\omega}(\lambda,\varepsilon),i_{n}\big)

\displaystyle=\Omega_{j}(\lambda)+jr^{1,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)+r_{j}^{\infty,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon),

(7.67)

with

\displaystyle r^{1,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)

\displaystyle:=c_{n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)-\Omega-I_{1}(\lambda)K_{1}(\lambda),

\displaystyle c_{n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)

\displaystyle:=c_{i_{n}}(\lambda,{\omega}(\lambda,\varepsilon)),

\displaystyle r_{j}^{\infty,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)

\displaystyle:=r_{j}^{\infty}\big(\lambda,{\omega}(\lambda,\varepsilon),i_{n}\big).

Coming back to (
7.63
) and using the Cantor sets introduced in Proposition
6.5
, Proposition
6.6
and
Proposition
6.2
one obtains by construction that for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\mathcal{C}_{n}^{\varepsilon}\setminus\mathcal{C}_{n+1}^{\varepsilon}=\bigcup_{(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{(0,0)\}\atop|l|\leqslant N_{n}}\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(0)}(i_{n})\bigcup_{(l,j,j_{0})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times(\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c})^{2}\atop|l|\leqslant N_{n}}\mathcal{R}_{l,j,j_{0}}(i_{n})\bigcup_{(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c}\atop|l|\leqslant N_{n}}\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(1)}(i_{n}),

(7.68)

with

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(0)}(i_{n})

\displaystyle:=\left\{\lambda\in\mathcal{C}_{n}^{\varepsilon}\quad\textnormal{s.t.}\quad|{\omega}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\cdot l+jc_{n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)|\leqslant\tfrac{4\gamma_{n+1}^{\upsilon}\langle j\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{l,j,j_{0}}(i_{n})

\displaystyle:=\left\{\lambda\in\mathcal{C}_{n}^{\varepsilon}\quad\textnormal{s.t.}\quad|{\omega}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)-\mu_{j_{0}}^{\infty,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)|\leqslant\tfrac{2\gamma_{n+1}\langle j-j_{0}\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{2}}}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(1)}(i_{n})

\displaystyle:=\left\{\lambda\in\mathcal{C}_{n}^{\varepsilon}\quad\textnormal{s.t.}\quad|{\omega}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)|\leqslant\tfrac{\gamma_{n+1}\langle j\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}\right\}.

Notice that using the inclusion

W^{q,\infty,\gamma}(\mathcal{O},\mathbb{C})\hookrightarrow C^{q-1}(\mathcal{O},\mathbb{C})

and the fact that
q=q_{0}+1
, one gets that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
(l,j,j_{0})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times(\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c})^{2},
the curves

\displaystyle\lambda\mapsto\omega(\lambda,\varepsilon)\cdot l+c_{n}(\lambda,\varepsilon),

\displaystyle\lambda\mapsto\omega(\lambda,\varepsilon)\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)-\mu_{j_{0}}^{\infty,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon),

\displaystyle\lambda\mapsto\omega(\lambda,\varepsilon)\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)

have a
C^{q_{0}}
regularity. Then, applying Lemma
3.6
combined with Lemma
7.3
gives for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\Big|\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(0)}(i_{n})\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma^{\frac{\upsilon}{q_{0}}}\langle j\rangle^{\frac{1}{q_{0}}}\langle l\rangle^{-1-\frac{\tau_{1}+1}{q_{0}}},

\displaystyle\Big|\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(1)}(i_{n})\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma^{\frac{1}{q_{0}}}\langle j\rangle^{\frac{1}{q_{0}}}\langle l\rangle^{-1-\frac{\tau_{1}+1}{q_{0}}},

(7.69)

\displaystyle\Big|\mathcal{R}_{l,j,j_{0}}(i_{n})\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma^{\frac{1}{q_{0}}}\langle j-j_{0}\rangle^{\frac{1}{q_{0}}}\langle l\rangle^{-1-\frac{\tau_{2}+1}{q_{0}}}.

Let us now move to the estimate of
\mathcal{S}_{0}
and
\mathcal{S}_{1}
defined in (
7.2
) that should be treated differently from the other terms. This is related to the discussion done at the beginning of the proof of Lemma
7.1
dealing with the validity of the estimate (
7.2
). By using Lemma
7.2
, we find for all
k\in\{0,1\}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{k}\lesssim\sum_{\underset{|j|\leqslant C_{0}\langle l\rangle,|l|\leqslant N_{k}}{(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{(0,0)\}}}\Big|\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(0)}(i_{k})\Big|+\sum_{\underset{\underset{\min(|j|,|j_{0}|)\leqslant c_{2}\gamma_{1}^{-\upsilon}\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}{|j-j_{0}|\leqslant C_{0}\langle l\rangle,|l|\leqslant N_{k}}}{(l,j,j_{0})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times(\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c})^{2}}}\Big|\mathcal{R}_{l,j,j_{0}}(i_{k})\Big|+\sum_{\underset{|j|\leqslant C_{0}\langle l\rangle,|l|\leqslant N_{k}}{(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c}}}\Big|\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(1)}(i_{k})\Big|.

(7.70)

Plugging (
7.2
) into (
7.70
) yields for all
k\in\{0,1\}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{k}

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma^{\frac{1}{q_{0}}}\Bigg(\sum_{|j|\leqslant C_{0}\langle l\rangle}|j|^{\frac{1}{q_{0}}}\langle l\rangle^{-1-\frac{\tau_{1}+1}{q_{0}}}+\sum_{\underset{\min(|j|,|j_{0}|)\leqslant c_{2}\gamma^{-\upsilon}\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}{|j-j_{0}|\leqslant C_{0}\langle l\rangle}}|j-j_{0}|^{\frac{1}{q_{0}}}\langle l\rangle^{-1-\frac{\tau_{2}+1}{q_{0}}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+\gamma^{\frac{\upsilon}{q_{0}}}\sum_{{|j|\leqslant C_{0}\langle l\rangle}}|j|^{\frac{1}{q_{0}}}\langle l\rangle^{-1-\frac{\tau_{1}+1}{q_{0}}}.

Consequently, we obtain

\displaystyle\max_{k\in\{0,1\}}\mathcal{S}_{k}

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma^{\frac{1}{q_{0}}}\Bigg(\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\langle l\rangle^{-\frac{\tau_{1}}{q_{0}}}+\gamma^{-\upsilon}\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}-1-\frac{\tau_{2}}{q_{0}}}\Bigg)+\gamma^{\frac{\upsilon}{q_{0}}}\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\langle l\rangle^{-\frac{\tau_{1}}{q_{0}}}

(7.71)

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma^{\min\left(\frac{\upsilon}{q_{0}},\frac{1}{q_{0}}-\upsilon\right)}.

Notice that the last estimate is obtained provided that we choose the parameters
\tau_{1}
and
\tau_{2}
in the following way in order to make the series convergent

\displaystyle\tau_{1}>d\,q_{0}\quad\hbox{and}\quad\tau_{2}>\tau_{1}+d\,q_{0}.

(7.72)

This condition is exactly what we required in (
7.64
). Concerning the estimate of
\mathcal{S}_{n}
for
n\geqslant 2
in (
7.2
) we may use Lemma
7.1
and Lemma
7.2
, in order to get

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{n}\leqslant\sum_{\underset{|j|\leqslant C_{0}\langle l\rangle,N_{n-1}<|l|\leqslant N_{n}}{(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{(0,0)\}}}\Big|\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(0)}(i_{n})\Big|+\sum_{\underset{\underset{\min(|j|,|j_{0}|)\leqslant c_{2}\gamma_{n+1}^{-\upsilon}\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}{|j-j_{0}|\leqslant C_{0}\langle l\rangle,N_{n-1}<|l|\leqslant N_{n}}}{(l,j,j_{0})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times(\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c})^{2}}}\Big|\mathcal{R}_{l,j,j_{0}}(i_{n})\Big|+\sum_{\underset{|j|\leqslant C_{0}\langle l\rangle,N_{n-1}<|l|\leqslant N_{n}}{(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c}}}\Big|\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(1)}(i_{n})\Big|.

Remark that if
|j-j_{0}|\leqslant C_{0}\langle l\rangle
and
\min(|j|,|j_{0}|)\leqslant\gamma_{n+1}^{-\upsilon}\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}
, then

\max(|j|,|j_{0}|)=\min(|j|,|j_{0}|)+|j-j_{0}|\leqslant\gamma_{n+1}^{-\upsilon}\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}+C_{0}\langle l\rangle\lesssim\gamma^{-\upsilon}\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}.

Therefore, (
7.2
) implies
