\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{n}\lesssim\gamma^{\frac{1}{q_{0}}}\Bigg(\sum_{|l|>N_{n-1}}\langle l\rangle^{-\frac{\tau_{1}}{q_{0}}}+\gamma^{-\upsilon}\sum_{|l|>N_{n-1}}\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}-1-\frac{\tau_{2}}{q_{0}}}\Bigg)+\gamma^{\frac{\upsilon}{q_{0}}}\sum_{|l|>N_{n-1}}\langle l\rangle^{-\frac{\tau_{1}}{q_{0}}}.

Under the assumption, we obtain (
7.72
)

\displaystyle\sum_{n=2}^{\infty}\mathcal{S}_{n}

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma^{\min\left(\frac{\upsilon}{q_{0}},\frac{1}{q_{0}}-\upsilon\right)}.

(7.73)

Plugging (
7.73
) and (
7.71
) into (
7.2
) gives

\Big|(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1})\setminus\mathcal{C}_{\infty}^{\varepsilon}\Big|\lesssim\gamma^{\min\left(\frac{\upsilon}{q_{0}},\frac{1}{q_{0}}-\upsilon\right)}

provided that the condition (
7.72
) is satisfied.
The condition (
7.64
) implies that

\min\left(\tfrac{\upsilon}{q_{0}},\tfrac{1}{q_{0}}-\upsilon\right)=\tfrac{\upsilon}{q_{0}}.

We then find, since
\gamma=\varepsilon^{a}
according to (
7.2
),

\Big|(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1})\setminus\mathcal{C}_{\infty}^{\varepsilon}\Big|\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{a\upsilon}{q_{0}}}.

This completes the proof of Proposition
7.2
.
∎

Now we are left to prove Lemma
7.1
and Lemma
7.2
used in the proof of Proposition
7.2
.

Lemma 7.1
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{0,1\}
and
l\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
such that
|l|\leqslant N_{n-1}.
Then the following assertions hold true.

(i)

For
j\in\mathbb{Z}
with
(l,j)\neq(0,0)
, we get
\,\,\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(0)}(i_{n})=\varnothing.

(ii)

For
(j,j_{0})\in(\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c})^{2}
with
(l,j)\neq(0,j_{0}),
we get
\,\,\mathcal{R}_{l,j,j_{0}}(i_{n})=\varnothing.

(iii)

For
j\in\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c}
, we get
\,\,\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(1)}(i_{n})=\varnothing.

(iv)

For any
n\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{0,1\},

\mathcal{C}_{n}^{\varepsilon}\setminus\mathcal{C}_{n+1}^{\varepsilon}=\bigcup_{\underset{N_{n-1}<|l|\leqslant N_{n}}{(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{(0,0)\}}}\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(0)}(i_{n})\cup\bigcup_{\underset{N_{n-1}<|l|\leqslant N_{n}}{(l,j,j_{0})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times(\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c})^{2}}}\mathcal{R}_{l,j,j_{0}}(i_{n})\cup\bigcup_{\underset{N_{n-1}<|l|\leqslant N_{n}}{(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c}}}\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(1)}(i_{n}).

Proof.

In all the proof, we shall use the following estimate coming from (
7.11
), namely, for all
n\geqslant 2
,

\displaystyle\|i_{n}-i_{n-1}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\sigma_{4}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|U_{n}-U_{n-1}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\sigma_{4}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|H_{n}\|_{q,s_{h}+\sigma_{4}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{\ast}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{n-1}^{-a_{2}}.

(7.74)

The fact that the previous estimate is valid only for
n\geqslant 2
is the reason why we had to treat the cases of
\mathcal{S}_{0}
and
\mathcal{S}_{1}
sparately in the proof of Proposition
7.2
.

(i)
We begin by proving that if
|l|\leqslant N_{n-1}
and
(l,j)\neq(0,0),
then
\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(0)}(i_{n})\subset\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(0)}(i_{n-1}).
Assume for a while this inclusion and let us check how this implies that
\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(0)}(i_{n})=\varnothing.
In view of (
7.68
) one obtains

\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(0)}(i_{n})\subset\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(0)}(i_{n-1})\subset\mathcal{C}_{n-1}^{\varepsilon}\setminus\mathcal{C}_{n}^{\varepsilon}.

Now (
7.68
) implies in particular
\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(0)}(i_{n})\subset\mathcal{C}_{n}^{\varepsilon}\setminus\mathcal{C}_{n+1}^{\varepsilon}
and thus we conclude

\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(0)}(i_{n})\subset\big(\mathcal{C}_{n}^{\varepsilon}\setminus\mathcal{C}_{n+1}^{\varepsilon}\big)\cap\big(\mathcal{C}_{n-1}^{\varepsilon}\setminus\mathcal{C}_{n}^{\varepsilon}\big)=\varnothing.

We now turn to the proof of the inclusion. Let us consider
\lambda\in\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(0)}(i_{n}).
By construction, we get in particular that
\lambda\in\mathcal{C}_{n}^{\varepsilon}\subset\mathcal{C}_{n-1}^{\varepsilon}.
Moreover, by the triangle inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\big|{\omega}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\cdot l+jc_{n-1}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\big|

\displaystyle\leqslant\big|{\omega}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\cdot l+jc_{n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\big|+|j|\big|c_{n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)-c_{n-1}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\big|

\displaystyle\leqslant\displaystyle\tfrac{4\gamma_{n+1}^{\upsilon}\langle j\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}+C|j|\|c_{i_{n}}-c_{i_{n-1}}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Therefore, combining (
6.30
), (
7.2
), (
7.2
) and the fact tht
\sigma_{4}\geqslant 2
, we infer

\displaystyle\big|{\omega}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\cdot l+jc_{n-1}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\big|

\displaystyle\leqslant\displaystyle\tfrac{4\gamma_{n+1}^{\upsilon}\langle j\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}+C\varepsilon\langle j\rangle\|i_{n}-i_{n-1}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\displaystyle\tfrac{4\gamma_{n+1}^{\upsilon}\langle j\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}+C\varepsilon^{2-a}\langle j\rangle N_{n-1}^{-a_{2}}.

In view of the definition of
\gamma_{n}
in Proposition
7.1
-
(\mathcal{P}2)_{n}
one gets

\exists c_{0}>0,\quad\forall n\in\mathbb{N},\quad\gamma_{n+1}^{\upsilon}-\gamma_{n}^{\upsilon}\leqslant-c_{0}\,\gamma^{\upsilon}2^{-n}.

Now remark that (
7.64
), (
7.1
) and (
7.2
) imply

\displaystyle 2-a-a\upsilon>1\quad\hbox{and}\quad a_{2}>\tau_{1},

(7.75)

and therefore one gets
\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}2^{n}N_{n-1}^{-a_{2}+\tau_{1}}<\infty.
It follows that, for
\varepsilon
small enough and
|l|\leqslant N_{n-1}
,

\displaystyle\big|{\omega}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\cdot l+jc_{n-1}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\big|

\displaystyle\leqslant\displaystyle\tfrac{4\gamma_{n}^{\upsilon}\langle j\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}+C\tfrac{\langle j\rangle\gamma^{\upsilon}}{2^{n}\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}\Big(-4c_{0}+C\varepsilon 2^{n}N_{n-1}^{-a_{2}+\tau_{1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leqslant\displaystyle\tfrac{4\gamma_{n}^{\upsilon}\langle j\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}.

Consequently
\lambda\in\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(0)}(i_{n-1})
and this achieves the proof.

(ii)
Let
(j,j_{0})\in(\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c})^{2}
and
(l,j)\neq(0,j_{0}).
If
j=j_{0}
then by construction
\mathcal{R}_{l,j_{0},j_{0}}(i_{n})=\mathcal{R}_{l,0}^{(0)}(i_{n})
and then the result follows from the point (i). Now let us discuss the case when
j\neq j_{0}.
Similarly to the point (i), in order to get the result it is enough to check that
\mathcal{R}_{l,j,j_{0}}(i_{n})\subset\mathcal{R}_{l,j,j_{0}}(i_{n-1}).

Let
\lambda\in\mathcal{R}_{l,j,j_{0}}(i_{n})
then from the definition of this set introduced in (
7.68
) we deduce that
\lambda\in\mathcal{C}_{n}^{\varepsilon}\subset\mathcal{C}_{n-1}^{\varepsilon}
and

\big|{\omega}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty,n-1}(\lambda,\varepsilon)-\mu_{j_{0}}^{\infty,n-1}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\big|\leqslant\displaystyle\tfrac{2\gamma_{n+1}\langle j-j_{0}\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{2}}}+\varrho^{n}_{j,j_{0}}(\lambda,\varepsilon),

(7.76)

where we set

\varrho^{n}_{j,j_{0}}(\lambda,\varepsilon):=\big|\mu_{j}^{\infty,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)-\mu_{j_{0}}^{\infty,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)-\mu_{j}^{\infty,n-1}(\lambda,\varepsilon)+\mu_{j_{0}}^{\infty,n-1}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\big|.

Then coming back to (
7.2
), one gets

\displaystyle\varrho^{n}_{j,j_{0}}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\leqslant|j-j_{0}|\big|r^{1,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)-r^{1,n-1}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\big|

\displaystyle+\big|r_{j}^{\infty,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)-r_{j}^{\infty,n-1}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\big|

\displaystyle+\big|r_{j_{0}}^{\infty,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)-r_{j_{0}}^{\infty,n-1}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\big|.

(7.77)

In view of (
6.179
), (
7.2
), (
7.2
) and the fact that
\sigma_{4}\geqslant\sigma_{3},
one obtains

\displaystyle\big|r^{1,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)-r^{1,n-1}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\|i_{n}-i_{n-1}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}\gamma^{-1}N_{n-1}^{-a_{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{2-a}\langle j-j_{0}\rangle N_{n-1}^{-a_{2}}.

In a similar line,
using (
6.250
), (
7.2
) and (
7.2
) yields

\displaystyle\big|r_{j}^{\infty,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)-r_{j}^{\infty,n-1}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|i_{n}-i_{n-1}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\sigma_{4}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}\gamma^{-2}N_{n-1}^{-a_{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{2(1-a)}\langle j-j_{0}\rangle N_{n-1}^{-a_{2}}.

Inserting the preceding two estimates into (
7.2
) gives

\displaystyle\varrho^{n}_{j,j_{0}}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\lesssim\varepsilon^{2(1-a)}\langle j-j_{0}\rangle N_{n-1}^{-a_{2}}.

(7.78)

Putting together (
7.78
) and (
7.76
) and using
\gamma_{n+1}=\gamma_{n}-\varepsilon^{a}2^{-n-1},
we deduce

\displaystyle\big|{\omega}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty,n-1}(\lambda,\varepsilon)-\mu_{j_{0}}^{\infty,n-1}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\big|

\displaystyle\leqslant\displaystyle\tfrac{2\gamma_{n}\langle j-j_{0}\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{2}}}-{\varepsilon^{a}\langle j-j_{0}\rangle}2^{-n}\langle l\rangle^{-\tau_{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+C\varepsilon^{2(1-a)}\langle j-j_{0}\rangle N_{n-1}^{-a_{2}}.

Since
|l|\leqslant N_{n-1}
,we can write

\displaystyle-{\varepsilon^{a}}2^{-n}\langle l\rangle^{-\tau_{2}}+C\varepsilon^{2(1-a)}N_{n-1}^{-a_{2}}\leqslant{\varepsilon^{a}}2^{-n}\langle l\rangle^{-\tau_{2}}\Big(-1+C\varepsilon^{2-3a}2^{n}N_{n-1}^{-a_{2}+\tau_{2}}\Big).

Now remark that (
7.1
) and (
7.2
) yield in particular

\displaystyle a_{2}>\tau_{2}\quad\hbox{and}\quad a<\tfrac{2}{3}.

(7.79)

Hence, we find for
\varepsilon
small enough

\displaystyle\forall\,n\in\mathbb{N},\quad-1+C\varepsilon^{2-3a}2^{n}N_{n-1}^{-a_{2}+\tau_{2}}\leqslant 0

and therefore

\big|{\omega}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty,n-1}(\lambda,\varepsilon)-\mu_{j_{0}}^{\infty,n-1}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\big|\leqslant\displaystyle\tfrac{2\gamma_{n}\langle j-j_{0}\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{2}}}\cdot

Consequently,
\lambda\in\mathcal{R}_{l,j,j_{0}}(i_{n-1})
and the proof of the second point is now achieved.

(iii)
Let
j\in\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c}.
In particular, one has
(l,j)\neq(0,0).
We shall first prove that if
|l|\leqslant N_{n-1}
and then
\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(1)}(i_{n})\subset\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(1)}(i_{n-1}).
As in the point (i) this implies that
\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(1)}(i_{n})=\varnothing.
Remind that the set
\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(1)}(i_{n})
is defined below (
7.68
). Consider
\lambda\in\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(1)}(i_{n})
then by construction
\lambda\in\mathcal{C}_{n}^{\varepsilon}\subset\mathcal{C}_{n-1}^{\varepsilon}.
Now by the triangle inequality we may write in view of (
6.251
) and (
7.2
) and the choice
\gamma=\varepsilon^{a}

\displaystyle\big|{\omega}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty,n-1}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\big|

\displaystyle\leqslant\big|{\omega}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\big|+|\mu_{j}^{\infty,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)-\mu_{j}^{\infty,n-1}(\lambda,\varepsilon)|

\displaystyle\leqslant\displaystyle\tfrac{\gamma_{n+1}\langle j\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}+C\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}|j|\|i_{n}-i_{n-1}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\sigma_{4}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\displaystyle\tfrac{\gamma_{n+1}\langle j\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}+C\varepsilon^{2(1-a)}\langle j\rangle N_{n-1}^{-a_{2}}.

Since
\gamma_{n+1}=\gamma_{n}-\varepsilon^{a}2^{-n-1}
and
|l|\leqslant N_{n-1}
, then

\big|{\omega}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty,n-1}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\big|\leqslant\displaystyle\tfrac{\gamma_{n}\langle j\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}+\tfrac{\langle j\rangle\varepsilon^{a}}{2^{n+1}\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}\Big(-1+\varepsilon^{2-3a}2^{n+1}N_{n-1}^{-a_{2}+\tau_{1}}\Big).

Notice that (
7.79
) implies in particular

\displaystyle a_{2}>\tau_{1}\quad\hbox{and}\quad a<\tfrac{2}{3}

(7.80)

and taking
\varepsilon
small enough we find that

\forall\,n\in\mathbb{N},\quad-1+\varepsilon^{2-3a}2^{n+1}N_{n-1}^{-a_{2}+\tau_{1}}\leqslant 0,

which implies in turn that

\big|{\omega}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty,n-1}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\big|\leqslant\tfrac{\gamma_{n}\langle j\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}.

Consequently,
\lambda\in\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(1)}(i_{n-1})
and this ends the proof of the third point.

(iv)
It is an immediate consequence of (
7.68
) and the points (i)-(ii) and (iii) of Lemma
7.1
.
∎

The next result deals with necessary conditions such that the sets in (
7.68
) are nonempty.

Lemma 7.2
.

There exists
\varepsilon_{0}
such that for any
\varepsilon\in[0,\varepsilon_{0}]
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
the following assertions hold true.

(i)

Let
(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{(0,0)\}.
If
\,\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(0)}(i_{n})\neq\varnothing,
then
|j|\leqslant C_{0}\langle l\rangle.

(ii)

Let
(l,j,j_{0})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times(\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c})^{2}.
If
\,\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{l,j,j_{0}}(i_{n})\neq\varnothing,
then
|j-j_{0}|\leqslant C_{0}\langle l\rangle.

(iii)

Let
(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c}.
If
\,\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(1)}(i_{n})\neq\varnothing,
then
|j|\leqslant C_{0}\langle l\rangle.

(iv)

Let
(l,j,j_{0})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times(\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c})^{2}.
There exists
c_{2}>0
such that if
\displaystyle\min(|j|,|j_{0}|)\geqslant c_{2}\gamma_{n+1}^{-\upsilon}\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}},
then

\mathcal{R}_{l,j,j_{0}}(i_{n})\subset\mathcal{R}_{l,j-j_{0}}^{(0)}(i_{n}).

Proof.

(i)
Assume
\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(0)}(i_{n})\neq\varnothing
, then we can find
\lambda\in(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1})
such that, using triangle and Cauchy-Schwarz inequalities,

\displaystyle|c_{n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)||j|

\displaystyle\leqslant 4|j|\gamma_{n+1}^{\upsilon}\langle l\rangle^{-\tau_{1}}+|{\omega}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\cdot l|

\displaystyle\leqslant 4|j|\gamma_{n+1}^{\upsilon}+C\langle l\rangle

\displaystyle\leqslant 8\varepsilon^{a\upsilon}|j|+C\langle l\rangle,

where we have used
\gamma=\varepsilon^{a}
and the fact that
(\lambda,\varepsilon)\mapsto\omega(\lambda,\varepsilon)
is bounded. Notice that

c_{n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)=I_{1}(\lambda)K_{1}(\lambda)+r^{1,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\quad\hbox{and}\quad\displaystyle\inf_{\lambda\in(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1})}I_{1}(\lambda)K_{1}(\lambda):=c_{1}>0.

Then, from (
6.26
), (
6.248
) and Proposition
7.1

(\mathcal{P}1)_{n}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\forall k\in\llbracket 0,q\rrbracket,\quad\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{\lambda\in(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1})}|\partial_{\lambda}^{k}r^{1,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)|

\displaystyle\leqslant\gamma^{-k}\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\|r^{1,n}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-k}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{1-ak}.

(7.81)

Thus, by choosing
\varepsilon
small enough, we can ensure by (
7.2
)

\inf_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\inf_{\lambda\in(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1})}|c_{n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)|\geqslant\tfrac{c_{1}}{2}.

Hence, by taking
\varepsilon
small enough we find that
|j|\leqslant C_{0}\langle l\rangle
for some
C_{0}>0.

(ii)
In the case
j=j_{0}
we get by definition
\mathcal{R}_{l,j_{0},j_{0}}(i_{n})=\mathcal{R}^{(0)}_{l,0}(i_{n}),
and then we use the point
(i).
In what follows we take
j\neq j_{0}
and we assume that
\mathcal{R}_{l,j,j_{0}}(i_{n})\neq\varnothing
then there exists
\lambda\in(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1})
such that

\displaystyle|\mu_{j}^{\infty,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)-\mu_{j_{0}}^{\infty,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)|

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\gamma_{n+1}|j-j_{0}|\langle l\rangle^{-\tau_{2}}+|{\omega}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\cdot l|

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\gamma_{n+1}|j-j_{0}|+C\langle l\rangle

\displaystyle\leqslant 4\varepsilon^{a}|j-j_{0}|+C\langle l\rangle.

Similarly to (
7.2
), we can prove

\displaystyle\forall k\in\llbracket 0,q\rrbracket,\quad\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{j\in\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c}}\sup_{\lambda\in(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1})}|j||\partial_{\lambda}^{k}r_{j}^{\infty,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)|

\displaystyle\leqslant\gamma^{-k}\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{j\in\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c}}|j|\|r_{j}^{\infty,n}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1-k}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{1-a(1+k)}.

(7.82)

By using the triangle inequality, Lemma
3.3
-(v), (
7.2
) and (
7.2
) we get for
j\neq j_{0}
,

\displaystyle|\mu_{j}^{\infty,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)-\mu_{j_{0}}^{\infty,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)|

\displaystyle\geqslant|\Omega_{j}(\lambda)-\Omega_{j_{0}}(\lambda)|-|r^{1,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)||j-j_{0}|-|r_{j}^{\infty,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)|-|r_{j_{0}}^{\infty,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)|

\displaystyle\geqslant\big(C_{0}-C\varepsilon^{1-a}\big)|j-j_{0}|

\displaystyle\geqslant\tfrac{C_{0}}{2}|j-j_{0}|

provided that
\varepsilon
is small enough. Putting together the previous inequalities yields for
\varepsilon
small enough
|j-j_{0}|\leqslant C_{0}\langle l\rangle,
for some
C_{0}>0.

(iii)
First remark that the case
j=0
is trivial. Now for
j\neq 0
we assume that
\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(1)}(i_{n})\neq\varnothing
then there exists
\lambda\in(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1})
such that

\displaystyle|\mu_{j}^{\infty,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)|

\displaystyle\leqslant\gamma_{n+1}|j|\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}+|{\omega}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\cdot l|

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\varepsilon^{a}|j|+C\langle l\rangle.

Using the definition (
7.2
) combined with
the triangle inequality, Lemma
3.3
-(iv), (
7.2
) and (
7.2
), we get

\displaystyle|\mu_{j}^{\infty,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)|

\displaystyle\geqslant\Omega|j|-|j||r^{1,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)|-|r_{j}^{\infty,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)|

\displaystyle\geqslant\Omega|j|-C\varepsilon^{1-a}|j|.

Combining the previous two inequalities and the second condition in (
7.80
) implies

\displaystyle\big(\Omega-C\varepsilon^{1-a}-2\varepsilon^{a}\big)|j|

\displaystyle\leqslant C\langle l\rangle.

Thus, by taking
\varepsilon
small enough we obtain
|j|\leqslant C_{0}\langle l\rangle,
for some
C_{0}>0.

(iv)
First notice that the case
j=j_{0}
is trivial and follows from the definition (
7.68
). Let
j\neq j_{0}
and
\lambda\in\mathcal{R}_{l,j,j_{0}}(i_{n})
, then by definition

\big|{\omega}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)-\mu_{j_{0}}^{\infty,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\big|\leqslant\tfrac{2\gamma_{n+1}\langle j-j_{0}\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{2}}}.

Combining (
7.2
) and (
3.14
) with the triangle inequality we infer

\displaystyle\big|{\omega}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\cdot l+(j-j_{0})c_{n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\big|

\displaystyle\leqslant\big|{\omega}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)-\mu_{j_{0}}^{\infty,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\big|

\displaystyle\quad+|jI_{j}(\lambda)K_{j}(\lambda)-j_{0}I_{j_{0}}(\lambda)K_{j_{0}}(\lambda)|+\big|r_{j}^{\infty,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)-r_{j_{0}}^{\infty,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\big|.

Thus, we find

\displaystyle\big|{\omega}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\cdot l+(j-j_{0})c_{n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\big|\leqslant

\displaystyle\tfrac{2\gamma_{n+1}\langle j-j_{0}\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{2}}}+|jI_{j}(\lambda)K_{j}(\lambda)-j_{0}I_{j_{0}}(\lambda)K_{j_{0}}(\lambda)|

\displaystyle+\big|r_{j}^{\infty,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)-r_{j_{0}}^{\infty,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\big|.

(7.83)

Without loss of generality, we can assume that
|j_{0}|\geqslant|j|
and remind that
j\neq j_{0}
. Then, from (
3.2.1
) and (
3.2.1
), we easily find

\displaystyle|jI_{j}(\lambda)K_{j}(\lambda)-j_{0}I_{j_{0}}(\lambda)K_{j_{0}}(\lambda)|

\displaystyle\leqslant|j|\big|I_{j}(\lambda)K_{j}(\lambda)-I_{j_{0}}(\lambda)K_{j_{0}}(\lambda)\big|+|j-j_{0}|\big|I_{j_{0}}(\lambda)K_{j_{0}}(\lambda)\big|

\displaystyle\leqslant\tfrac{\langle j-j_{0}\rangle}{\min(|j|,|j_{0}|)}\cdot

Applying (
6.248
), we find for
j\neq j_{0}\in\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c}
,

\displaystyle\big|r_{j}^{\infty,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)-r_{j_{0}}^{\infty,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\big|\leqslant

\displaystyle C\varepsilon^{1-a}\big(|j|^{-1}+|j_{0}|^{-1}\big)

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle C\varepsilon^{1-a}\tfrac{\langle j-j_{0}\rangle}{\min(|j|,|j_{0}|)}\cdot

Plugging the preceding estimates into (
7.2
) yields

\displaystyle\big|{\omega}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\cdot l+(j-j_{0})c_{n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\big|\leqslant

\displaystyle\tfrac{2\gamma_{n+1}\langle j-j_{0}\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{2}}}+C\tfrac{\langle j-j_{0}\rangle}{\min(|j|,|j_{0}|)}\cdot

Therefore, if we assume
\displaystyle\min(|j|,|j_{0}|)\geqslant\tfrac{1}{2}C\gamma_{n+1}^{-\upsilon}\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}
and
\tau_{2}>\tau_{1}
, then we deduce

\big|{\omega}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\cdot l+(j-j_{0})c_{n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\big|\leqslant\tfrac{4\gamma_{n+1}^{\upsilon}\langle j-j_{0}\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}\cdot

This ends the proof of the lemma by taking
c_{2}=\frac{C}{2}.

∎

We shall now establish that the perturbed frequencies
\omega(\lambda,\varepsilon)
satisfy the Rüssemann conditions. This is done by a perturbation argument from the equilibrium linear frequencies
\omega_{\textnormal{Eq}}(\lambda)
for which we already know by Lemma
3.5
that they satisfy the transversality conditions.

Lemma 7.3
.

Let
q_{0}
,
C_{0}
and
\rho_{0}
as in Lemma
3.5
. There exist
\varepsilon_{0}>0
small enough such that for any
\varepsilon\in[0,\varepsilon_{0}]
the following assertions hold true.

(i)

For all
l\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\setminus\{0\},
we have

\inf_{\lambda\in[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]}\max_{k\in\llbracket 0,q_{0}\rrbracket}\big|\partial_{\lambda}^{k}\left({\omega}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\cdot l\right)\big|\geqslant\tfrac{\rho_{0}\langle l\rangle}{2}.

(ii)

For all
(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d+1}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
such that
|j|\leqslant C_{0}\langle l\rangle,
we have

\forall n\in\mathbb{N},\quad\inf_{\lambda\in[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]}\max_{k\in\llbracket 0,q_{0}\rrbracket}|\partial_{\lambda}^{k}\big({\omega}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\cdot l+jc_{n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\big)|\geqslant\tfrac{\rho_{0}\langle l\rangle}{2}.

(iii)

For all
(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c}
such that
|j|\leqslant C_{0}\langle l\rangle,
we have

\forall n\in\mathbb{N},\quad\inf_{\lambda\in[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]}\max_{k\in\llbracket 0,q_{0}\rrbracket}\big|\partial_{\lambda}^{k}\big({\omega}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\big)\big|\geqslant\tfrac{\rho_{0}\langle l\rangle}{2}.

(iv)

For all
(l,j,j_{0})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times(\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c})^{2}
such that
|j-j_{0}|\leqslant C_{0}\langle l\rangle,
we have

\forall n\in\mathbb{N},\quad\inf_{\lambda\in[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]}\max_{k\in\llbracket 0,q_{0}\rrbracket}\big|\partial_{\lambda}^{k}\big({\omega}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)-\mu_{j_{0}}^{\infty,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\big)\big|\geqslant\tfrac{\rho_{0}\langle l\rangle}{2}.

Proof.

(i)
From the triangle and Cauchy-Schwarz inequalities together with (
7.62
), (
7.2
) and Lemma
3.5
-(i), we deduce

\displaystyle\displaystyle\max_{k\in\llbracket 0,q_{0}\rrbracket}|\partial_{\lambda}^{k}\left({\omega}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\cdot l\right)|

\displaystyle\geqslant\displaystyle\max_{k\in\llbracket 0,q_{0}\rrbracket}|\partial_{\lambda}^{k}\left({\omega}_{\textnormal{Eq}}(\lambda)\cdot l\right)|-\max_{k\in\llbracket 0,q\rrbracket}|\partial_{\lambda}^{k}\left(\overline{\mathrm{r}}_{\varepsilon}(\lambda)\cdot l\right)|

\displaystyle\geqslant\displaystyle\rho_{0}\langle l\rangle-C\varepsilon\gamma^{-1-q}N_{0}^{q\overline{a}}\langle l\rangle

\displaystyle\geqslant\displaystyle\rho_{0}\langle l\rangle-C\varepsilon^{1-a(1+q+q\overline{a})}\langle l\rangle

\displaystyle\geqslant\displaystyle\tfrac{\rho_{0}\langle l\rangle}{2}

provided that
\varepsilon
is small enough and

1-a(1+q+q\overline{a})>0.

(7.84)

Notice that the condition (
7.84
) is automatically satisfied by (
7.2
) and (
7.1
).

(ii)
As before, using the triangle and Cauchy-Schwarz inequalities combined with (
7.62
), (
7.2
), Lemma
3.5
-(ii) and the fact that
|j|\leqslant C_{0}\langle l\rangle
, we get

\displaystyle\displaystyle\max_{k\in\llbracket 0,q_{0}\rrbracket}|\partial_{\lambda}^{k}\left({\omega}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\cdot l+jc_{n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\right)|

\displaystyle\geqslant\displaystyle\max_{k\in\llbracket 0,q_{0}\rrbracket}|\partial_{\lambda}^{k}\left({\omega}_{\textnormal{Eq}}(\lambda)\cdot l+jI_{1}(\lambda)K_{1}(\lambda)\right)|

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad-\max_{k\in\llbracket 0,q\rrbracket}|\partial_{\lambda}^{k}\left(\overline{\mathrm{r}}_{\varepsilon}(\lambda)\cdot l+jr^{1,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\right)|

\displaystyle\geqslant\displaystyle\rho_{0}\langle l\rangle-C\varepsilon^{1-a(1+q+q\overline{a})}\langle l\rangle-C\varepsilon^{1-aq}|j|

\displaystyle\geqslant\displaystyle\tfrac{\rho_{0}\langle l\rangle}{2}

for
\varepsilon
small enough and with the condition (
7.84
).

(iii)
As before, performing the triangle and Cauchy-Schwarz inequalities combined with (
7.62
), (
7.2
), (
7.2
), Lemma
3.5
-(iii) and the fact that
|j|\leqslant C_{0}\langle l\rangle
, we get

\displaystyle\displaystyle\max_{k\in\llbracket 0,q_{0}\rrbracket}\big|\partial_{\lambda}^{k}\big({\omega}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\big)\big|

\displaystyle\geqslant\displaystyle\max_{k\in\llbracket 0,q_{0}\rrbracket}|\partial_{\lambda}^{k}\left({\omega}_{\textnormal{Eq}}(\lambda)\cdot l+\Omega_{j}(\lambda)\right)|

\displaystyle\displaystyle\quad-\max_{k\in\llbracket 0,q\rrbracket}\big|\partial_{\lambda}^{k}\big(\overline{\mathrm{r}}_{\varepsilon}(\lambda)\cdot l+jr^{1,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)+r_{j}^{\infty,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon))\big)\big|

\displaystyle\geqslant\displaystyle\rho_{0}\langle l\rangle-C\varepsilon^{1-a(1+q+q\overline{a})}\langle l\rangle-C\varepsilon^{1-a(1+q)}|j|

\displaystyle\geqslant\displaystyle\tfrac{\rho_{0}\langle l\rangle}{2}

for
\varepsilon
small enough with the condition (
7.84
).

(iv)
Arguing as in the preceding point, using (
7.2
), (
7.2
), Lemma
3.5
-(iv)-(v) and the fact that
0<|j-j_{0}|\leqslant C_{0}\langle l\rangle
(notice that the case
j=j_{0}
is trivial), we have

\displaystyle\max_{k\in\llbracket 0,q_{0}\rrbracket}\big|\partial_{\lambda}^{k}\big({\omega}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\cdot l

\displaystyle+\mu_{j}^{\infty,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)-\mu_{j_{0}}^{\infty,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\big)\big|\geqslant\displaystyle\max_{k\in\llbracket 0,q_{0}\rrbracket}\big|\partial_{\lambda}^{k}\big({\omega}_{\textnormal{Eq}}(\lambda)\cdot l+\Omega_{j}(\lambda)-\Omega_{j_{0}}(\lambda)\big)\big|\displaystyle

\displaystyle-\max_{k\in\llbracket 0,q\rrbracket}\big|\partial_{\lambda}^{k}\big(\overline{\mathrm{r}}_{\varepsilon}(\lambda)\cdot l+(j-j_{0})r^{1,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)+r_{j}^{\infty,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)-r_{j_{0}}^{\infty,n}(\lambda,\varepsilon)\big)\big|

\displaystyle\geqslant\displaystyle\rho_{0}\langle l\rangle-C\varepsilon^{1-a(1+q+q\overline{a})}\langle l\rangle-C\varepsilon^{1-a(1+q)}|j-j_{0}|

\displaystyle\geqslant\displaystyle\tfrac{\rho_{0}\langle l\rangle}{2}

for
\varepsilon
small enough. This ends the proof of Lemma
7.3
.
∎

Appendix A
Modified Bessel functions

In this short section we shall collect some properties about Bessel and modified Bessel functions that were used in the preceding sections. We refer to
[
53
]
for an almost exhaustive presentation of these special functions.

We define first the Bessel functions of order
\nu\in\mathbb{C}
by

J_{\nu}(z)=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-1)^{m}\left(\frac{z}{2}\right)^{\nu+2m}}{m!\Gamma(\nu+m+1)},\quad|\mbox{arg}(z)|<\pi.

Notice that when
\nu\in\mathbb{N}
we have the following integral representation, see
[
47
, p. 115]
.

J_{\nu}(x)=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\pi}\cos\big(x\sin\theta-\nu\theta\big)d\theta.

(A.1)

We shall also introduce the Bessel functions of imaginary argument also called modified Bessel functions of first and second kind

I_{\nu}(z)=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{\left(\frac{z}{2}\right)^{\nu+2m}}{m!\Gamma(\nu+m+1)},\quad|\mbox{arg}(z)|<\pi

(A.2)

and

K_{\nu}(z)=\frac{\pi}{2}\frac{I_{-\nu}(z)-I_{\nu}(z)}{\sin(\nu\pi)},\quad\nu\in\mathbb{C}\setminus\mathbb{Z},\quad|\mbox{arg}(z)|<\pi.

For
j\in\mathbb{Z},
we define
K_{j}(z)=\displaystyle\lim_{\nu\rightarrow j}K_{\nu}(z).

We give now useful properties of modified Bessel functions.

\blacktriangleright

Symmetry and positivity,
see
[
2
, p. 375]

\forall j\in\mathbb{N},\quad\forall\lambda\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*},\quad I_{-j}(\lambda)=I_{j}(\lambda)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*}\quad\mbox{and}\quad K_{-j}(\lambda)=K_{j}(\lambda)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*}.

(A.3)

\blacktriangleright

Anti-derivative,
see
[
2
, p. 376]
. If we set
\mathcal{Z}_{\nu}(z)=I_{\nu}(z)
or
e^{i\nu\pi}K_{\nu}(z)
, then for all
\nu\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\frac{d}{dz}\left(z^{\nu+1}\mathcal{Z}_{\nu+1}(z)\right)=z^{\nu+1}\mathcal{Z}_{\nu}(z).

(A.4)

\blacktriangleright

Power series expansion for
K_{j}
,
see
[
2
, p. 375]
.

\begin{array}[]{l}\displaystyle K_{j}(z)=\tfrac{1}{2}\left(\tfrac{z}{2}\right)^{-j}\sum_{k=0}^{j-1}\tfrac{(j-k-1)!}{k!}\left(\tfrac{-z^{2}}{4}\right)^{k}+(-1)^{n+1}\log\left(\tfrac{z}{2}\right)I_{j}(z)\\
\displaystyle\mbox{\hskip 113.81102pt}+\tfrac{1}{2}\left(\tfrac{-z}{2}\right)^{j}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left(\psi(k+1)+\psi(j+k+1)\right)\tfrac{\left(\frac{z^{2}}{4}\right)^{k}}{k!(j+k)!},\end{array}

(A.5)

where
\psi(1)=-\boldsymbol{\gamma}
(Euler’s constant)  and
\forall m\in\mathbb{N}^{*},\,\psi(m+1)=\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{m}\tfrac{1}{k}-\boldsymbol{\gamma}.

In particular we have the expansion

K_{0}(z)=-\log\left(\frac{z}{2}\right)I_{0}(z)+\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{\left(\frac{z}{2}\right)^{2m}}{(m!)^{2}}\psi(m+1).

(A.6)

\blacktriangleright

Integral representation for
K_{\nu}
,
see
[
47
, p. 133]

For all
a,b>0
for any
\nu,\mu\in\mathbb{C}
satisfying
-1<\textnormal{Re}(\nu)<2\textnormal{Re}(\mu)+\frac{3}{2}
one has

\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{x^{\nu+1}J_{\nu}\left(bx\right)}{(x^{2}+a^{2})^{\mu+1}}dx=\frac{a^{\nu-\mu}b^{\mu}}{2^{\mu}\Gamma(\mu+1)}K_{\nu-\mu}(ab).

(A.7)

\blacktriangleright

Nicholson’s integral representation,
see
[
53
, p. 441]
. Let
j\in\mathbb{N}
then

(I_{j}K_{j})(z)=\tfrac{2(-1)^{j}}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}K_{0}(2z\cos(\tau))\cos(2j\tau)d\tau.

(A.8)

Another similar representation can be found in
[
47
, p. 140]

\displaystyle(I_{j}K_{j})(\lambda)=\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}J_{0}\big(2\lambda\sinh(t/2)\big)e^{-jt}dt.

(A.9)

\blacktriangleright

Holomorphic property of the product
I_{j}K_{j}
.
Let
j\in\mathbb{N}
then the function
z\mapsto(I_{j}K_{j})(z)
is holomorphic on the half plane
\mbox{Re}(z)>0.

\blacktriangleright

Monotonicity of
I_{\nu}K_{\nu}
, see for instance
[
9
,
22
]
.The map
(\lambda,\nu)\in(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*})^{2}\mapsto I_{\nu}(\lambda)K_{\nu}(\lambda)
is strictly decreasing in each variable.

\blacktriangleright

Asymptotic expansion of small argument,
see for instance
[
2
, p. 375]
.

\forall j\in\mathbb{N}^{*},\quad I_{j}(\lambda)\underset{\lambda\rightarrow 0}{\sim}\tfrac{\left(\frac{1}{2}\lambda\right)^{j}}{\Gamma(j+1)}\quad\mbox{ and }\quad K_{j}(\lambda)\underset{\lambda\rightarrow 0}{\sim}\tfrac{\Gamma(j)}{2\left(\frac{1}{2}\lambda\right)^{j}}.

(A.10)

\blacktriangleright

Asymptotic expansion of large argument for the product
I_{j}K_{j}
, see for instance
[
2
, p. 378]
.

\forall N\in\mathbb{N}^{*},\quad I_{j}(\lambda)K_{j}(\lambda)\underset{\lambda\rightarrow\infty}{\sim}\frac{1}{2\lambda}\left(1+\sum_{m=1}^{N}\tfrac{\alpha_{j,m}}{(2\lambda)^{2m}}\right),

(A.11)

with

\displaystyle\alpha_{j,m}=(-1)^{m}\tfrac{(2m)!}{4^{m}\big(m!\big)^{2}}P_{m}(\mu_{j}),\quad P_{m}(X)=\prod_{\ell=1}^{m}\big(X-(2\ell-1)^{2}\big),\quad\mu_{j}=4{j^{2}}.

(A.12)

In particular,

I_{j}(\lambda)K_{j}(\lambda)\underset{\lambda\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}0.

(A.13)

\blacktriangleright

Asymptotic expansion of high order for the product
I_{j}K_{j}
, for more details see
[
43
]
.

I_{j}(\lambda)K_{j}(\lambda)\underset{j\rightarrow\infty}{\sim}\tfrac{1}{2j}\left(\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\tfrac{b_{m}(\lambda)}{j^{m}}\right)\left(\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{m}\tfrac{b_{m}(\lambda)}{j^{m}}\right),

(A.14)

where for each
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
b_{m}(\lambda)
is a polynomial of degree
m
in
\lambda^{2}
defined by

b_{0}(\lambda)=1\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\forall m\in\mathbb{N}^{*},\,b_{m}(\lambda)=\sum_{k=1}^{m}(-1)^{m-k}\tfrac{S(m,k)}{k!}\left(\tfrac{\lambda^{2}}{4}\right)^{k}

and the
S(m,k)
are Stirling numbers of second kind defined recursively by

\forall(m,k)\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\times\mathbb{N},\quad S(m,k)=S(m-1,k-1)+kS(m-1,k),

with

S(0,0)=1,\mbox{ }\forall m\in\mathbb{N}^{*},S(m,1)=1\mbox{ and }S(m,0)=0\mbox{ and if }m<k\mbox{ then }S(m,k)=0.

We shall also prove the following result which has been frequently used before.

Lemma A.1
.

Let
N_{0}\geqslant 2.
Consider the sequence
(N_{m})_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
defined by (
5.82
). Then for all
\alpha>0
, we have

\sum_{k=m}^{\infty}N_{k}^{-\alpha}\underset{m\rightarrow\infty}{\sim}N_{m}^{-\alpha}.

Proof.

We consider the positive decaying function

t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*}\mapsto N_{0}^{-\alpha\left(\frac{3}{2}\right)^{t}}=\exp\left(-\alpha\ln(N_{0})e^{t\ln\left(\frac{3}{2}\right)}\right),

and apply to it a series-integral comparison, namely

\sum_{k=m+1}^{\infty}N_{k}^{-\alpha}\leqslant\int_{m}^{\infty}\exp\left(-\alpha\ln(N_{0})e^{t\ln\left(\frac{3}{2}\right)}\right)dt=\int_{0}^{\infty}\exp\left(-\alpha\ln(N_{0})e^{u\ln\left(\frac{3}{2}\right)}e^{m\ln\left(\frac{3}{2}\right)}\right)du.

Now remark that

N_{m}^{\alpha}\exp\left(-\alpha\ln(N_{0})e^{u\ln\left(\frac{3}{2}\right)}e^{m\ln\left(\frac{3}{2}\right)}\right)=\exp\left(\alpha\ln(N_{0})\left(1-e^{u\ln\left(\frac{3}{2}\right)}\right)e^{m\ln\left(\frac{3}{2}\right)}\right).

Since

\forall u\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*},\quad 1-e^{u\ln\left(\frac{3}{2}\right)}<0,

then we deduce that

\forall u\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*},\quad N_{m}^{\alpha}\exp\left(-\alpha\ln(N_{0})e^{u\ln\left(\frac{3}{2}\right)}e^{m\ln\left(\frac{3}{2}\right)}\right)\underset{m\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}0

and

\forall u\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*},\forall m\in\mathbb{N},\quad 0\leqslant N_{m}^{\alpha}\exp\left(-\alpha\ln(N_{0})e^{u\ln\left(\frac{3}{2}\right)}e^{m\ln\left(\frac{3}{2}\right)}\right)\leqslant N_{0}^{\alpha}\exp\left(-\alpha\ln(N_{0})e^{u\ln\left(\frac{3}{2}\right)}\right)\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+}).

Applying dominated convergence theorem, we obtain

\sum_{k=m+1}^{\infty}N_{k}^{-\alpha}\underset{m\rightarrow\infty}{=}o\left(N_{m}^{-\alpha}\right).

As a consequence

\sum_{k=m}^{\infty}N_{k}^{-\alpha}=N_{m}^{-\alpha}+\sum_{k=m+1}^{\infty}N_{k}^{-\alpha}\underset{m\rightarrow\infty}{\sim}N_{m}^{-\alpha}.

∎
